It is the one client-facing parallel representation earlier "no parallel representation" sweeps never named; SPEC-5 widens the row's CAUSE only and the new cause is permanent and non-retryable, so the table stays correct and is not an edit site.
- **DECISION: the docs-mirror trailer is an OBLIGATION statement rather than a completion claim.** It reads "Each reader-facing reference page that mirrors these sections moves with the section it mirrors. The edits the non-spec deliverables must carry are these.", so a `(DOCS-1)` parenthetical names the deliverable that OWES the edit. Deleting the paragraph, appending under the old "carries" framing, re-attributing to another id and adding a deferral carve-out were all rejected.
- **`docs/reference/state-machines.md:138` is the ONLY docs mirror of the §6.2 projection sentence,** and it carries all three replaced clauses; §4.6.1's parallel bullets have no docs mirror at all. No tier-11 gate holds that page against §6.2 or §4.6.1, so nothing catches the stale page at implementation time.
- **`docs/operator-guide/troubleshooting.md:41`'s `setup_command_failed` is the `error_type` label of `lenny_warmpool_warmup_failure_total`, not the REST envelope's `details.reason`.** The row sits under a warm-pool diagnosis block whose query is `rate(lenny_warmpool_warmup_failure_total[5m]) by (reason)`. The only client-facing docs mirror of §15.1's widening is `docs/reference/error-catalog.md:129` (DOCS-3), and `docs/client-guide/error-handling.md` carries no such row.
- **`PrepareWorkspace` is the only client-streaming RPC on this contract,** so the first-frame rule needs no sibling; and `PrepareWorkspaceRequest` is NOT a §4.1 stream envelope (it declares no `oneof`), so §4.1's envelope paragraph and its tier-0 gate do not reach the two new fields.
- **`idempotentRepeat: true` has exactly ONE production caller, `ConfigureWorkspace` (sdkwarm.go:217);** `StartSession` (session.go:111) and `Resume` (resume.go:50) both pass false. That is what makes the started-session rule's single `ConfigureWorkspace` exemption exactly right and a `StartSession`/`Resume` exemption wrong.
- **The reclaim hold does not exist in the shipped tree at all.** `errSlotReclaimInProgress`, `isSlotReclaimInProgress` and the string `slot_reclaim_in_progress` appear nowhere under `pkg/`, `tests/` or `schemas/`, so CODE-6 introduces the whole mechanism. The non-spec prose calling those symbols "unchanged from the shipped text" was corrected by the reconciliation pass.
- **`ShutdownRequest` fields 7 and 8 are free (1, 2, 3, 5 and 6 live, 4 reserved) and `ShutdownResponse` gains `slot_reclaim = 3` and nothing else.** There is no `slot_was_started` field anywhere in the staging or the tree; the orchestrator brief has asserted one for at least six rounds.
- **The reconciliation pass rebuilt the checklist and the deliverable index.** The SPEC lane stands S1=SPEC-5, S2=SPEC-1, S3=SPEC-2, S4=SPEC-3, S5=SPEC-4, S6=SPEC-6; every code step's `Depends on` gained its direct spec-step ids and S6 was dropped from S7's; and `## Deliverable index` carries TWENTY lines (SPEC-1 to SPEC-6, SCHEMA-1, CODE-1 to CODE-9, CONF-1, DOCS-1 to DOCS-3). The standing "nineteen lines" entry is stale.
- **`## Open decisions for human to make` now carries entries 20, 21, 22, 26 and 27.** 23, 24 and 25 were resolved and their blocks deleted; 21 and 27 gained a recommendation, a ground, alternatives and a confidence; 20, 22 and 26 carry the question and its ground alone. Any entry saying the section holds one decision, or 11/16/17/18, is reading a superseded pass.
- **DECISION (open decision 24, RESOLVED): the pairing rule and the mid-session-create rule take NO error category and NO `ErrorCode`, and §15.4's sentence stands unnarrowed.** Both are answered on a bare `status.Errorf` with no `WithDetails`, so there is no envelope for any section to state a category on; the teardown-pairing and start-confirmation refusals are the same shape. The proto comment claiming every RPC failure carries a structured envelope is pre-existing and contradicted by every bare status the shipped adapter already returns.
- **DECISION (open decision 25, RESOLVED): the `Shutdown` row's "states which teardown it asks for" phrasing STANDS.** The same sentence already carries the entry-selection sense, §4.7.1 states the correction four sentences below its own heading, and no staged artefact reads the phrase literally. The four-site wording sweep stays available as a copy-edit against the applied spec and gates nothing.
- **`## Impacts on other proposals` carries 0080, 0073, 0075, 0078, 0072, 0079, R1b and R12, one row each.** 0072 shares SEVEN files with this proposal, four of them colliding inside one section; 0079 shares five spec files with every collision anchor-distinct. Neither loses a deliverable's subject, and four of 0072's own citations have drifted against the tree, recorded in its row rather than corrected there.
- **The claim-DELETE-before-projection race is real, reachable and PRE-EXISTING.** `ProjectOccupancyPhase`'s no-claim arm keys on the phase the projection last wrote, so a claim created, bound and deleted inside a WarmPoolController failover window reconciles as no-claim plus `Current=idle` and hits `default: return "", false`: the unscrubbed pod stays in idle inventory carrying the failed bind's registry entry, tree, credential file and armed timers, and `ClaimSlot` re-offers it with no used-pod guard. It is open decision 27 and wants its own proposal; SPEC-4 makes the phase dependency explicit rather than introducing it.
- **`o.Current` is the phase the projection last WROTE, not "the phase the pod is in".** Any spec sentence of the form "the claim is deleted while the pod projects X" is an assertion about reconcile ordering rather than about the claim.
- **A demotion cannot leave a bind meeting the hold.** `DemoteSDK` runs `releaseSessionSlot` synchronously inside its own RPC, so the pod-warm bind sequence that follows resolves no entry and takes the create-and-stamp rule. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/sdkwarm.go:274-302.
- **The shipped `Shutdown` already answers a clean exit on the no-entry path,** returning `ExitedCleanly: closeErr == nil` with every destructive act inside `bound := removed && st.sessionID != ""`. So the staged reclaim-outcome rule records shipped behaviour on the `absent` arm rather than imposing new behaviour there.
- **`pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/one_session_only_test.go:34` cites a `credSessionID` stickiness backstop that no longer exists** (`grep credSessionID pkg/adapter` returns nothing). It is a stale comment rather than a shipped guard, and no reading should rest on it.
- **Every bind-sequence request already carries `coordination_generation`,** so the identity-free `mid_session` form of `PrepareWorkspace`/`FinalizeWorkspace` is still replica-fenced by §10.1 even though it asserts no attempt identity. That is a second fence beside the gateway-side admission guard the staging rests it on.

- **The production adapter-`Shutdown` caller set is FIVE sites behind ONE builder.** `Client.Shutdown` (client.go:807-809) and `Client.ShutdownRecycle` (:860-867) both funnel into the unexported `Client.shutdown` (:813-824), the only production constructor of `adapterv1.ShutdownRequest` in the tree. The five callers are slotbinder.go:542, :574, binder.go:2037, :2043 and cmd/lenny-gateway/user_revocation.go:129. There is NO crash-takeover and no coordinator-handoff `Shutdown`; `pkg/gateway/coordination/` contains no `Shutdown` string, and the re-adopt sends `CoordinatorFence` alone. CODE-4 sets `unconditional_teardown` in the builder, so no caller edit is owed. Derived independently by at least eight shards; do not re-derive.
- **There are TWO recycle-carrying `Shutdown` senders and they reach DIFFERENT arms.** The concurrent path (`Binder.ReleaseSlot`) sends a plain unconditional `Shutdown` at slotbinder.go:542 and then `ShutdownRecycle` at :574, so the second finds no entry and answers `absent`. The session-mode path (`Binder.Release`'s recycle branch, binder.go:1974 → :1994 → `shutdownAdapter(..., true)` → :2037) sends the recycle request as the session's ONLY teardown, so the entry is live and the outcome is `reclaimed` on the removing arm. That path is production-live: `exclusiveBindRequest` sets `Recycle: match.Recycle` (start.go:2520,:2561), so any exclusive pool with recycling reaches it on every non-failed release.
- **`startPodScrub` is asynchronous.** It spawns a goroutine and returns (podscrub.go:40-42), so firing clause three from the removing arm blocks nothing under the slot guard or the reclaim hold. It does run CONCURRENTLY with the per-slot cleanup those two protect, which no staged text orders.
- **DECISION: CODE-1's handler has ONE exit, `answerShutdown(outcome, exitedCleanly, untokened)`,** which counts the untokened row, runs clause three (`if rc := req.GetRecycle(); rc != nil { s.startPodScrub(rc) }`) and builds the response; the shipped trailing clause three at session.go:283-291 is DELETED in the same edit, or the scrub runs twice and `ReportPodScrub` double-sends. Two returns deliberately bypass the helper because they perform nothing: the two-field precondition's `INVALID_ARGUMENT` and the shipped empty-session-id check at session.go:228-230.
- **DECISION: the per-slot guard's scope is a PREDICATE plus a derivation table, never an RPC enumeration.** A section that does path work against the slot's tree outside `s.mu` acquires that slot's guard before the resolve that hands out its paths and holds it until that path work ends; a section whose path work runs inside `s.mu` takes none. The predicate selects `PrepareWorkspace`, `FinalizeWorkspace`, `RunSetup` and `Resume` on the admission side and the three destructive sections on the other. EVIDENCE for the exemptions: `assignCredentialsSlot` writes the credential file inside its own `s.mu` section (slotcreds.go:24-44); `ConfigureWorkspace`'s only write is `writeSessionManifest` under `Server.ManifestDir` (manifest.go:280-283, server.go:111-114), which `slotlayout.RemoveTree` does not touch (tree.go:58-69).
- **`Server.Resume` is the adapter's largest UNLOCKED path write,** which is why the enumeration that omitted it was wrong. `checkpointRootsForSession` copies the roots under `s.mu` and RELEASES it (slot.go:183-207); `restoreChunks` then fetches every chunk over the network and runs `workspace.ExtractTree` with no lock (resume.go:169-208, called at :106).
- **DECISION: the guard is `map[string]chan struct{}` (capacity-one semaphores) and both hand-out forms are context-bounded.** `lockSlotGuard(ctx, slotID) (func(), bool)` and `acquireSlotGuardForResolve(ctx, slotID) (func(), error)` both select the send against `ctx.Done()`. The expiry disposition is NOT uniform: a destructive section that cannot acquire proceeds unguarded and logs `slot_guard_not_acquired` (which is today's shipped behaviour, so no regression); an admission RPC that cannot acquire is refused with `ctx.Err()`, because proceeding unguarded is how a materialization re-creates a tree the reclaim just removed.
- **DECISION: `Shutdown` compares under `s.mu` FIRST, acquires the guard only on the arm that destroys, and RE-DECIDES under `s.mu` afterwards.** Every arm that removes nothing answers from the fast path and never waits, because the guard's holders are unbounded in wall time (`workspace.MaterializeWithPolicy`/`MaterializeOverlayWithPolicy` take no ctx at all, materialize.go:157,:203; `RunSetup` runs under a `setupPolicy.timeoutSeconds` spec/14:99 permits to be absent) while the reclaim's budget is `max(cleanupTimeoutSeconds / maxConcurrentSessions, 5)`s and a timed-out reclaim is charged a permanent leak. The re-decision is sound against every remover: the only writer that can remove an entry without taking the guard is §10.1.4 pass 1, which removes under `s.mu` alone, and the re-decision catches exactly that and answers `absent`.
- **The guard STRICTLY CONTAINS the reclaim hold on a single-pass reclaim.** `defer unlockSlot()` is registered before `defer release()` and Go runs defers LIFO, so `release()` runs first. Consequence: a guarded workspace RPC racing a reclaim BLOCKS rather than being refused, and the hold's refusal survives only for the unguarded entry-creating RPCs and, on the §10.1.4 two-pass path, in the window between `deregisterStartedSessions` (hold, no guard) and `terminateHeldSession` (guard). That is why the hold is tested at BOTH `acquireSlotGuardForResolve` and `ensureSlotStateLocked`: they are one predicate at two points, not two mechanisms.
- **DECISION: §10.1.4's ten-second budget is RE-SCOPED rather than divided.** `onHoldTimeout`'s single `context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), 10*time.Second)` becomes the pass's GUARD-ACQUISITION deadline alone, and `terminateHeldSession` mints its own ten-second close context after the acquisition returns, which `emitFinalUsage` and `Runtime.Close` run on. BECAUSE the shipped pass is a serial loop over pass 1's whole member set sharing one context (holdstate.go:196-205,:237,:249), so any wait charged to it is charged to every LATER member. SPEC-3's staged §5.2 sentence already bounds "the cleanup's close" per cleanup, so this is a mis-scoping correction rather than a new rule. ALTERNATIVES rejected: a per-member slice of the shared budget (invents arithmetic and still burns member 1's own grace); deleting the guard from `terminateHeldSession` (needs an exception to the guard predicate and re-opens the `ExtractTree`-versus-`removeSlotTree` race); one per-member context covering both acquisition and close (a parked holder still burns member 1's close grace).
- **DECISION: `releaseSessionSlot` splits into a guard-acquiring form and `releaseSessionSlotUnderGuard`,** because `Resume` holds the slot's non-re-entrant guard across its whole call and its seven rollback sites (resume.go:69,:73,:89,:107,:126,:134,:141) would otherwise self-deadlock on every `Resume` failure, which no happy-path test reaches. It also gains a `context.Context` first parameter; every production caller has a handler ctx in scope. Production callers: session.go:133,:147,:157 and sdkwarm.go:236,:241,:251,:298. `Server.ReleaseSlotForTest` (export_test.go:32-35) gains the ctx too and has five callers (integrationlevel_test.go:84, credexpiry_test.go:320, checkpoint_stream_test.go:1012, tracingcontext_addressing_test.go:425, one_session_only_test.go:77).
- **`releaseSessionSlot` calls NO `Runtime.Close`.** Its whole body is `deregisterSlot` → `removeSlotTree` → `cancelPodMCPIfRuntimeIdle` (slotsession.go:214-220). Only `Shutdown` and `terminateHeldSession` (holdstate.go:251-252) close a runtime on a cleanup path.
- **`ReleaseSlotReservation` gains a `leaked bool` and that adds no mechanism.** `podclaim.SlotClaimer.ReleaseSlot` already takes a fourth `leaked` argument; the shipped `ReleaseSlotReservation` hard-codes the literal `false` for it (slotbinder.go:502) while `Binder.ReleaseSlot` passes a computed one (:559). So `Binder.ReleaseSlot` was NEVER the only producer of `leaked=true`: `BindReservedSlot` sets `sbe.Leaked` and `applySlotRetryPolicy` passes it. Any text saying the parameter does not exist makes CODE-5's staged four-argument call fail to compile.
- **`Binder.Prepare` sets `leaseAssigned = true` at binder.go:954,** two statements after its LAST `reclaim()` site (:950) and before its only success return, so every `Prepare` reclaim sees the flag false and `failPhase`'s credential-release half is unreachable from `Prepare`. `:1053` is inside `Binder.Launch`'s `BindResult` literal, a different function. `Binder.Launch` passes the literal `true` at :998, so the refusal harm is `Launch`'s alone.
- **DECISION: CODE-8 is a closure SIGNATURE change, `reclaim := func(cause error)`, plus an enumerated call-site sweep.** Both reclaim closures are `func()` with no parameters and no returns (binder.go:866-869, :997-1000), and every call site declares its failing error in its own `if`/`else if`, so a guard written into the bodies does not compile in `Prepare` and silently never fires in `Launch`. The parameter is named `cause` rather than `err` because `Prepare` reuses a function-scope `err`. Call sites: :883, :918, :923, :935, :950 in `Prepare`; :1010, :1021, :1032 in `Launch`. The two reconnect-failure early returns call `ReclaimClaimed` instead and are OUTSIDE the sweep.
- **`Binder.Launch`'s only reachable refusal is `SLOT_BIND_ALREADY_STARTED`,** because `Launch` mints no token, so the identity rule cannot fire there. That refusal means a live started session owns the entry, which is why no credential release may sit on the refusal arm: `failPhase`'s release is the session-wide `LeasesBySession` walk and would strip the live session's leases.
- **The `Shutdown` two-field precondition is a wire-visible break on every in-tree test literal, and the compiler cannot see it.** The fields are proto scalars, so a skipped sweep produces no build error and six tiers go red together. The sweep set is `grep -rn "ShutdownRequest{" --include=*_test.go pkg/ tests/` — roughly sixty call sites across roughly twenty-six files in tiers 1, 2, 3, 4, 7a, 9 and 10 — and it is stated by the reproducing grep rather than enumerated, following the precedent CODE-6 set for its own widening. The sweep belongs to the CODE-1 step that introduces the precondition, not to the CODE-4 mint step, because production callers get the field from the builder.
- **`tests/tier11_docs/alert_catalog_crosscheck_test.go` DOES NOT EXIST and never did.** The only file of that basename is `pkg/observability/metrics/alert_catalog_crosscheck_test.go`, a unit-tier `package metrics_test` file whose two tests derive every set they compare from `rules.Catalog()`, `MetricCatalog()` and `AlertSupportCatalog()` at run time. It holds NO name inventory and reads neither `docs/reference/metrics.md` nor `spec/16`, so a counter with no alert referencing it owes it nothing. This CLOSES the standing Open asking whether CODE-9 owes it an entry; the citation must be DELETED rather than repointed.
- **The metric-gate topology, settled and re-derived by six shards.** The only gate holding a metric to BOTH `docs/reference/metrics.md` and §16.1 is `tests/tier11_docs/adapter_metric_catalog_test.go`, whose source of truth is a regex over `pkg/adapter/metrics.go` alone, with `undocumentedAdapterMetrics` and `specCatalogPending` as its only (currently empty) exemptions. `spec161Metrics` in `pkg/observability/metrics/catalog_test.go` is reconciled BOTH ways against `MetricCatalog()` and never against §16.1 prose, and it deliberately OMITS every adapter row carrying the §16.9 scrape deferral. So the ADAPTER series is gated on both catalogs with no test edit and must NOT enter `spec161Metrics`; the GATEWAY series has no docs gate anywhere in the tree.

A nil hook is a silent no-op, so an unwired hook emits nothing while every tier stays green.
- **`pkg/adapter/metrics.go` declares NO methods on `*Server`** — every helper there is a package-level `incX(...)` — and no adapter counter carries a pod label today. CODE-1's staged `s.noteShutdownMetUntokenedEntry(sessionID)` is a `*Server` method, which is what lets it read `s.podID`, and no deliverable declares it.

The one borderline held was DOCS-2's replacement `Shutdown` row stating both "`superseded` ... so nothing was released" and, two sentences later, the general slot-release sentence; not filed, because the staged §4.7 row carries the identical general sentence and delegates it to §4.7.1's named rules, the general-narrowed-by-specific-in-the-same-block pattern is refuted four times over, and DOCS-2 itself argues nobody implements the contract from that page (non-spec-changes.md:2289-2296).

- **DESIGN GENERATION: the consolidation commit `fcac6a13c`.** It rewrote §4.7.1's admission and `Shutdown` cascades into ONE numbered list, rules 1-15, and every other site (the §4.7 row, §7.1, §5.2, §6.2, §15.4) now reaches a rule BY NUMBER. Its own commit message enumerates nine defects it fixed. `git show fcac6a13c -- <spec-changes.md>` is 209 lines and is the highest-yield single command on this proposal; two of one round's three findings came out of it in ten minutes.
- **The consolidation's reduction HELD for the cascade and left five smaller rules unconsolidated:** the reclaim hold's window, the leaked-slot consequences, the whole-pod-scrub residue disposition, the clean-exit/`released` exception, and the claim-deletion retirement trigger. Those five are where every remaining single-source finding lives.
- **DECISION: §15.4's fifteen-row per-rule wire table is DELETED** (`spec.1.fix-G1.1`). §15.4's bind-attempt block is now a pointer at §4.7.1 plus the conformance criterion; the slot-identifier reclaim-hold block is untouched and still publishes `ABORTED`. Every cell the table carried is already in the rule it named, including the `OK` the reviewer thought was new: rule 15 says the outcomes "are answered on a successful RPC". ALTERNATIVES rejected: the reverse reduction (strip status/ErrorCode/category from §4.7.1 and let §15.4 own them), because rule 5's own ordering argument turns on its category; keeping the table with only the non-redundant cells, because what survives is nothing.
- **Four §15.4 references survive the table's deletion and must not be swept:** the reclaim-hold block citations, the "§15.4 publishes non-conformance" criterion citations, the third-party-harness `ABSENT` claim, and every `// spec: §15.4` code citation. Each is about the retained criterion or the retained hold block.
- **DECISION: the reclaim hold ends only on a cleanup that COMPLETED, and otherwise holds for the life of the pod.** Completion is "every act that cleanup owes the slot has returned without error". The code form is `completed := false`, a `defer func(){ if completed { release() } }()`, and `completed = closeErr == nil && treeErr == nil` set after the tree removal. GROUND: spec/05:556 already says "If cleanup fails, the slot is leaked — the pod continues but the slot is not reclaimed until pod termination", so no new mechanism, marker, field or state is invented, and admitting a successor onto a residual `credentials.json` is a credential-isolation fail-open. ALTERNATIVES rejected: a per-slot "unreclaimed" marker (a second object with the hold's own lifetime); accepting the residue and qualifying §5.2's fresh-workspace guarantee; a dedicated permanent status or third error code; a counter or gauge for a held identifier (pulls in spec/16, the alert rules, a runbook and the N4 deferral).
- **The completion predicate differs by site, deliberately, and all three are stated.** `Shutdown` uses `closeErr == nil && treeErr == nil`; `releaseSessionSlot` uses the tree removal alone because it closes no runtime; `terminateHeldSession` uses BOTH after `non-spec-recheck-2.3.fix-G1.1` made it capture `Runtime.Close`'s error, because §5.2 names the runtime close among the acts that cleanup owes the slot and bounds it for that very site at ten seconds.
- **DECISION: CODE-1's cleanup-outcome report stays keyed on `closeErr` ALONE.** The `errors.Join(closeErr, treeErr)` re-key was minted and REVERTED (`non-spec-recheck-2.3.fix-G2.1`), with `slog.Warn("slot_tree_removal_failed", "slot_id", sessionID, "error", treeErr)` recording the failure instead. `exited_cleanly` keeps `closeErr == nil && (live || treeErr == nil)`. See the Trap; the re-key retires a pod on the first failed `os.RemoveAll` at an ordinary session end.
- **SPEC-3 now stages THREE §5.2 anchors, not two.** The action list, the scrub-model append, and a replacement of the `**Slot cleanup:**` bullet's leaked-outcome sentence carrying an exception: a cleanup that closes the runtime session cleanly and fails only in removing the SLOT TREE reports `released` and is not `leaked`. The leaked terminal, the timeout formula and the CRD rule in that bullet all stand unedited.
- **DECISION: the exception's failure predicate is "the slot tree", one term at all four sites,** defined once with a parenthetical naming the workspace directory, the session and artifact directories and the credential directory. BECAUSE `removeSlotTree` is one delegation to `slotlayout.RemoveTree`, which iterates `slotRoot()`, `Sessions`, `Artifacts` and `CredentialsDir`, continues past each failure and returns only the FIRST error, so no caller can learn WHICH removal failed and any spec sentence distinguishing them is unimplementable. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/slot.go:208-212; pkg/adapter/slotlayout/tree.go:58-69.
- **DECISION: CODE-4's attempt-scoped credential release is extended to `Binder.Prepare`'s credential-assignment failure arm.** Both gateway helpers mint EVERY provider's lease before the RPC: `assignSlotCredentials` at slotbinder.go:379 and :393 then the RPC at :403 (caller :307), `assignCredentials` at binder.go:1226 and :1252 then the RPC at :1256 (caller :949). So one rule holds at both bind entry points, unconditional and outside the refusal guard. ALTERNATIVES rejected: a text-only "state the leak truthfully and accept it" (the test bullet is an assertion, so it would pin the leak as required behaviour); releasing only on the refusal arm; a session-wide `ReleaseSession` there (it strips a successor's or a live session's leases).
- **On the base bind path a failure at `assignCredentials` releases NOTHING today.** `reclaim()` calls `failPhase(ctx, sb, leaseAssigned, ...)` and `leaseAssigned` is set true at binder.go:954, AFTER the call site at :949-952, and `failPhase` gates `releaseCredentials` on that flag. A shipped defect this proposal widens by one reachable cause and then closes.
- **`credassign.Service.Release(leaseID)` is an explicit no-op for an unknown lease id** (credassign.go:375-384), so an attempt-scoped release followed later by a session-wide `ReleaseSession` double-releases nothing. `usercreds.Materializer.MintProto` writes into the same lease store, and both `Release` and `ReleaseSession` funnel into one `releaseLocked`, so narrowing from the session walk to per-lease-id drops nothing the shipped path released.
- **DECISION: the structured-warn form for the three newly-recorded adapter failures is fixed once,** in CODE-6's `releaseSessionSlot` clause: `slot_tree_removal_failed` and `runtime_close_failed`, both carrying `slot_id` and `error`, matching the `slot_guard_not_acquired` form. All three names collide with nothing (`grep -rn` over `*.go` returns zero), and snake_case slog names are already the tree's convention.
- **The `log/slog` import state is settled per file.** `pkg/adapter/holdstate.go` already imports it; `pkg/adapter/slotsession.go` does not (context/sort/codes/status); `pkg/adapter/session.go` imports `log` and `errors` but not `log/slog`. The staging covers both gaps. `errors` being present is why `errors.Join` needed no import edit while it stood.
- **DECISION: the §4.7 `Shutdown` row's slot-release predicate is "whenever the request REMOVES an entry", not "whenever the adapter HOLDS an entry".** Rule 13 holds an entry and performs neither teardown, so the old predicate is false. The consolidation fixed the row and left the retired predicate in the Design paragraph, which `spec.1` then filed and fixed.
- **DECISION: "A superseded reclaim is neither a failed reclaim nor a leaked slot." is DELETED from the §4.7 row,** and the row's clause narrowed to "; this row restates neither". The reader who needs the not-leaked fact meets it at §7.1's clean-exit sentence and at the §16.1 counter row.
- **DECISION: §7.1 CITES §4.7.1 for the `superseded` and `absent` meanings rather than restating them.** That is what makes §15.4's "each stated once, by that section" and §4.7.1's "stated here and nowhere else" true with no edit to either. Consequence: summary.md's SPEC-2 bullet must stop crediting §7.1 with "the meaning of the superseded and absent answers".
- **DECISION: §4.7.1's attempt-mismatch rule is narrowed to "No `Shutdown` naming a bind attempt removes such an entry; within this cascade only the unconditional-teardown rule does."** BECAUSE three shipped adapter paths release an untokened entry with no `Shutdown` at all, so the old "released only under the unconditional-teardown rule or by the pod's retirement" was false: `Server.DemoteSDK` (sdkwarm.go:296-298), `ConfigureWorkspace`'s three failure rollbacks (sdkwarm.go:236,:241,:251) and the §10.1.4 hold-timeout termination (holdstate.go:190). A rule block scoped to `Shutdown` cannot carry a cross-section inventory of entry removal without rotting.
- **The refusal a later bind meets at an UNTOKENED entry is the STARTED-SESSION rule, never the attempt identity rule.** The identity rule's condition requires the resolved entry to carry a NON-EMPTY token, so it never reaches an untokened entry. Two staged sites mis-attributed it and were corrected.
- **DECISION: §4.7.9 step 5's trailing "rather than leaving the pod holding the session's state" is DELETED,** leaving the sentence a bare pointer at §7.1. The outcome half was false exactly for the one-session pod the staged §7.1 paragraph carves out. This LANDS the last known un-narrowed universal of the one-session carve-out sweep, and the sweep grep now returns no site asserting that a pre-`running` bind failure leaves nothing on the pod unconditionally.
- **DECISION: §29.4 step 13 CITES §4.7 for the graceful-shutdown signal's co-tenancy condition and states only that the step does not occur when the end leaves a bound co-tenant.** The verbatim-reuse defence was replaced by the single-home rationale: identity today is not a mechanism for identity tomorrow.
- **DECISION: the two long Design paragraphs were rewritten as one edit** (`spec.1.fix-G3.1`), reducing each to its design choices plus citations of the staged blocks, in the form the already-reduced paragraphs at spec-changes.md:116-122 and :136-141 use. The Design section is proposal-only rationale; nothing under spec/, docs/, schemas/ or charts/ quotes it, so trimming it falsifies no tree file.
- **DECISION: the whole-pod scrub's coverage, verification and `onScrubFailure` disposition stay in §5.2; §6.2's pre-`running` paragraph cites them and keeps only what it owns** (which of the pod's two exits ends a one-session pod's pre-`running` residue, and that the scrub reaches neither the registry entry nor the armed §4.9 timers). Both staged enumerations were third and fourth statements of a shipped §5.2 procedure.
- **DECISION: the claim-deletion retirement trigger stays stated in full at SPEC-4's two shipped-parallel landings** (the §6.2 fence trigger list and §4.6.1's projection bullet) and is cut to a citation in §6.2's post-fence prose and in the Design paragraph. The fence/§4.6.1 pair is pre-existing shipped duplication (spec/04:416, spec/06:80) this proposal must re-key in both places; the third and fourth copies are the ones the staging minted.
- **DECISION: the closed entry-creating-RPC list lives in §4.7.1's `**Admission.**` preamble and §5.2 cites it.** §4.7.1 owns the gateway-adapter RPC contract and its numbered rules quantify over that set; §5.2 owns the hold. Rule 2 delegates the hold's scope and refusal to §5.2 while §5.2 takes the request set from §4.7.1: two different facts crossing, not a citation loop. Do not "fix" it.
- **§10.1.4's hold-timeout pass does NOT terminate the pod.** It lowers the coordinator-hold gauge and terminates only the sessions the adapter started; the adapter process and the pod survive and keep serving. CORRECTS an earlier note that read "the pod is self-terminating on that path". EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/holdstate.go:160-205.
- **`onHoldTimeout` clears `s.hold.active` at holdstate.go:184 BEFORE pass 1 at :190,** so by the time pass 2 runs the hold-state interceptor allowlist refuses nothing and every entry-creating RPC is served again. The reclaim hold is the only thing standing between a failed cleanup and a successor bind at that site, which is why its predicate is load-bearing rather than cosmetic.
- **`terminateHeldSession` calls `s.noteRuntimeClosed` unconditionally at holdstate.go:251,** so `runtimeLive` believes the session is gone even when the close it follows failed. That is why the hold, rather than `runtimeLive`, has to carry the failed-close case.
- **The adapter-side `leaked` scrub outcome feeds only `drainLedger.RecordLeak` and the per-release `maxSessionsPerPod` retirement; it does NOT touch occupancy,** and it never moves `lenny_adapter_leaked_slots`, whose only production producer is `slotstate.Registry.MarkLeaked` from the bind-failure accounting at start.go:2844. A lens pricing a new `leaked` producer must not assume the gauge moves.
- **`Binder.Resume` sends NO workspace, setup or credential RPC.** Its only pod-side calls are `connect` (resolveSandbox/DialAdapter/NegotiateVersion) and `cl.Resume`, with the checkpoint chunks carried ON the `Resume` request. This is what keeps SPEC-3's and SPEC-4's stage-scoped one-session sentences from being false for an exclusive §7.3 re-attach, and it is the first thing to check before re-deriving that tension. EVIDENCE: podsession/binder.go:1590-1619.
- **`AssignCredentials` has exactly two adapter-side senders and both sit inside a bind attempt,** so CODE-6's `allowStarted: false` on that resolve cannot refuse a credential rotation or renewal. There is no mid-session `AssignCredentials` path to the adapter; proactive renewal goes through `RotateCredentials`, which resolves via the read-only `slotStateLocked`.
- **The production adapter-`Shutdown` sender set is FIVE sites behind ONE builder, re-verified in five separate rounds of this window:** slotbinder.go:542, :574, binder.go:2037, :2043, cmd/lenny-gateway/user_revocation.go:129, all funnelling through the unexported `Client.shutdown` at adapterclient/client.go:813. The only non-test `adapterv1.ShutdownRequest{}` literal is client.go:814. The §11.4 revoke fan-out uses the same builder, so it cannot hit the two-field precondition's `INVALID_ARGUMENT`.
- **The `UnconditionalTeardown: true` test sweep set is tiers 0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 7a, 9 and 10, which is exactly what S16's tier line lists.** `grep -rln "ShutdownRequest{" --include=*_test.go pkg/ tests/` returns 64 hits across 26 files and nothing outside `pkg/` and `tests/`.
- **`removeSlotTree` can only return non-nil through `slotlayout.RemoveTree`, which `os.RemoveAll`s four directories and returns nil for a path that does not exist.** No shipped test can force `treeErr` non-nil, which is why the staged `Server.removeSlotTreeFn` seam exists and why it is wired on the `Shutdown` path alone.
- **`slotlayout.EnsureTree` is `os.MkdirAll` plus `os.Chmod` per directory and is documented idempotent,** so a successor materializes INTO a residual tree rather than over a fresh one. That is the ground under the completion-keyed hold.
- **The staged §5.2 "graceful window of ten seconds" for the §10.1 hold-timeout termination is the shipped constant** at holdstate.go:201, not a figure the proposal invents, and it is NOT in spec/10 (which states only the 120s `coordinatorHoldTimeoutSeconds`). In the shipped tree it is ONE budget shared across every member of pass 2; CODE-6 splits it into a guard-acquisition deadline plus a per-member ten-second close context.
- **The per-member close-context split does not regress the pass's worst case.** `SocketRuntimeProcess.Close` takes the sibling early return for a non-last session and returns nil immediately, so only the last member ever consumes the grace, which is the premise the shipped shared-budget comment states.
- **The start-confirmation undo cannot destroy a successor's live runtime session.** The window from `s.Runtime.Start` returning to `s.noteRuntimeStarted` is one line (session.go:155, :163), so a reclaim plus a whole successor bind plus the successor's own start cannot interleave. Promoted from the archive because rule 8's text reads as though the undo fires against a successor's entry, so a fresh reliability lens re-derives it every time.
- **The agent pod's `RestartPolicy` is `Never`** (podspec.go:980, pinned by podspec_test.go:94-95), so an adapter crash ends the pod rather than restarting the container in place. This kills "the hold is in-memory, so 'for the life of the pod' is unmeetable" outright.
- **`ProjectOccupancyPhase` takes exactly four inputs** — `Current`, `HasClaim`, `Binding`, `RewarmStarted` — and the recycle setting is NOT one of them; its own doc comment says so. With no claim it switches on the pod's phase alone: `Reserved → Idle`, `Claimed → Draining`, everything else `("", false)`. SPEC-4's re-keying transcribes that, so it adds no churn and tightens the scrub-before-idle invariant.
- **§4.6.1, §6.2's projection prose and §6.2's fence all state the occupancy projection in the SHIPPED tree,** so SPEC-4 has to keep three copies in sync; the fourth copy, in §6.2's pre-`running` prose, is the one the staging minted and the one that was cut.
- **`docs/reference/state-machines.md:138` is the ONLY docs mirror of the projection,** and it carries all three replaced clauses verbatim. §4.6.1's parallel bullets have no docs mirror, and no tier-11 gate holds that page against §6.2 or §4.6.1.
- **The docs mirror set for this proposal is exactly four pages and all four are staged:** state-machines.md (DOCS-1), adapter-contract.md (DOCS-2), error-catalog.md (DOCS-3), metrics.md (CODE-9). Re-confirmed in five separate rounds of this window. `docs/api/internal.md` documents a retired RPC set and names no bind-sequence RPC, so it is not a mirror.
- **Nothing gates `docs/reference/error-catalog.md`.** `grep -rn error-catalog tests/ scripts/ cmd/ Makefile` returns nothing, and this scope call now has a home in the summary's shipped-defects section rather than only inside the staged prose.
- **The gateway-side metric catalog has NO sweep holding a series to both §16.1 and `docs/reference/metrics.md`.** `tests/tier11_docs/adapter_metric_catalog_test.go` reads `pkg/adapter/metrics.go` alone; `tests/tier2_component/observability/catalog_crosscheck_test.go` is the ALERT catalog. So the adapter-side counter is gated in both directions and the gateway-side counter is gated nowhere. This closes the standing Open that asked.
- **The ordering S6-before-S10 is load-bearing for tier 11.** SPEC-6 lands the adapter series' §16.1 row before CODE-9 registers it; any reordering that puts the registration first turns tier 11 red.
- **`cmd/lenny-compliance/schemaassert.go` DERIVES its "closed §15.1 error-code catalog" by regexing `enum ErrorCode` out of the embedded proto,** so SCHEMA-1's two new values widen that conformance catalog rather than breaking it, and both its tests stay green because both sides regenerate. The proto enum's "The catalog below mirrors spec §15.1" comment is already loose in the tree (six shipped values have no §15.1 row) and no test reconciles the two.
- **`## Impacts on other proposals` now carries TEN proposal rows:** 0080, 0073, 0075, 0078, 0072, 0079, 0077, 0071, R1b and R12. The 0073 row gained the CODE-6 close-context replacement (the shared pass context at holdstate.go:197-203 is 0073's own deliverable, by `git blame` to commit 3997f502b2); 0077 is an early draft staging no text, whose per-slot derivation at slot assignment is newly constrained by CODE-6's entry-creation gate; 0071 collides in two test files (`socketruntime_test.go` and `tests/spec-map.json`) and its CODE-1 deletes `deliverToSession`, against which four sites narrate the third residue symptom.
- **`## Open decisions for human to make` now carries 20, 21, 22 and 27.** Entry 26 (whether a hand-maintained `docs/reference/error-catalog.md` needs a gate) was ANSWERED and deleted by the `f8` firing, its substance moved to the defects section; identifier 26 is retired and must not be reused. 21 and 27 carry a recommendation with ground, alternatives and a confidence; 20 and 22 carry the question and its ground alone.
- **`## Defects in the shipped tree that this proposal does not stage` carries twenty-three entries** after `f8` added the DOCS-3 reconciliation-gate entry beside the §15.1 missing-catalog-rows entry. The `## Deliverable index` is last, twenty deliverable lines plus its trailing testing paragraph, and its DOCS-3 line is present.
- **`**Accepted failure modes.**` sits at the end of `## Non-goals` and has been left there by every firing from `f1` to `f8`.** It is a bold-labelled block rather than a heading and rather than one of the four parts `## Summary` takes; several "recorded under the accepted failure modes" pointers resolve to it, so a later pass that rehomes it must move it verbatim.
- **The §7.4 mid-session admission guard resolves the binding from the replica's own `podRegistry` and answers 409 `TARGET_NOT_READY` when it holds none,** so a foreign attempt cannot obtain one. The shipped `PrepareWorkspace` call at upload_to_session.go:128 carries no mid-session marker today; only the finalize at :134 does.
- **The §5.2 whole-pod scrub is verified clause by clause against the staged claims, again:** step 0 purges every per-slot `credentials.json`, step 2 is `rm -rf /workspace/slots/*`, step 6 stat-checks `/workspace/slots/`, `/tmp`, `/dev/shm` and `/run/lenny/slots/` and marks the scrub failed on residue, `onScrubFailure: warn` is the default returning the pod with a `scrub_warning` annotation, `maxScrubFailures` defaults to 3, and step 7 is the `vm-restart` retirement.
- **`pkg/adapter/podscrub.go` is purely on-disk.** It enumerates children of `/workspace/slots` and `/run/lenny/slots` and touches neither `s.slots` nor the §4.9 timers, so SPEC-4's "the registry entry and the armed timers are not reached by that scrub" is true.

### Traps

- **Do not re-gate the cleanup-outcome report on `st.started`.** "Simplifying" the two predicates back into one reintroduces the double report through CODE-2's rollback and advances `sessions_served` for a session that never ran. The tier-1 "Claimed but not yet recorded" case exists to fail when that happens.
- **Do not gate the runtime teardown on `runtimeLive` either.** The tempting local fix for the mismatched sentence is to move pkg/adapter/session.go to `runtimeLive` so the code matches the spec text. It inverts the fail-closed direction: CODE-2's rollback fires only when `Runtime.Start` returns, so a start that hangs would leave the runtime serving the abandoned session forever. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/session.go:155-163.
- **Do not widen §6.2's fence to move `slot_cleanup ──→ leaked` into the either-concurrency block.** It is the obvious way to make a prose terminal claim true, and it changes shipped state-machine semantics, cascades into docs/reference/state-machines.md:251, and breaks the tier-11 matched-pair test. G1 removed the terminal claim from SPEC-4's prose instead, which is the smaller fix.
- **Do not edit spec/06:152 or docs/reference/state-machines.md:235.** The advice holds and the reason pass 1 recorded for it does not. The annotation reads "workspace ready, session dispatched to runtime with its session identifier", which is the runtime-has-been-given boundary (`runtimeLive`) rather than the admitted-start boundary, and SPEC-4's prose anchors to it rather than replacing it, which is what keeps DOCS-1 to the single added row it stages. An agent trusting the old gloss concludes SPEC-4's prose mis-cites the annotation when it does not. The residual "dispatched versus acknowledged" question is now CLOSED: the staged prose no longer attributes its boundary to the trigger at all. See the Settled entry on the closed `running` boundary.
- **Do not move the SPEC-2 block below §7.1's fence.** §7.1's atomicity paragraph physically sits inside the fenced flow listing, between the step-8 line and the line continuing it, so any paragraph inserted after it also lands inside the fence. The staged instruction says so explicitly. Below the fence it would sit among the `uploadToken` prose. EVIDENCE: spec/07:5,23,24,54.
- **Do not put a per-slot scrub rule in §5.2's `**Slot cleanup:**` bullet.** The bullet is under a `maxConcurrentSessions > 1` heading, so on a single-session pod §4.7's unqualified `ReportSessionScrub` row would govern instead. The concurrency-independent home is the `**Scrub model.**` paragraph at spec/05:453.
- **TRIED AND WITHDRAWN: the no-retry rule.** SPEC-2 originally said the failed attempt is not retried. Priced honestly that is roughly eleven mirrored exception clauses across §5.2, §6.2, §15.1, §29 and three error-catalog rows, plus a new `SlotReason` value (§5.2's "Client error on exhaustion" sets `error.category` from the failure reason, and an unacknowledged reclaim is a disposition rather than a reason), and it withdraws availability during exactly the partial pod outage that made the reclaim fail. The placement rule replaced it. Do not re-derive the retryability framing.
- **TRIED AND WITHDRAWN, and do not go the other way either: deleting the no-retry clause outright.** With the retry unconstrained and a lagging `Shutdown` still executing under the same identifier there are two unfenced outcomes. (1) The reclaim lands between the retry's `PrepareWorkspace` and its `StartSession`: `claimSessionSlot` re-creates an empty entry and tree and starts the session on it silently, and CODE-2's survived-the-start confirm does not catch it, because the entry it checks is the one its own claim just recreated. (2) The reclaim lands after the retry is running and tears down a healthy session. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/slotsession.go:74-90.
- **TRIED AND WITHDRAWN: fencing the lagging `Shutdown` by generation.** It looks like a free answer because `ShutdownRequest` already carries `coordination_generation`. The generation is per-session-coordinator and does not advance between two attempts of the same session on the same replica, and adding a per-attempt field is blocked by programme rule S-2, which reserves the single `schemas/lenny-adapter.proto` edit to step R1b.
- **MISTAKE: the proposal had already found and mispriced the placement exclusion.** non-spec-changes.md:574-579 rejected it as "the larger alternative ... not staged here", pricing only its code cost and never pricing the spec-contradiction surface the no-retry rule creates. Priced honestly it is four small Go edits and two spec clauses against roughly eleven spec and doc sites plus a new reason value. Do not re-derive that bullet's conclusion; the mispricing is the finding.
- **MISTAKE: the report rule was stated twice with opposite content.** SPEC-2 had the racing `StartSession` report the reclaim outcome while SPEC-3 forbade any report for the same reclaim. It cost a full round, and the cost was structural rather than verbal: each block stated its own version of one rule instead of citing a single statement of it. This is why the rule now lives in one paragraph that the others cite.
- **MISTAKE: a `leaked` `ReportSessionScrub` was used as a "this pod is bricked, retire it" side-channel** (non-spec-changes.md:189-195,:584-588). That is an accounting RPC repurposed as a health signal, and it cost the proposal a self-contradiction with SPEC-3 plus a double `sessions_served` advance. It was reached for because the ledger is not concurrency-gated (see the next trap), so a single `leaked` report really does retire the pod on the spot.
- **The leak ledger is not concurrency-gated.** `UnhealthyThreshold(1) == 1`, so one `leaked` report on a `maxConcurrentSessions: 1` pod stamps `lenny.dev/drain-request` immediately. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/recycle/scrubreporter_seams.go:184-197; slothealth.go:121-140.
- **The "bricked pod" premise is real but not distinctive to the racing rollback.** `SocketRuntimeProcess.Close` closes the listener whenever the released session was the last active one, and the listener is bound exactly once at adapter start. An ordinary session end reaches the same state through the same call, so retiring only on the racing path fixes no class. This is why deleting the report is safe.
- **`Binder.DrainSandbox` removes nothing from placement synchronously.** It only stamps the `lenny.dev/drain-request` annotation for the WarmPoolController, and `ClaimSlot`'s pass-1 scan of claimed pods reads the per-pod claim and never the Sandbox phase. Any claim that "at `maxConcurrentSessions` 2 the drained pod is no longer a candidate" is wrong. EVIDENCE: podsession/slotbinder.go:601; podclaim/slotclaimer.go:95-107,:417-469.
- **MISTAKE (nearly filed): the staged "not retried" clause does not ban every post-first-RPC retry.** It is grammatically governed by "A reclaim the adapter does not acknowledge", so it bans the retry only for the unacknowledged-reclaim class. Read the sentence's subject before filing on it. The parenthetical this entry used to carry, that `applySlotRetryPolicy` skips the retry when `sbe.Leaked`, is stale under the placement design: CODE-5 and its tier-1 case assert the retry STILL runs on a leaked disposition and only carries `ExcludePod`. Read the current CODE-5 text rather than any older gloss of the retry rule.
- **Dead end: "the adapter cannot see this leak".** `lenny_adapter_leaked_slots` is emitted by the gateway (gatewaymetrics_credential.go:219-223) despite the name and despite §6.2 calling it adapter health metadata, and `applySlotRetryPolicy` already marks a slot leaked without the adapter knowing. Withholding the adapter's report does not blind the gauge; CODE-5's `accountSlotFailure` feeds it from the gateway side. Two lenses chased this and both refuted themselves.
- **Dead end: "the withheld report contradicts §4.7 or §12".** §4.7's `ReportSessionScrub` row, spec/05:453 and spec/12:481 all scope their reporting rule to "a session release", and the proposal deliberately frames the pre-start reclaim as not one, so the whole family of findings gets refuted. What survives is the `leaked` terminal, which spec/06:160 requires to be counted with no scoping to session releases.
- **Dead end: "SPEC-3's insert does not reach an exclusive pool because §5.2's bullet is concurrency-scoped".** spec/05:395 and :453 already make slots and the per-slot cleanup uniform across concurrencies, and the shipped §4.7 `ReportSessionScrub` row generalises the per-slot cleanup to every session release with no concurrency qualifier (spec/04:692). The scoping ambiguity is pre-existing. Do not file it.
- **Dead end: "`releases the slot reservation afterwards` contradicts the leaked hold".** `ReleaseSlot(leaked=true)` skips the decrement and the occupancy-zero disposition, so the two are compatible. Three lenses checked this and dropped the finding.
- **Dead end: "the §7.1 rollback omits credential-lease release".** §7.1 step 23 is the existing spec basis and `failPhase` already cites it for the pre-attached failure path, so the code staging's `releaseCredentials` is anchored. EVIDENCE: podsession/binder.go:1065-1078,:1259-1268.
- **Do not read the reclaim as scoped away from pods that retire.** "The pre-attached disposition governs the pod; this reclaim governs the slot state on a pod that is released or reused rather than terminated" cannot mean that: on the concurrent path a failed bind whose slot was the pod's last occupant also retires the pod (`ReleaseSlot` deletes the claim at `remaining==0`), and the proposal compensates there. EVIDENCE: podclaim/slotclaimer.go:880-885.
- **`§15.4.2` is the repo's established citation for the CH-RUNTIMEOPS `terminate` signal,** even though the frame's own card is §28.5.3 and §15.4.2 only names the DRAINING state. Do not file it as a false citation; the tree already cites it that way. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/drain_test.go:12,:53.
- **The `slot_cleanup ──→ leaked` scoping asymmetry is pre-existing.** The same asymmetry already applies to the shipped `running ──→ slot_cleanup` edge, `slotstate` ignores the concurrency scoping entirely, and §5.2 and §6.2's leaked-semantics prose state the outcome unconditionally. Three lenses judged it below the bar. A later round asking why the leaked terminal is unreachable on a single-session pod should know it is structural.
- **The leaked-occupancy hold has no durable backing, and that is pre-existing.** The Redis `active_slots` counter's only rehydration source is `SessionStore.GetActiveSlotsByPod` over Postgres rows with `state='active'`, and a failed bind has no active row. The same hole already exists for the shipped leaked class. A later round wanting to close it should widen §5.2's rehydration text rather than §7.1.
- **Dead end, closed on the refute side: the `concurrent_slots_exhausted` gloss.** spec/05:549 reads "pods exist but all slots are full", which does not literally describe a pool whose only candidate was disqualified. It is ALREADY loose in the shipped tree: `ClaimSlot` returns `ErrNoConcurrentSlot` whenever the pool list is non-empty and no candidate is placeable, `ErrTenantMismatch` maps to the same reason, and candidates skipped by `expiredByUptime` do too. Adding one more skip reason does not falsify a gloss that already over-claims. Five lenses reached it and each refuted itself. EVIDENCE: podclaim/slotclaimer.go:505-522; podclaimerror_internal_test.go:87-88.
- **Citation ranges to normalise, not to file.** Several proposal citations give `SocketRuntimeProcess.Close` as `:441-467` and the listener bind as `:155-161`; the real spans are `:435-467` and `:156-161`. The cited ranges sit inside the real ones rather than being plainly false, so they were left alone. A future citation check should normalise them.
- **Settled, formerly disputed: "On the default disposition the pod is replaced." (spec/04:686) has exactly ONE site.** The pass-1 refutation asserted the sentence also appears verbatim at spec/29:696. It does not: `grep -rn` over spec/ and docs/ returns spec/04:686 alone, and §29 item 12 carries a differently-worded restatement ("the adapter closes the session runtime and the pod is replaced"). Two lenses confirmed this independently. The refutation's conclusion still stands, because the sentence is already loose today for any per-slot `Shutdown` on a co-tenanted pod, so the proposal does not introduce the looseness; its evidence does not. A reviewer arguing a missed §29 edit site from the two-site claim will find only one site. UNVERIFIED: whether the row wants "on the default disposition at the session's release" is still undecided.
- **Disputed, unresolved: is the "workspace-prep handler inserts the entry after the reclaim was answered" race filable?** `spec.1.review-security.1` says no, because both `resolvePrepareStagingDir` and `FinalizeWorkspace` call `ensureSlotPaths` at the top of the handler, before the slow work, so a client-side deadline almost always postdates the insert. `spec.1.review-reliability.1` says the handler runs `ensureSlotPaths` with no ctx check even on an expired deadline and a code-lane reviewer should look. Neither corrects the other; both agree the remedy would be an adapter-side tombstone, which the summary rejects as a non-goal.
- **MISTAKE, refuted at least eight times: "§7.1 says the exclusive pod retires while §6.2:283 and §7.2 step 3 release it back to the pool".** Every dress of this has been built and dropped: false citation, unreachable disposition, the residue riding a pooled pod back into inventory, and the tenant-isolation framing. The §6.2 fence retires a failed pod, spec/05:455 says so outright, and the original refutation quoted spec/06:234's release-to-pool clause and reasoned past it. A later round wanting it needs NEW evidence that a pooled replacement pod actually keeps the residue, which means reading the mid-resume terminal handler's release path in the gateway and showing it is not a drain. Filing it on the same evidence costs two verifiers and closes nothing.
- **MISTAKE, four separate occurrences: the bound/started drift.** Three fixers restated the runtime-teardown precondition as "every bound entry the call removed"; the §29.4 rationale that carried the third is now rewritten onto §29.4's own Preconditions paragraph and that finding is discharged, so do not re-file it. `bound ⊋ started`: `assignCredentialsSlot` binds at AssignCredentials while only `claimSessionSlotUnderLock` sets `started`. The fourth occurrence runs the other way: a sentence that names the predicate correctly but assumes the `exited_cleanly` signal is uniform across a range the predicate splits. Check every new sentence that names the runtime teardown against §4.7's "a session whose start the adapter has admitted", and every new sentence that reasons about `exited_cleanly` against the `started` boundary as well as the `bound` one. In each case the conclusion was right and only the stated ground was wrong, so the fix is the predicate phrase rather than the decision. Do not run the drift the other way either, which a round-4 lens nearly did against CODE-2: `claimSessionSlotUnderLock` sets `st.sessionID` AND `st.started` together (slotsession.go:87-88), so CODE-2's guard `!ok || st.sessionID != sessionID` is satisfied at every `noteRuntimeStarted` call site, SDK-warm (sdkwarm.go:261, on the `fresh` arm after the claim) and `Resume` (resume.go:50 then :144) included. `assignCredentialsSlot` binds EARLIER; it is not the only binder, and the guard drops no record.
- **MISTAKE that cost a round: a narrow conclusion generalised into blanket cover.** A WATCHOUT closed with "The same holds for item 13's `terminate` frame. Do not file it as a missed edit site." What it had actually established was only that item 13 is not falsified by the TWO-TEARDOWN SPLIT, because item 13 is scoped to a session that ran. The co-tenancy gate is a DIFFERENT sentence of SPEC-1 and bites exactly the session ends item 13 enumerates, so item 13 was a real edit site and round 5 had to find it again. When writing a "do not file" note, say which sentence of the change it clears.
- **TRIED AND WITHDRAWN, four alternatives to §5.2's placement rule.** A third carrier that clears the row's §4.6 pod binding (a new gateway store write, and `ClaimSlot` pass 1 still admits a same-tenant claimed pod with free capacity, so it does not even deliver a different pod); a durable per-row exclusion (changes the created-state pod binding from create through start); an adapter tombstone of answered reclaims (touches pkg/adapter/slot.go and slotsession.go, which a later campaign position rewrites, and summary.md:154 already prices it); and deleting the constraint outright, which the standing trap already bars.
- **Do not write that a retried start inherits the first attempt's credential file or its armed §4.9 timers.** Only the slot TREE is inherited. `assignCredentialsSlot` rewrites both wholesale from the request's lease set, and a stale timer no-ops against a re-created entry. A reviewer's suggested fix over-claimed at exactly this point, and landing it would have added two new false claims.
- **Do not write "the retried start re-binds cleanly" in the acknowledged arm.** The reservation was released on failure and the retry re-reserves nothing, so the retried session runs on occupancy the counter no longer counts. That is pre-existing and deliberately outside this proposal, but an arm that asserts cleanliness is next round's finding.
- **`running` is bound vocabulary and every state-word occurrence is backticked.** Do not introduce a second, unbackticked sense (for example "ending a session the gateway believes is running") next to a bullet whose backticked `running` an earlier group is protecting. Say "takes the session off the shared runtime process" instead.
- **"pre-`running`" collides with an established term at a different level:** the session states `created`, `ready`, `starting` and `finalizing`. Do not simplify §5.2's corrected sentence back to a bare "pre-`running`". Three surviving "pre-`running`" phrases in the proposal are LABELS for the rule, the edge or the paragraph rather than range assertions, and a grep-and-replace that rewrites them adds words and no content.
- **Do NOT touch the "A start that races the reclaim" bullet's closing sentence** ("Neither ordering reports a cleanup outcome, because the slot never reached `running`"). Round 3 adjudicated it true-as-scoped and four later lenses re-derived the temptation. It reads like the same defect as the deleted §7.1 sentence and is not. If a round does file on it, the correction is the predicate ("the reclaim finds no session the runtime holds"), never the conclusion.
- **Do NOT touch spec/07:220's "Pre-attach terminal collapse" paragraph.** No replacement pod is claimed on those edges, so "no live workspace or running runtime to seal" stays true, and the §7.2 preamble deletion sharpens the contrast rather than breaking it. Five lenses have swept it as a candidate matching site.
- **Do NOT extend the §29 co-tenancy carve-out to §29.9 item 4b.** That bullet records that the specification does not state at what point of the eviction path the `terminate` frame is sent, which is an ordering claim the co-tenancy gate does not answer.
- **The §29.4 step-13 anchor is NOT unique, and it now occurs FOUR times.** "([§15.4.3](...), §28.5.3)." occurs at spec/29:645, :711, :887 and :982; :711 is the intended site. CORRECTED: earlier passes recorded two sites and then three. The instruction resolves only because it scopes itself to "§29.4's numbered step 13". Do not drop that scoping if the block is reworded, and do not report the anchor as unique in a sweep.
- **Do not file the §5.2 retry-policy trigger reading.** "The policy is triggered by an adapter-emitted slot-failure event for a running session, so SPEC-2's placement constraint reaches no bind failure" is a real tension between §5.2:544-557 and §6.2:283-286, and it is pre-existing: the code settles it the other way (`applySlotRetryPolicy` branches on `*podsession.SlotBindError`) and §5.2's own non-retryable list includes a bind-time reason. Three lenses dropped it.
- **Do not file the cross-replica actor problem in §7.2.** Step 3's "the connection that attempt still holds" against step 4's "fencing any stale coordinator" looks like a mis-assigned actor. Step 1 ("cancel the in-flight restoration RPCs") already presumes the sequence's replica owns those RPCs, and the reclaim is in fact issued by the failing attempt's own goroutine, so step 3 inherits the assumption rather than introducing it. Four lenses.
- **Do not file either `leaked` gloss.** §6.2:148's `slot_cleanup ──→ leaked` annotation and §5.2:562's whole-pod replacement trigger both gloss `leaked` as "cleanup timeout exceeded", which does not describe a reclaim the adapter never answered. §5.2's own bullet already says "If cleanup fails, the slot is leaked", broader than either gloss, and the shipped code already sets `leaked = err != nil || !cleanly` on the ordinary session end. Three lenses.
- **Do not file the `slot_cleanup ──→ released` annotation.** "(slot workspace removed, processes killed, slot released)" is today a word-for-word mirror of §5.2's three-action list, and SPEC-3 widens that list to five, so the annotation becomes an abbreviated gloss of a longer list. An abbreviated annotation states nothing false, and the structurally identical §5.2/§6.4 enumeration finding was already refuted. Three lenses, one of them standing next to the row while editing DOCS-1.
- **Do not file §11.4 revoke step 3.** "The pod's runtime adapter initiates graceful shutdown (SIGTERM to agent, wait up to 10s, then SIGKILL)" sits under a list scoped to "all active sessions", which are `started` by construction, and it is already false for any per-slot `Shutdown` on a co-tenanted pod. Pre-existing looseness this proposal widens rather than creates. A round that widens the revoke's scope should re-check the line.
- **Do not file §4.1's retained second sentence as drift this proposal creates.** Before the edit, sentence 2 already said "per-slot teardown"/"whole-pod teardown" while sentence 3 said "per-session teardown"/"whole-pod scrub". The edit adds a naming mismatch to a pre-existing one. It stays the standing OPEN on vocabulary, and four lenses have declined it.
- **Do not file the §4.7 "the pod's session mode" term collision.** The specification binds "session mode" to `executionMode: session`, while the axis that selects the starting RPC is pod-warm versus SDK-warm, which the same §4.7 table already spells two rows above. Four lenses derived it and all four declined it as wording. The fix is two words and belongs outside the loop; a fifth derivation is waste.
- **Dead end, and here are its five sites: "the withheld report contradicts §4.7 or §12".** spec/04:692, spec/05:453, spec/12:481 and :494, schemas/lenny-adapter.proto:308-319 and docs/reference/adapter-contract.md:81 all scope their reporting rule to "a session release", and the proposal frames the pre-start reclaim as not one. Grepping `ReportSessionScrub` across spec/, docs/ and schemas/ lands on exactly this set. CORRECTED: spec/05:545 was in that list and does not belong there. Its `**Slot cleanup:**` bullet reads "On slot completion or failure ... The adapter reports each slot cleanup outcome (`released` or `leaked`)", which is a trigger rather than a session-release scoping. The dead-end verdict has survived many rounds anyway, so a round that wants to re-open the bullet-versus-scrub-model tension must argue from the bullet's OWN trigger and from SPEC-3's new pointer sentence (spec-changes.md:520), never from the "session release" framing.
- **MISTAKE nearly filed: the recycle-disposition `Shutdown` against §5.2:459.** "The adapter closes the ending session's runtime" is not falsified by SPEC-1's no-op sentence. The two-RPC split is an implementation fact; the spec still models one request carrying the disposition beside the teardown, so the applied spec is self-consistent and the divergence is pre-existing.
- **MISTAKE nearly filed twice: the withheld report as a residual-state relaxation.** Neither "a tenant drives N pre-`running` attempts onto one pod without advancing `recycle.maxSessionsPerPod`" nor "SPEC-3 relaxes the residual-state bound" clears the bar. Today's failed-bind path sends no `Shutdown` at all, so the counter does not advance there either, and the per-slot cleanup the proposal ADDS is what reclaims the residue. The whole-pod scrub and `maxScrubFailures` remain the backstops.
- **MISTAKE nearly filed: the reclaim as a deadlock sink.** "§7.2 step 1 stops the 300s `resuming` watchdog and step 3 then blocks on an unbounded adapter RPC, so `resuming` becomes the deadlock sink §6.2 forbids" does not survive: the code lane bounds the reclaim with `slotCleanupBudget` (5s floor), the Design paragraph names §5.2's figure, and §7.1's own leaked disposition presupposes the gateway gives up. Two lenses.
- **MISTAKE nearly filed: the replica-local leak ledger at multi-replica scale.** `slothealth.Tracker.leaked` and `.events` are in-process maps, so a pod may never reach `ceil(maxConcurrentSessions/2)` while its cluster-wide Redis occupancy stays held. The fact is true and the finding is a close variant of the already-refuted occupancy-hold family, whose refutation granted the other retirement routes (`recycle.maxSessionsPerPod`, `maxPodUptimeSeconds`) as the bound.
- **MISTAKE nearly filed: the create-time-reserved bullet's unscoped `leaked`.** A create-time-reserved slot exists only on a concurrent pool, so the bullet's unqualified `leaked` is correct on its own domain and is not the unscoped-`leaked` defect an earlier round fixed twice. Two lenses.
- **MISTAKE nearly filed: the tree-removal window on the acknowledged branch.** The round-4 bullet attaches "a reclaim whose tree removal is still running when the retry materializes the same tree" to the ACKNOWLEDGED branch, where the removal has completed before the gateway returns the error. It survives on the reading that a client which times out and retries `POST /start` concurrently with the still-in-flight compensation produces exactly that interleaving. Defensible; do not spend a round on it.
- **Read the §7.1 block's long line whole before editing any part of it.** Two sentences roughly 250 words apart on one physical line pull opposite ways, and the same false proposition was stated a second time in the Design copy with "first pod-side RPC" where the staged paragraph says "first such RPC", so a grep anchored on one wording misses the other. Rounds 1 and 2 both fixed this paragraph by deleting one restatement and both left the neighbouring one standing. The paragraph's disease is structural: it derives consequences from rules other sections own, so sweep the WHOLE paragraph rather than removing them one finding at a time.
- **§7.1's obligation has TWO bounds and every delegation names only the lower one.** Any future edit that hands a new path to §7.1 must state what happens on that path once the slot is `running`.
- **`context.WithoutCancel(ctx)` in CODE-4's compensation is load-bearing.** It is what makes the reclaim survive a cancelled resume context; a later edit that "simplifies" it back to the inbound ctx silently deletes the mid-resume cancel case.
- **Do NOT close the §7.2 gap by pointing at §7.3.** The §7.3 appended sentence binds "a step in this flow that fails", and a client, parent or admin cancel is an external terminal trigger rather than a failing step, which is exactly why the proposal edits the §6.2 cancel bullet separately.
- **The imprecisions that were verified and declined, so nobody re-verifies them.** "The row keeps its §4.6 pod binding" (§4.6 is "Pod Lifecycle Controllers" and owns the `SandboxClaim` binding, while the session row's create-time pod is persisted at §7.1 step 5, so the attribution is loose rather than false); the Design's "closes by stating that the client never receives a `session_id`" (that sentence sits mid-paragraph at spec/07:23 with more sentences after it); SPEC-2's §6.2 note "Neither bullet enumerates that sequence" (its load-bearing half, that neither names the step-4 bump, is true). Do not spend a verifier pair on any of them.
- **Round 3 deleted the only text that stated why a §7.3 re-attach cannot land on the reclaiming pod,** and the Design paragraph's "placed by neither mechanism" lost one of its two antecedents with it. The mechanism still holds in code, so this is a record gap rather than a coverage gap. A round asking "what keeps a re-attach off the reclaiming pod" should read the Settled entry on `Claimer.Claim` rather than reopen the question.
- **MISTAKE, a FOURTH occurrence of the unscoped `leaked` terminal, and the most expensive.** A fix round appended a sentence to §5.2's `**Scrub model.**` paragraph asserting the `leaked` disposition, the held occupancy and the whole-pod replacement trigger with no concurrency qualifier, inside the paragraph the proposal itself declares to hold "on a pod of either concurrency", against staged §7.1's explicit "stated for concurrent occupancy and do not apply there". Five lenses filed it in one round. The structural cause is that the scrub-model paragraph is deliberately concurrency-independent, so ANY sentence appended there that names `leaked`, the trigger or the gauge needs its own scope. It was fixed by splitting the sentence by pod concurrency; check every future append against this.
- **Do NOT collapse that finding into the standing "the `slot_cleanup ──→ leaked` scoping asymmetry is pre-existing" trap.** That trap is about shipped §5.2 and §6.2 prose being loose against the fence. The round-8 defect was a contradiction between two sentences THIS proposal stages, created by a fix and against the proposal's own recorded DECISION. A verifier that merges the two refutes a live finding with an unrelated refutation.
- **Do NOT add a bind-path or a start-state qualifier to §5.2's leak-accounting sentence.** The bind-path qualifier is precisely the carve-out by code path that §5.2's trigger forbids, and it would falsify three untouched shipped sentences (spec/05:545, spec/06:160, docs/reference/state-machines.md:251). The path question is answered in the code lane by the third `accountSlotFailure` caller; the start-state question is answered by CODE-1's `live` gate. CITATION RE-POINTED (the `f4` firing deleted the conformance-gap bullet this trap used to cite): the same no-carve-out argument now lives in CODE-5's `accountSlotFailure` doc comment ("which the trigger states with no carve-out by code path", non-spec-changes.md:515) and in its caller list at non-spec-changes.md:537-569.
- **Do NOT surface `treeErr` on the started path unconditionally to close a clean-exit gap.** It reclassifies ordinary session-end slots as `leaked` under `Binder.ReleaseSlot`'s `err != nil || !cleanly`, which is the §6.2 accounting change the proposal explicitly declines. The form that works, and that landed, is gating on the report predicate (`live`) rather than on the teardown predicate.
- **Do NOT simplify CODE-1's three predicates into fewer.** The report gate and the response gate are both `live`; the runtime teardown stays `st.started` and must, because CODE-2's rollback fires only when `Runtime.Start` returns, so a start that hangs under a `runtimeLive` teardown gate would leave the runtime serving an abandoned session forever. Three predicates, two of which are the same one, is the correct shape.
- **MISTAKE that cost a whole open-decisions firing: a scope call resting on shipped behaviour a staged deliverable removes.** An out-of-scope row argued "no staged deliverable adds a second producer" from `BindReservedSlot` swallowing its own reservation-release error, while the same proposal's CODE-4 retires exactly that swallow. The false ground then propagated into the summary, the SPEC-4 exclusion paragraph and two edge-case bullets. When adjudicating a scope call on this proposal, check the ground against the STAGED tree, not the shipped one.
- **MISTAKE, three rounds on one sentence: the tier-7a assertion list.** Round 1 wrote three universals ("`runtimeLive` is empty", "`runtimeIdleLocked` is true", "the runtime was closed exactly once") beside a co-tenanted variant that falsifies the first two and a `FailedPrecondition` invariant that is interleaving-scoped. Round 2 shrank the list and produced a smaller false universal. Round 3 deleted both sentences that described a distribution over interleavings and fixed one interleaving instead. The list itself was the defect: a shared assertion list beside per-variant and per-interleaving axes.
- **Do NOT reintroduce a "the runtime was closed exactly once" assertion, in any form.** As a call count it is two: the reclaim closes under the `started` gate and CODE-2's rollback closes again. As a process-teardown count it is zero on the co-tenanted variant, because `SocketRuntimeProcess.Close` early-returns on `!p.connected` and again while a started co-tenant keeps the active set non-empty. Whether the reclaim's close or the rollback's close tears the process down depends on where the park sits relative to `accept`, which is a fixture decision nothing has made. The effect is pinned instead by the shipped `TestSocketRuntimeProcessCloseScopedToSlot_spec_5_2` and by the tier-1 co-tenancy case.
- **TRIED AND WITHDRAWN: fixing the tier-7a case by admitting both interleavings.** Dropping the park and running the barrier form makes CODE-2's guard exercised only on the runs that happen to lose the race, which turns the case's own subject into a lottery under a stress budget, and every assertion becomes conditional on an arm the test can only identify by reading the outcome it is asserting.
- **CODE-1's at-most-one-report-across-interleavings rule is stated ONCE,** in the CODE-1 section beside the `live` gate that carries it. The test inventory points at it. Restating it in the tier-7a paragraph is exactly what produced the round-3 contradiction.
- **Do NOT add `noteRuntimeClosed` to CODE-2's rollback.** `noteRuntimeStarted` refused, so the session was never inserted; the call is a no-op and it cannot remove a co-tenant. Adding it to make a mis-scoped test assertion pass would be a code change answering a prose defect.
- **MISTAKE, the one-caller reading a second time: "`StartSession` is the only site the compensation can race".** CODE-4 compensates `Binder.Resume` two pages later, and the proposal's own text says the adapter's `Resume` leaves the identical residue. The two statements sat in one file for several rounds and cost a missing rollback, a missing tier-7a arm and an attribution error in the summary. Any future sentence naming `StartSession` alone on this surface must be checked against `resume.go:50,:140,:144` and `sdkwarm.go:217,:261`.
- **Do NOT put the credential-lease release back inside `compensateFailedSlotBind`,** and do not gate it there with a stage check, a `req.SlotID != ""` check or a nil-credentials check. Every such conditional makes the function's contract depend on which caller it has, which is the coupling that produced the finding. The release belongs to the wrapper that owns the stages that mint a lease.
- **Do NOT make the bind-path lease release conditional on `assignSlotCredentials` having SUCCEEDED.** It mints per provider in a loop and returns on the first error, so a failed credential-assignment stage can already hold minted leases. Unconditional in the wrapper is the fail-closed choice and `ReleaseSession` is a documented no-op for a session holding none.
- **The §7.2 step-2 anchor is a mid-line fragment.** The staged sentence lands BEFORE the `final_workspace_ref` sentence rather than at the end of step 2, because both sit on one physical line. It is deterministic and appliable, so it is not a finding, but an implementor expecting an append-to-end will be surprised, and a reviewer must not "fix" it by re-anchoring.
- **Dead end, and here is its resolution: `docs/reference/adapter-contract.md:75` is the site and `:81` is not.** The `Shutdown` row at :75 is the reader-facing mirror of the §4.7 row SPEC-1 rewrites and is falsified in four ways, only one of which depends on the contested "is a pre-start reclaim a session release" framing; frame any finding on the drain-gate and usage-flush halves. The four sibling sites (`adapter-contract.md:81`, `security-principles.md:33`, `execution-modes.md:68`, `multi-tenancy.md:72`) all say "at each session release", which SPEC-3 widens rather than falsifies. Earlier passes let the DEFERRED and the dead-end trap pull opposite ways on :81; they do not.
- **Dead end: "`SlotID == SessionID` falsifies §5.2's `new slot` and the Fresh workspace guarantee".** Three lenses built and dropped it. "New slot" reads as a new reservation, the placement rule keeps the hazard off every retry §5.2 places, and it is already false in the shipped tree, where no `Shutdown` is sent on a failed bind at all so the tree always survives. The staged change strictly improves it.
- **Do NOT file `spec/12:913` or `spec/15:1795` against SPEC-1's co-tenancy gate.** They are the same class as the §11.4 revoke trap: each is already false for a co-tenanted pod against the shipped `boundRemains` gate, and each states a delivery intent rather than a delivery guarantee. A round that widens the gate's scope re-checks all three together.
- **Do NOT file §5.2's `**Recycling and integration levels.**` sentence** ("recycling runs with no CH-RUNTIMEOPS exchange between sessions") against SPEC-1. `drainViaLifecycle` already fires under `!boundRemains` in the shipped adapter, so the sentence is loose today and SPEC-1 restates rather than widens.
- **Do NOT file the unbound co-tenant against the drain gate any more.** A registered-but-unbound co-tenant has an empty `sessionID` and so does not hold the signal off, but after CODE-1 the signal needs `started` AND no bound entry, and the residual case is an ordinary started session end, which is shipped and pre-existing.
