A fifth lands on the same refutation.
- **Do NOT file the `concurrent_slots_exhausted` gloss against the placement exclusion, again.** spec/05:549 defines the reason as "pods exist but all slots are full" while the exclusion makes a not-full pod produce it, but `ClaimSlot` already returns `ErrNoConcurrentSlot` for every non-tenant-mismatch candidate it skips, and `ErrTenantMismatch` maps to the same reason. Six lenses across the window have now built and dropped this.
- **MISTAKE nearly filed, the security family, five dresses in one round and four in another and five again in a third.** `superseded`/`absent` as pod self-reports exempting a slot from the leak accounting; a compromised adapter refusing every fenced teardown; the epoch as a guessable small integer; the exclusive-pod carve-out returning a recycling pod carrying credential residue; a late `StartSession` re-creating the entry and leaving an orphan holding live credentials; the withheld report relaxing the `maxSessionsPerPod` residual bound; the epoch as a fail-open default because zero is permissive. Each dies on the standing bar that the lie must make the gateway DO something it does not do today, on the pre-existing `leaked = err != nil || !cleanly` self-report, or on the cleanup having removed `CredentialsDir` and cancelled the §4.9 timers first.
- **MISTAKE nearly filed, four capacity dresses in one round and four reliability dresses in another.** The hold refusal counted against the pod's unhealthy threshold; the §10.1 fence making the compensation unsendable after a handoff; the pre-`running` leak losing its occupancy on a Redis reset; the hold's accounting sentence appended to the concurrency-independent scrub-model paragraph; the hold refusing a §5.2-placed retry during the adapter's own cleanup; §5.2's Fresh workspace guarantee falsified by the hold; a replica lost mid-bind refusing an identifier for the pod's life. Each is either recorded as an accepted failure mode in the proposal's own edge cases, or is the pre-existing baseline, or is a barred close variant.
- **Do NOT conclude "nothing changed" from an empty diff against the snapshot the brief names.** In every round of this window `spec-rN` and `spec-rN-start` were byte-identical to the live proposal, because the snapshot is taken at the round's own start. Run `diff -rq` over the WHOLE `scratchpad/cp-snap/0081` directory first and diff against the newest snapshot that actually differs. Worse, that snapshot is sometimes two rounds back: at round 8 the newest differing snapshot was `spec-r6`, and the six real hunks were written in round 7's FIX stage after round 7's own lenses had run, so a round-7 shard saying "there is none" was true when written and misleading afterwards.

- **MISTAKE that nine lenses over seven sweeps repeated: checking proto field-number FREENESS and stopping there.** Every one of them verified that SCHEMA-1's nine numbers are unused, and none of them looked for a test that pins the field SET. `TestShutdownMessagePostRemovalDescriptor_spec_4_1` turns red on exactly those additive fields, and `buf breaking` is silent on an additive edit, which is the gate a reviewer reaches for and the wrong one. When a proposal adds a proto field, grep `tests/` for `Fields().Len()` and `assertFieldSet` over the messages it opens, not only the proto for the number.
- **MISTAKE, reproduced by at least eight lenses in one window: the `awk` range over a one-line proto message.** `awk '/^message X \{/,/^\}/'` and `sed -n "N,/^}/p"` both run straight past `message AssignCredentialsResponse {}` into the NEXT message and print `RotateCredentialsRequest`'s field set, which is how the retired "field 1 collides with `session_id = 1`" trap was manufactured. `grep -n "^message AssignCredentialsResponse "` with a trailing space also MISSES the line. Use `grep -n "^message X\b"` then `sed -n`, or a brace-balanced parse. Renumbering SCHEMA-1 on the strength of the old form lands a defect.
- **Do NOT hand-edit `tests/claim-map.json`.** It is generator output under a tier-0 byte-identity gate; a hand-added row turns tier 0 red on landing and the next seeding run drops it silently. The authoring site is the `EXPLICIT` list in `scripts/seed-claim-register.py`, and the generator must be re-run with `--out tests/claim-map.json` in the SAME commit. Every row in `EXPLICIT` carries a `note`; the validator does not require one, so that is convention rather than a gate.
- **MISTAKE in a finding's own `suggested_fix`: `errors.Is` against an adapter-local sentinel, from the gateway.** `errSlotReclaimInProgress` and `isSlotReclaimInProgress` are unexported and in `pkg/adapter`, which NO production file under `pkg/gateway` imports (only component tests do), and sentinel identity does not survive gRPC. A fixer taking that suggestion literally writes code that cannot compile, or worse a message-string match. The status code is the only signal that crosses the boundary.
- **Do NOT open an `Aborted` case in `SlotBindError.Reason()`** to close the resume classification gap. CODE-2's rollback relies on the same transient default, so an `Aborted` case there would make both the rollback and the hold refusal non-transient on the concurrent-bind path. The arm belongs in `isTransientPodClaimError`, which is a different classifier on a different path.
- **Do NOT put a test for `holdOrFailOnResumeError` or `isTransientPodClaimError` in `start_test.go`.** That file is `package sessionserver_test` and cannot see either unexported function. Two findings named it. The home is `resume_setup_demotion_internal_test.go`.
- **Do NOT delete the `ReportSessionScrub` mention from the `adapter-contract.md` `Shutdown` row.** `TestAdapterContractNamesTheShutdownRPCUnderItsWireName` requires the row to CONTAIN "end-of-session teardown", "recycle disposition", "ReportSessionScrub" and "ReportPodScrub". DOCS-2 must QUALIFY the mention rather than remove it, and must keep the row on ONE physical line, because the gate reads it with `lineContaining(page, "| `Shutdown` |")`. The same four substrings are why the stale row ships green today: no gate catches its absence or a wrong fix.
- **DOCS-2's prose may not cite spec section numbers.** `.claude/rules/doc-content.md` bars `§4.7.1`/`§15.4` in reader-facing docs. The page already violates this once at :393; that is not licence.
- **Do NOT file `adapter-contract.md:64` (the `DemoteSDK` row) as its own finding.** §4.7.1's caller rule makes the demotion the act that drops the caller's epoch, which leaves a one-line RPC summary INCOMPLETE rather than false, and the row is a verbatim mirror of spec/04:674, which this proposal does not edit. Three lenses declined it on that ground. The :75 fix should still pick it up.
- **The checklist renumber left stale prose step references, and they are not findings.** `non-spec-changes.md:1668` still says "Between S4 and S6 the unedited file still passes" where SPEC-4 is now S5. Substantively harmless; a fixer already in that paragraph should correct it. Do not read a step number in non-spec prose as evidence of the current sequence.
- **MISTAKE nearly filed: S9's `Depends on: S1, S8` does not close over S4,** although CODE-6 implements SPEC-3's reclaim-hold paragraph, which lands at S4. Held back because S4 precedes S9 in the one stated execution sequence and §15.4's own hold block (SPEC-5, S1, which IS named) carries the wire-observable requirement CODE-6 implements. A round that wants this must argue from a pipeline that dispatches by `Depends on` rather than by listed order.
- **Do NOT re-file the wide "create or resolve a registry entry" predicate, in its security dress either.** The dresses tried this window: `RevokeCredentials` refused mid-cleanup with `ABORTED` so a mandatory §4.9 revocation is refused; `Attach`/`Interrupt`/`ReportUsage` answering `FailedPrecondition` during a hold where §15.4 calls a permanent status non-conformant; the §11.4 revoke `Shutdown` answered `absent`. All die on the same two grounds: during the hold no entry exists so the resolve half is vacuous, and `removeSlotTree` has already taken `CredentialsDir` while `deregisterSlotLocked` has already cancelled every armed timer, so a revoke has nothing left to revoke. Reopening it means attacking the "create or resolve is this change's own bind-sequence vocabulary" scoping directly.
- **MISTAKE, and the bar is DEAD: "do NOT file CODE-1's early return skipping clause three".** That trap rested on the retired per-entry-epoch design, where only a fenced compensation carried a non-zero epoch and never set the recycle disposition. Under the token, `shutdownReclaimOutcome`'s switch tests entry ABSENCE before the unconditional flag, so `unconditional_teardown` against an absent entry takes the non-removing early return — and that is exactly what the concurrent `ShutdownRecycle` always is, because `Binder.ReleaseSlot` already tore the entry down. The staged handler therefore dropped `startPodScrub` on a production path, the finding was CONFIRMED, and the fix is the single `answerShutdown` exit. Do not reuse the old bar, and do not trust the self-refutation that went with it ("the unconditional form does NOT early-return on an absent entry"), which is false against the staged switch order.
- **Do NOT file `compensateFailedSlotBind`'s `default: return false` as a fail-open.** `SLOT_RECLAIM_OUTCOME_UNSPECIFIED` plus `exited_cleanly: false` maps to not-leaked, where today's `err != nil || !cleanly` maps it to leaked, so it reads as inverting `slotbinder.go:533-543`'s stated fail-closed posture. It dies twice: CODE-1's handler sets an outcome on every return path so UNSPECIFIED is unreachable for the first-party adapter, and staged §7.1 enumerates exactly two incomplete-reclaim conditions so the default arm is spec-conformant. The only reacher is a hypothetical third-party adapter, a framing the material skeptic has refuted three times. A later round needs a first-party reachability argument.
- **The caller-latch clear diverges between the lanes, it is INERT, and it is now FILED.** §4.7.1 says a caller holds no epoch once it has issued any RPC that removes the entry, naming `DemoteSDK` and "a `Shutdown` answering `reclaimed`", while CODE-6 clears only on `DemoteSDK` and on `ShutdownReclaim` answering `RECLAIMED`; the plain `Client.Shutdown` and `ShutdownRecycle` keep their signatures, read no outcome and clear nothing. Five lenses reached it and declined; `non-spec.6.review-fresh.1` filed it as a staged-code-contradicts-staged-spec finding, which is the loop's own scope rule, so the inertness below is the reason it is cheap to fix rather than a reason to close it. The direction is fail-open, and it is unreachable because no gateway connection carries a bind sequence after an ordinary `Shutdown`: `BindResult.Adapter` is per attempt and is closed at session end (`defer result.Adapter.Close()`, slotbinder.go:544). Reopening it needs a path where a plain `Shutdown` is followed by a bind-sequence RPC or a compensation on the SAME connection.
- **MISTAKE nearly filed, the security family again, five more dresses in one window.** A lagging `AssignCredentials` re-creating the entry and writing `credentials.json` plus §4.9 timers for an abandoned session (same route as the recorded `StartSession` variant, killed by CODE-4's unconditional `releaseCredentials` and by the occupancy-zero scrub sweeping `/run/lenny/slots/`); the epoch fence stranding credential residue (every adapter deregistration path reaches a `removeSlotTree`, so `superseded` and `absent` both entail the predecessor's tree, credential directory and timers are already gone); `emitFinalUsage` narrowing from `bound` to `started` leaking a delegation-tree budget (best-effort with a stream-close fallback §8.3 tolerates, and no bound-but-unstarted session reaches a `Shutdown` in the shipped tree); the pod-self-reported `leaked` bound as a regression (the adapter's power to force "not leaked" is IDENTICAL on both sides of the change — before, `exited_cleanly`; after, `slot_reclaim` plus `exited_cleanly`); and the §4.7 no-op sentence as a mandatory-credential-purge bypass (killed by the row's unedited tail).
- **Do NOT file the resume-path latency family; it is DISCLOSED rather than open.** On an exclusive pool `Binder.Resume`'s compensation runs with `slotCleanupBudget` degenerating to the whole pool `cleanupTimeoutSeconds` (default 60, documented "Must be `> 0`" with no upper bound) inside the client's request. The proposal names and prices it, and the standing five-dresses trap already declined the family because §16.5 samples successful creates only. A future round must argue from a STATED budget it breaches, never from the wall clock.
- **The snapshot trap has a fourth and a fifth form.** Fourth: this window's `spec-rN` directories REUSE the names of the previous window's, so `spec-r3` … `spec-r9` under `scratchpad/cp-snap/0081.../` belong to a different document generation and ordering the listing by NAME is misleading — and mtime order is misleading too, because `spec-r4` sorts newer than `spec-r9`. Fifth: an empty diff can mean "the previous round produced no fix" rather than "you diffed the wrong snapshot", which is only distinguishable by `diff -rq` across the whole chain. In several rounds the newest differing snapshot was a `-prefix` directory, because the fix stage runs between the prefix snapshot and the next round's. Use `git diff` on the proposal file when the snapshots are ambiguous.
- **Do NOT read `spec/06:160`'s attribution of `lenny_adapter_leaked_slots` to the adapter as a premise.** The gauge is GATEWAY-registered and gateway-emitted (gatewaymetrics_credential.go:219-223) despite the name and despite the spec sentence. Five more lenses built a finding on "the adapter cannot know what the gateway observed" this window; the premise does not exist.
- **FILED and live: CODE-4's credential release is keyed on the SESSION, so it can destroy a concurrent successor attempt's leases, and two standing entries look like they refute it and do not.** The `materializeSlot` wrapper runs `compensateFailedSlotBind` first, blocking up to `slotCleanupBudget` on a `context.WithoutCancel`, and only then calls `b.releaseCredentials(req.SessionID)`, which iterates `LeasesBySession([sessionID])` and releases every lease under that key (credassign.go:400-410), while `assignSlotCredentials` mints per attempt under the same key (slotbinder.go:379). The design's own headline scenario, a client whose context expired retrying into the same slot, puts attempt 2 past `assignSlotCredentials` inside that window: the epoch fences the pod-side reclaim to `superseded` and the very next statement destroys the successor's leases anyway. Look-alike (1): the Trap "Do NOT make the bind-path lease release conditional on `assignSlotCredentials` having SUCCEEDED" bars a STAGE condition; this is the release's KEY, and releasing exactly the lease ids this attempt minted stays unconditional over a partial assign. Look-alike (2): the refuted "CODE-4's unconditional `releaseCredentials` releases leases a snapshotless resume-rebuild may still hold" died on "the rebuild re-mints on every attempt"; a concurrent second `/start` does not re-mint after the release. The proposal states the correct rule for the resume path and does not carry it to the bind path (non-spec-changes.md:701-704).
- **Do NOT cite "§15.4 names no status code for the hold refusal" as evidence of a gap.** Three earlier findings were refuted on that premise and a later fix CLOSED it: §15.4 now publishes `ABORTED` and the two non-conformance cases. Reading the refuted list as a map of what the text still lacks misleads in this direction; re-read the text. The same staleness runs the other way for anyone quoting the refutations of "the missing wire spelling of an epochless `Shutdown`".
- **The snapshot trap has a SIXTH form: `diff -rq` can report the spec staging as unchanged when it changed.** In one round `diff -ru <snapshot> <proposal dir>` listed only the checklist and the non-spec file, while a direct `diff -u` of the two `.spec-changes.md` files showed a real delta. Diff the single file explicitly rather than trusting the recursive listing, and remember that mtime order across `scratchpad/cp-snap/0081.../` is not round order (`spec-r4` sorts newer than `spec-r9`) and that this window reuses the previous window's directory names.
- **Do NOT move the §7.2 reclaim after step 4.** It sits at step 3, before the `coordination_generation` bump, and that ordering is the only reason a mid-resume compensation is not fenced by the bump it precedes. A later edit that reorders them makes every mid-resume reclaim fail the generation check and leak the slot.
- **The SCHEMA-1 window under `**Decisions.**` and the R1b row under `## Impacts on other proposals` both assert the S-2 second-window precondition.** They agree today and are deliberately not folded into each other: the decisions bullet carries it as this proposal's step-ordering constraint, the impacts row as an assertion about the programme step. An edit to one owes the same edit to the other.

All three die on the same two grounds already recorded for the wide predicate, and the natural remedy is a §15.4 wording tightening, which is spec-lane. Anyone reopening must attack the "create or resolve is this change's own bind-sequence vocabulary" scoping directly. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/slotcreds.go:102-146; slotsession.go:274-283.
- **Do NOT read the refuted-list entries about the shared-entry epoch inheritance as current.** Six of seven shapes one lens re-derived from the amendment text were refuted as STALE against a pre-`2aca9851b` snapshot rather than on their merits. Diff against the newest differing snapshot and grep the load-bearing quote before deriving anything from remembered text.
- **The standing context is where the answers are, and reading it end to end is the cheapest first move.** Six of seven candidates a fresh reader generates on this document are already in it, several as explicit "do not re-derive" entries, and four consecutive lens shards this window recorded that reading it first turned a full re-derivation into a spot check. The refuted-family index in Traps is the highest-value part; keep it at length and do not split the consolidated security-family and capacity-family entries back into individual lines.

- **MISTAKE, filed twice and still standing: "refuses" is overloaded after the amendment, and the Testing section was never swept.** Post-amendment a refusal is the `SUPERSEDED`/`ABSENT` answer, which CODE-4 maps to `leaked=false` and CODE-5 excludes from `ExcludePods`; pre-amendment "the adapter refuses" could only mean an errored RPC. The tier-4 datastore-crossing case carried the pre-amendment sense verbatim from commit 2811d55fe. Whoever edits that section must sweep every "refuse"/"refused" for the place "did not answer" is meant, and must NOT sweep the other twenty, which are the reclaim-hold and epoch refusals and are correct.
- **Do NOT name `releaseSessionSlot` as a `Runtime.Close` park site.** Two fix rounds wrote tier-1 hold bullets telling an implementor to park or panic `Runtime.Close` at `releaseSessionSlot`; its body reaches no such call. Only the §10.1.4 disjunct is writable, and `removeSlotTree` is `slotlayout.RemoveTree(st.paths)` with no injectable seam, so that row has no parkable destructive step either.
- **The snapshot trap, seventh and eighth forms.** Seventh: in several rounds the named snapshot was byte-identical to the working tree INCLUDING the review log, so `diff -rq` returned nothing at all and the real delta was the UNCOMMITTED working-tree change — use `git diff HEAD -- proposals/0081_*/` then. Eighth: the newest DIFFERING snapshot is often a `-prefix` directory, because the fix stage runs between the prefix snapshot and the next round's, and in one round the brief's named snapshot was two rounds stale. Run `diff -rq` across the whole `scratchpad/cp-snap/0081...` chain before budgeting a round on "read the delta first"; at least fifteen shards in this window opened by discovering the delta did not exist.
- **MISTAKE the credit-inventory gate punishes, and nobody named the gate for six rounds: creating a `slot*_test.go` file.** Two of this proposal's new files match it by basename and neither was registered until the r5 fix. The trap has a second edge that is CONTINGENT and easy to miss: rule 1 is a body match over the whole tree, so an EXISTING unregistered file becomes mandatory the moment an edit adds `slotstate.`, `ClaimSlot(`, `ReleaseSlot(`, `ReserveSlotOnPod(`, `claimAtCreate(` or `BindReservedSlot(` to it. `pkg/gateway/sessionserver/start_test.go` and `resume_setup_demotion_internal_test.go` are both outside the inventory at zero matches today and are both plausible homes for a `BindReservedSlot(` literal. Do not describe the name rule as a `slot*_test.go` prefix glob; the regexp matches `slot` anywhere in the basename.
- **The DOCS-2 audience-mismatch clause is FILED, and the older "do not file it" entry is superseded.** DOCS-2's rationale says the block reaches "the third-party adapter author the page is written for" (non-spec-changes.md:1320-1321, repeated at summary.md:694) and paraphrases the page's line 10 as "the complete reference for the gateway-to-adapter gRPC protocol" (:1294-1295). `docs/reference/adapter-contract.md` says it is the complete reference for the protocol between the adapter sidecar and "your runtime binary" (:10), calls the adapter container Lenny-managed (:18), and says of the gRPC section "Your runtime binary never sees them directly" (:53); it never names a third-party adapter author, and the proposal's own spec-changes.md:705 puts that audience on §15.4. Six lenses across three rounds derived it and declined it as rationale prose that never reaches `docs/`; `non-spec-recheck-3.3.review-citations.1` FILED it on the seventh derivation and its own WATCHOUT says this entry must not be read as refuting the filing, because the filing is about the false GROUND rather than about whether the block should land. The fix is one clause: "so the gateway-to-adapter contract this page covers states the epoch and the hold". WHAT SURVIVES as a bar: do NOT file the OPPOSITE finding, that the full §15.4 bind-epoch and reclaim-hold contract must be republished on that page. That is over-reach for a runtime-author-facing page, and the defensible scope is the `Shutdown` row's now-false statements.
- **Do NOT restate the Non-goals bullet's "adds accounting where there is none" half.** The bullet's operative claim, that no threshold changes, is exactly true, and the falsifier let the bullet stand for that reason. Its justification half is not the whole of CODE-5: on `applySlotRetryPolicy` the accounting already exists and its shipped discriminator is `relErr != nil` alone (start.go:2834-2854), and CODE-5 widens it to `sbe.Leaked || relErr != nil`, so an unacknowledged reclaim on that path moves from the windowed arm to the persistent-leak arm. That disposition change is stated correctly elsewhere in the proposal. A later pass rewriting the bullet should keep the threshold claim and drop the "where there is none" half rather than copying it forward.
- **MISTAKE, twice in one phase: a firing published a provenance statement about a file state that does not exist.** `f6` recorded "the 0080 row itself was not touched" and the summary recorded that the row "was applied at firing 5 of this phase and reversed afterwards ... so the row stands as it was written at firing 4". Both are false: the baseline `2f473c14e` carries firing 4's five-file version of the row while HEAD carries the eleven-file rewrite, so the rewrite was applied and never reversed, and open decision 19 rested on the false premise. The summary half was deleted with entry 19; the log half stands as a wrong line rather than being edited, per the no-editing-a-ledger-entry rule. Before asserting that a firing's edit was reversed, diff the named baseline against HEAD for that one block; a phase's own bookkeeping about itself is the least-checked prose in the proposal.
- **MISTAKE that cost a whole loop: a fix stage declared a lane converged without sweeping `### Open` for entries marked FILED.** CODE-4's session-keyed credential release was recorded FILED-and-live with a named remedy, the fix round after it did not apply it, and the next round's reliability and security lenses both re-derived and re-filed it. Deferred and FILED entries against files the current lane MAY edit are the cheapest findings in the pile; check them before anything else.
- **Do NOT re-derive the capacity family; it has now been declined by six performance passes over text that did not move.** The replica-local `slothealth` ledger against the durable Redis occupancy, the exclusive-pool resume budget degenerating to the whole `cleanupTimeoutSeconds`, the compensation pinning a client request goroutine, the blob-outage mass drain, and the correlated re-attach storm are all true, all recorded as Open items or accepted failure modes, and all declined on the shipped-baseline or open-decision ground. Filing in this lens needs a mechanism that is NEW in the staged text. One durability asymmetry is worth having stated once: `ReleaseSlot(leaked=true)` skips the counter decrement whose doc comment names the §12.4 Postgres fallback, so the withheld occupancy is DURABLE while the `slothealth` count meant to bound it is per-replica and in-memory — pre-existing sharding, but this proposal changes the RATE, because a `leaked` disposition becomes the routine answer to any unanswered compensating `Shutdown` rather than needing `ReleaseSlotReservation` to error.
- **MISTAKE, the `awk` proto-range artifact, now reproduced by more than FORTY lenses across three windows and STILL the single most repeated waste on this proposal.** Nearly every lens that checks SCHEMA-1's field numbers builds `AssignCredentialsResponse` a false field set by running an `awk '/^message X \{/,/^\}/'` range over the one-line `message AssignCredentialsResponse {}` at schemas/lenny-adapter.proto:1033, which never matches `^}` and falls through into `RotateCredentialsRequest` (:1035-1059, fields 1 session_id, 2 leases, 3 rotation_trigger, reserved 4, 5 coordination_generation). Every one of them then nearly files "field 1 collides with `session_id`", and one earlier window actually landed that as a Trap before it was retired. CORRECTED for the token design: the nine RESPONSE numbers this entry used to list belonged to the withdrawn epoch and are gone. The live field set is the token one in Settled (`PrepareWorkspaceRequest` 5 and 6, `FinalizeWorkspaceRequest` 6, `RunSetupRequest` 5, `AssignCredentialsRequest` 4, `ResumeRequest` 16, `ShutdownRequest` 7 and 8, `ShutdownResponse` 3), and it is settled; do not check it again. If you must, use `grep -n "^message X"` plus `sed -n '/^message X {$/,/^}$/p'`, or a brace-depth walker.
- **MISTAKE, and it corrects a claim earlier passes carried: "a new test FUNCTION in a listed file costs nothing".** That is true of `validate-maps` and false of the second tier-0 gate. Once a file is in `slotAddressCaseFiles`, `TestSlotAddressCasesAreCreditedToEverySectionTheyAnnotate` demands a `tests/spec-map.json` credit for every section the file's annotations name, per case when the map registers that file case by case. Three files this proposal adds cases to are registered case by case with narrow whole-file credit (`slotretry_test.go`, `slotclaimer_test.go`, `recycle_scrub_path_test.go`). Check the target file's own registration granularity before concluding a case is free.
- **Do NOT land a `§7.1`-annotated case in `pkg/adapter/slotsession_test.go`.** It IS in `slotAddressCaseFiles` and carries no 7.1 credit (whole-file 4.9, 5.2, 6.1, 6.4, 15.4 plus 4.7 by subtree), so such a case turns tier 0 red at S9 and forces further spec-map staging for no benefit. This is why the CODE-6 tier-1 cases all land in the one new file.
- **Do not re-open the credit question; it is settled.** One early shard wrote that a case-by-case-registered file must be fixed with per-case rows and that a whole-file entry is wrong. At least six later shards read `creditsMissing` (spec_map_slot_address_registration_test.go:950-963) and established that it consults `wholeFile` in BOTH branches, so a whole-file credit satisfies a case in a per-case-mode file. That is why `slotclaimer_test.go`'s §5.2-only tier-2 cases owe nothing and why S12 stages whole-file credits for `concurrent_workspace_test.go` rather than per-case rows. A round that "corrects" the staging on the old reading adds rows that flip a file's mode for no gain.
- **MISTAKE, the third reclaim-hold producer, and BOTH orderings survive it.** The two grounds CODE-5 gives for excluding the §5.2 retry are about a hold the RECLAIM opened; after CODE-6 the abandoned attempt's own `releaseSessionSlot` rollback opens one too and nothing reports it. Ordering (a) rollback-then-retry: the retry is refused by the hold and spends its last attempt. Ordering (b) retry-then-rollback: the retry created its entry and staged its tree first, so the lagging rollback deregisters the RETRY's entry and deletes the RETRY's tree. The hold closes neither. CODE-5's "When it does not fire" sentence describes (b) and stays TRUE; a fixer who replaces it wholesale withdraws a live hazard.
- **The standing refutation "`releaseSessionSlot` is fully synchronous, so the hold ends before the requesting RPC answers" is scoped to callers still WAITING.** On the abandoned-attempt path the gateway stopped waiting, which is the premise of the whole compensation design (`context.WithoutCancel`), so the hold outlives the request that opened it. Anyone re-checking the third-producer finding must not stop at that entry, and the same caveat applies to the two `DemoteSDK` dead-end entries, which are about a caller that does wait.
- **Do NOT refute the queue head-of-line amplification with the `ExcludePods` "yields to the TAIL" refutation.** They are different mechanisms: the refuted dress was about an excluded waiter re-queueing, while the filed one is that the FIFO admits only the head's `attempt(ctx)`, so a synchronous compensation inside `materializeSlot` blocks every queued request for that pool. At the spec's own defaults the arithmetic lands exactly on `maxQueueWaitSeconds` (60/4 = 15s budget, two attempts, 30s).
- **MISTAKE, a fourth occurrence: the fix stage did not sweep `### Traps` and `### Open` for entries marked FILED.** The CODE-5 `slotBinder`-interface sentence took four filings across three windows before a fix stage took it, and the standing entry naming that exact cost was already in the log when the fourth filing happened. Read the FILED entries first; they are the cheapest findings in the pile and they are the ones that recur.
- **Do NOT file `docs/api/internal.md`.** It is a hand-authored gRPC page already fictional against the shipped proto: it declares `rpc StopSession`, `rpc UploadFiles` and a `StartSessionResponse{success, error_code, error_message}` the proto does not have, and its status table lists no `ABORTED` while the shipped adapter already returns it. SCHEMA-1 neither creates nor worsens that. Five lenses reached it; it wants its own proposal.
- **Do NOT file the `lenny_slot_pod_replacement_total` label mismatch.** spec/16:128 labels it `pool` AND `k8s_pod_name` while the gateway hook is `slotReplacement func(pool string)`; CODE-5 keeps that signature and only adds callers, so the mismatch is pre-existing and neither created nor widened here.
- **Do NOT file the `concurrent_slots_exhausted` reuse, again, in its operational dress either.** `ClaimSlot`'s shipped `expiredByUptime` skip already returns `ErrNoConcurrentSlot` for a pod with free capacity, so spec/05:549's "all slots are full" gloss is already falsified in kind. Seven lenses across two windows have now built and dropped it; a filing needs a harm the uptime-skip precedent does not already carry.
- **Do not schedule a lens over text that has not moved.** The operational lens fired five times on this proposal for one filing, which was refuted; the performance lens fired roughly a dozen times with at least six over unmoved text. Run `diff -rq` across the whole snapshot chain first, and when only the review log differs, read `### Open` and the prior shards of that lens and stop.
- **Reading this file needs the Read tool, not `bash`.** Bash output over roughly 2KB is persisted to a tool-results file and only a preview is shown, and the Read tool refuses more than 25k tokens per call, so `## Standing context` comes back in passes of 100 to 180 lines. Budget for that before planning a round.
- **WATCHOUT: SPEC-4's §6.2 edge must keep exactly one space either side of `──→`.** The tier-11 check is a raw `strings.Contains` on `"receiving_uploads ──→ slot_cleanup"`, and the shipped fence uses `──→` (U+2500 U+2500 U+2192) with the trigger paren at column 41 and a 41-space continuation indent. Re-flowing the block breaks nothing mechanical except that string.
- **Do NOT copy a neighbouring field's generation comment into a SCHEMA-1 field.** `adapter_proto_generation_scope_test.go` counts exact occurrences of the operational-fence sentence and pins the count at twelve, so one copied comment turns tier 0 red.
- **WATCHOUT: `Server.reclaiming` needs a lazy-init guard the way `s.slots` has one.** Adapter tests build `Server` by struct literal and a write to a nil map panics. CODE-6 states the lazy seed for the epoch counter for exactly that reason and says nothing about the map; whoever writes `reclaimSlotLocked` must not forget it.
- **Do not let the `_ = removeSlotTree(st)` discard's replacement grow.** CODE-6 turns it into a `slog.Warn` (and `pkg/adapter/slotsession.go` gains a `log/slog` import it does not have today). That log line carries no contract and pins no test deliberately: the edge-case bullet is written as an accepted failure mode with an observable trace rather than a half-built accounting path. Promoting it to a metric or a counter re-opens the design the r5 fix closed.
- **The credit-inventory trap has a contingent second edge nobody has costed.** If an implementor lands the tier-1 "reserved bind path" case in `pkg/gateway/sessionserver/start_test.go`, its `BindReservedSlot(` literal matches `slotSurfaceCallRE` and pulls that whole shipped file into `slotAddressCaseFiles`; because it has no per-case rows it then enters the credit gate in WHOLE-FILE mode, demanding credits for every section every annotation in it names against whole-file credits of 4.1 and 11.4 only. That could be a large unbudgeted cascade. The proposal's general sentence covers it in principle and nobody has priced it. CONFIRMED this window: `start_test.go` is outside the inventory today at zero matches and its only spec-map credits are the `pkg/gateway/...` (4.1) and `pkg/gateway/sessionserver/...` (11.4) directory globs, while it already cites §15.1 and §4.4 — so the cascade is a credit failure on its PRE-EXISTING citations, not on the new case.

- **MISTAKE, the sentence five rounds copied: "a row carrying a `PodAssignment` goes through `BindReservedSlot` and never enters `applySlotRetryPolicy`".** The guard is a conjunction. A recovery-state row (`resume_pending`, `resuming`, `awaiting_client_action`) carrying a stale dead-pod `PodAssignment` — which is exactly the §7.3 resume-rebuild the function's own doc comment names at start.go:2585-2590 — falls through to :2606-2609 and into the retry policy. The cited span `start.go:2594-2604` is wrong on both ends and straddles the guard and the fallthrough. The sentence stood in summary.md's open decision 17 `Ground:`, was FILED by `non-spec-recheck-3.1.review-citations.1` and fixed by qualifying it with "in a non-recovery state". Two things to carry: the CONCLUSION was never wrong (a recovery row is policy-placed, so the exclusion covers it and the residue stays bounded to the create-time-reserved path), and an earlier verifier had established the same tree fact while refuting a DIFFERENT finding without noticing this sentence. When a verifier derives a tree fact that contradicts a sentence it is not looking at, say so.
- **WATCHOUT: the `release` closure `reclaimSlotLocked` returns must take `s.mu` ITSELF, and nothing staged says so.** CODE-1 writes `defer release()` while `s.mu` is still held and unlocks on the next line, and `terminateHeldSession` defers it with the lock never held, so a `*Locked`-style release deadlocks both sites and a bare `delete(s.reclaiming, id)` is a data race `-race` will find in the tier-7a hold suite. Five shards across two lanes derived this independently and none filed it, because it is ordinary implementation judgement; whoever lands S9 should state it in the helper's doc comment.
- **WATCHOUT: put the §10.1.4 per-member `defer release()` in the pass-2 LOOP, not only inside `terminateHeldSession`.** `deregisterStartedSessions` (pass 1) takes a hold on every started member under one `s.mu`, and `onHoldTimeout` (pass 2) destroys them one at a time under a single shared 10s context with no early exit. A panic inside an early member's `terminateHeldSession` leaks every remaining member's hold for the life of the pod, which is the exact failure CODE-1's own rationale gives as the reason its release is deferred. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/holdstate.go:190-205; slotsession.go:375-396.
- **The §10.1.4 N-identifier hold is not a capacity finding, and here is the refutation that kills it.** Pass 1 holds up to `maxConcurrentSessions` identifiers at once for as long as the whole 10s pass. In the SHIPPED tree that same window is strictly WORSE: pass 1 already deregisters the entry, so a rebind onto the identifier succeeds, and pass 2's `removeSlotTree(m.state)`, whose paths derive from the slot identifier, then deletes the FRESH session's tree. The hold closes that latent bug. A filer needs a harm that survives this; three lenses built the capacity dress and each dropped it.
- **WATCHOUT: do NOT "simplify" `nextBindEpochLocked`'s lazy `time.Now().UnixNano()` seed into a constructor seed.** Adapter tests build `Server` by struct literal, so a constructor-only seed leaves the field at zero, which is the wire's "unfenced" value — every fenced reclaim in the test suite would become unconditional and the suite would still pass green. CODE-6 gives that reason; a later round that reads the lazy seed as untidy will delete the property.
- **WATCHOUT: `Server.reclaiming` needs a lazy-init guard the way `s.slots` has one,** for the same struct-literal reason. CODE-6 states the lazy seed for the epoch counter and says nothing about the map; a write to a nil map panics.
- **Dead end, several dresses: the reclaim hold reaching a non-bind RPC.** `CoordinatorFence` answered `codes.FailedPrecondition` during a hold where §15.4 calls a permanent status non-conformant; `RevokeCredentials`/`Attach`/`Interrupt`/`ReportUsage` the same; `extendCredentialLeaseSlot` returning an empty success. All die on the adjudicated vacuous-resolve reading (during the hold no entry exists, so only the CREATING half of "create or resolve" is reachable) plus the Settled fact that every one of those handlers resolves through `slotStateLocked`/`boundSlotState` rather than `ensureSlotStateLocked`. Reopening means attacking the scoping reading directly.
- **Do NOT file §28:861's `status`-frame relay against the hold.** §28 says a `status` frame is relayed "on a pod whose registry entry for that session has already been released", which is exactly the hold's window, and SPEC-3's hold sentence refuses "any request that would create or resolve a registry entry". It dies because the hold is scoped to the gateway-adapter gRPC surface (§15.4 states the refusal as the gRPC status `ABORTED`), an intra-pod CH-RUNTIMEOPS frame is not a request on that surface, and §28 explicitly exempts `status` from live-binding confirmation. Anyone re-filing must argue that the word "request" reaches intra-pod frames, which §15.4 contradicts.
- **MISTAKE nearly filed, and it costs a full read of two files: "SPEC-3's whole-pod backstop overstates coverage, because `RemoveTree` removes FOUR trees and the scrub touches only two".** True that nothing in scrub steps 0-6 removes or verifies `/sessions/{sessionId}/` or `/artifacts/{sessionId}/`, which `RemoveTree` does remove and which §6.4 assigns to slot cleanup (spec/06:386). It dies because the staged sentence names exactly the two things the scrub does and claims no more, and the gap is pre-existing and equally true of any failed per-slot cleanup on the ordinary release path. A re-filer needs a harm the pre-existing framing does not already own.
- **MISTAKE nearly filed: the bounded-cohort credential exposure.** On a `maxConcurrentSessions: N, recycle.enabled: false` pool no whole-pod scrub ever runs, so a `credentials.json` left by a partially-failed in-handler cleanup stays readable by every co-tenant through the shared `lenny-cred-readers` group for the cohort's life. It dies on the staged clause's own scope: the non-recycling arm claims only that the residue "does not outlive the pod", which is true (the pod terminates after the cohort drains), and the exposure is shipped best-effort `removeSlotTree` behaviour the staging does not change.
- **MISTAKE nearly filed, twice, from opposite lanes: the `refusal_reason` graceful-refusal arms against §15.4's "reports zero on a bind-sequence response".** `ConfigureWorkspaceResponse.refusal_reason` and `StartSessionResponse.refusal_reason` exist for a refusal that resolves no entry and therefore has no epoch to report, which would make a third-party adapter non-conformant. `grep -rn "refusal_reason\|RefusalReason" pkg/adapter/*.go` returns nothing, so neither arm is reachable in the shipped adapter and both construction sites are the success arm. Same hypothetical-third-party class the loop has refuted repeatedly.
- **MISTAKE nearly filed: the staged §7.2 step-3 reclaim against the tree's mid-resume cancel path.** `handleDelete` (sessionserver.go:2922-2959) and `handleTransition` (:2985-3005) write the row state and bump the coordination generation and release no pod at all, so the obligation looks unimplementable. It dies because §7.2 step 3's "release the replacement pod" is ITSELF unimplemented today, so adding the reclaim to an unimplemented step makes the tree no less conforming. A filer must first show where the replacement pod IS released on that edge.
- **MISTAKE nearly filed: the tier-1 classifier rows omit a credential-assignment `codes.Aborted` row.** The prose says "the credential site's row is the one this pins" while only a workspace-stage and a start-stage row are staged. `Reason()` switches on the code first and refines on `e.Stage` only inside the `PermissionDenied, FailedPrecondition` arm, so `Aborted` takes the transient default at EVERY stage and a third row would assert the same branch. Additional coverage, below the bar.
- **The snapshot trap, ninth and tenth forms.** Ninth: the non-spec staging has now been byte-identical since `non-spec-r6`, so an empty diff against any of `non-spec-r7`, `-r8`, `non-spec-recheck-2-r1/-r2/-r3-start` means the file genuinely has not moved rather than that you diffed wrong; in more than twenty shards of this window the named snapshot was byte-identical to the working tree and the only differing files were `summary.md`, `status.md` and the review logs. Tenth: `diff -ru` over the whole directory dumps 30KB of unchanged status and review-log prose and costs a round, so run `diff -rq` per file FIRST, and when only the review log differs, read `### Open` and the prior shards of your own lens and stop. When the snapshots are all identical, the real delta is the uncommitted working tree: `git diff HEAD -- proposals/0081_*/`.
- **Do not schedule a lens over text that has not moved, and this window is the proof.** Every one of the twelve-plus shards in `spec-recheck-2.2`, `non-spec-recheck-2.1`, `non-spec-recheck-2.2` and `non-spec-recheck-3.1` opened by discovering there was no delta, and every one returned an empty findings list. The one filed defect of the whole window (the `bindConcurrentSlot` conjunction) came from a citation sweep of text that HAD moved. Run `diff -rq` across the snapshot chain before budgeting a round.
- **Reading this file needs the Read tool, not `bash`.** Bash output over roughly 2KB is persisted to a tool-results file and only a preview is shown, and the Read tool refuses more than 25k tokens per call, so `## Standing context` comes back in passes of 100 to 150 lines and the whole log takes roughly twenty-five calls. Budget for that before planning a round, and read `### Traps` and `### Open` first: they are where the answers are.

- **MISTAKE, the same sentence failing in BOTH directions: §4.7.1's "Every other RPC on this contract neither carries nor reports ..." closure.** Written outside the `Shutdown` block it excluded `Shutdown`, and five lenses filed it; moved INTO the `Shutdown` block its "other" resolved against `Shutdown` and excluded the five bind carriers. It is now deleted and the carriage rule lives in exactly one sentence at the enumeration site ("`StartSession` and `ConfigureWorkspace` carry none, and no other request on this contract carries one"). Do not reintroduce a free-standing "every other RPC" carriage sentence in any position.
- **Do NOT delete or "harmonize" the OTHER "Every other RPC" sentence.** §4.7.1's "Every other RPC on this contract resolves an entry the session already holds, creates none, and is outside these rules" is scoped to the ADMISSION rules, sits before `Shutdown` is introduced, and is correct for the pod-scoped RPCs. The DOCS-2 paragraph uses the same admission-scoped phrasing and is likewise correct.
- **MISTAKE, two rounds on one sentence: per-RPC reachability narration inside §4.7.1's Admission paragraph.** Round 1 wrote it as a universal (which refused every `StartSession`), round 3 narrowed it to a closed enumeration, and round 3's lens filed the enumeration. It is now DELETED rather than narrowed a third time, because every rule in the cascade is self-selecting by its own condition and a per-RPC reachability claim beside them has no job. A third enumeration, however qualified, is the same answer one step smaller.
- **MISTAKE: `ShutdownResponse.slot_was_started` was the code lane's intruder into a spec lane that never admitted it.** Its only consumer anywhere was one §16.1 counter whose stated purpose was false on both halves: §7.1 obliges the compensation exactly when the adapter may have admitted the start, so `reclaimed` over a started entry is the DESIGNED outcome, and a `reclaimed` answer already means the tokens compared equal, so the counter could not observe an identity-gate failure at all. Field and row both deleted. The operational question is already carried by `lenny_slot_failure_total{error_type=...}`.
- **Do NOT flip the `Shutdown` four-way compare so a named reclaim removes an untokened entry.** "Token versus empty = reclaimed" looks like the fix for the untokened residue. An untokened entry can be the one a SUCCESSOR's own `StartSession` created for a session that is now running, so removing it tears down a live session. `superseded` there is the fail-closed answer; only its old justification ("no compensated path produces an entry carrying no token") was false.
- **Do NOT reclassify the untokened-entry `superseded` as a reclaim that "did not complete"** to get the §5.2 whole-pod replacement trigger to collect it. The same untokened entry may belong to a session the pod is legitimately serving and the gateway cannot tell; replacing that pod destroys a live session.
- **`superseded` for a tokenless entry is CORRECT on the `Shutdown` path and WRONG on the bind path.** The bind-path identity refusal fires only when the request's non-empty token differs from the entry's non-empty token. Do not sweep the phrase "carries no token" out of the `Shutdown` sites, and do not import it into the bind-path gate.
- **Do NOT put the reclaim hold back inside the admission cascade as "rule 1".** §5.2's hold reaches "any request that would create or resolve a registry entry", which is a WIDER set than the requests §4.7.1's cascade governs; a `Checkpoint` resolves an entry and is refused by the hold while §4.7.1 says it is outside the rules entirely. State the hold by reference to §5.2 as a check that precedes the cascade and carries its own scope.
- **The hold's precedence over the no-entry rule is load-bearing, not decoration.** During a hold the entry is already deregistered, so a `mid_session` `PrepareWorkspace` resolves no entry and the no-entry rule would answer the PERMANENT `FAILED_PRECONDITION` where §5.2 promises a transient refusal the caller retries.
- **"Carries `bind_attempt`" means the message TYPE declares the field, never that the value is non-empty.** The fields are bare proto3 scalars with no presence. That reading is what lets the well-formedness condition exclude `StartSession`/`ConfigureWorkspace` with no carve-out clause. Any rewrite that loses it needs the carve-out back.
- **`ensureSlotStateLocked`'s rule switch has NO field-validity case and must not grow one.** The well-formedness gate sits above it, at each handler's entry. Anybody reading the switch alone concludes the create branch fires unconditionally on an absent entry, which is correct only because a malformed request never reaches the function.
- **WATCHOUT: `mid_session` is a bare proto3 bool on a CLIENT-STREAMING message, so "omitted" and "false" are the same bytes.** A later `PrepareWorkspace` frame carrying an empty `bind_attempt` where the first carried one is explicitly not a disagreement, but a later frame whose `mid_session` is false where the first was true IS a refusal. No spec or staged-code text states the sender's obligation to repeat `mid_session` on every frame; `sendUpload` builds every frame from scratch (adapterclient/client.go:269-284), and CODE-8 is the sender.
- **MISTAKE that cost most of a pass: deriving that `superseded` is unreachable on the retry path.** The chain (a retry meeting a surviving entry is refused and creates nothing, so the lagging reclaim always compares equal) assumes the abandoned attempt created an entry. The reachable path is the attempt whose first entry-creating RPC never reached the adapter: §7.1 still owes a reclaim, the successor creates a fresh entry under its own token, and the lagging reclaim meets it and is answered `superseded`. The proposal's own bullet already contemplates it.
- **MISTAKE nearly filed twice: "§4.1's retained second sentence becomes a false field inventory once the two new `ShutdownRequest` fields land".** It dies on the tree: `ShutdownRequest` ALREADY carries `reason` (2), `deadline_ms` (3) and `coordination_generation` (6) beside the address, so the clause was never an exhaustive field list. One lens lost a full verification round to it.
- **Do NOT file §5.2:459's recycle-`Shutdown` payload list as missing `unconditional_teardown`.** It enumerates the scrub payload (recycle disposition, `podId`, `cleanupCommands`, `cleanupTimeoutSeconds`) and already omits `session_id`, `reason`, `deadline_ms` and `coordination_generation`, all shipped. §4.7's row states the caller rule generically. A filer needs an argument that the sentence is read as the complete field set.
- **Do NOT file §11.4's revoke fan-out as a missing edit site.** §11.4 enumerates no `ShutdownRequest` fields, so no §11 sentence becomes false; the obligation on that caller to set `unconditional_teardown` is carried by the §4.7 row and by the code lane. The same reasoning covers §5.2's recycle boundary sentence.
- **Do NOT file the §15.1 `SETUP_COMMAND_FAILED` cause drift a second time.** It is now staged as a widening under SPEC-5, its exclusion sentence is untouched, spec/06:290 is re-keyed on the gRPC code, spec/07:208 is a one-way enumeration that stays true, and `docs/operator-guide/troubleshooting.md:41` was adjudicated not-a-site because the remedy stays right for the setup-command cause. The row ALSO over-claims before this proposal applies, because shipped `RunSetup` answers `FailedPrecondition` for "adapter is not configured with a workspace root".
- **Do NOT file the §16.1.1 "Used on" column as going stale.** §16.1.1 is the single source of truth for attribute NAMES, both labels are already in it, the column is descriptive, and nothing mechanical parses it. Three lenses have declined it.
- **Do NOT file the `k8s_pod_name` label on SPEC-6's adapter row as unemittable.** The adapter caches the Downward API `POD_NAME`. The earlier "the site cannot supply that label" fix was about the GATEWAY free function `slotReclaimLeaked`, which holds neither the pool nor the pod name, and it must not be generalised to the adapter. At least eight lenses have rebuilt this.
- **WATCHOUT: DOCS-3 is named in the checklist and defined nowhere in `non-spec-changes.md`.** One fix group deleted it and a later one re-minted it with a different subject. If two groups are told to define DOCS-3 in one round, the definitions collide; check what the checklist's S7 line currently means before writing.
- **Do NOT file a §15.4 completeness finding, now for the fifth time.** The refuted set is the missing wire spelling of the unconditional form, the missing status code for the hold refusal, the missing per-outcome response rule, the zero-frame `PrepareWorkspace` response, and the unnamed `ErrorCode` for the hold refusal. Each dies on the same ground: a semantically-stated requirement plus a correct first-party implementation is not a correctness defect.
- **The snapshot trap fired on EVERY round of this window, in both lanes.** `spec-r2` through `spec-r6` and `non-spec-recheck-3-r4`/`-r5` were each byte-identical to the working tree, so `diff -rq` returned nothing and "read the changed sections first" had no target. More than twenty shards opened by discovering it. Run `diff -rq` across the whole snapshot chain first, take the newest snapshot that actually differs, and when every snapshot is identical use `git diff HEAD -- proposals/0081_*/`.
- **MISTAKE that burned six fix groups in one round: findings written against the pre-revision text.** The hand revision moved roughly eighty lines and rewrote whole blocks, and three groups resolved to NO EDIT because the quoted string no longer existed anywhere. Verify a finding's quoted STRING against the current file before designing a fix, and never trust its line number.
- **MISTAKE, the parallel-rewrite seam: the hand revision edited three files at once and left the defects BETWEEN them.** SPEC-6 staged a metric depending on a `ShutdownResponse` field the spec staging never published; SPEC-2 kept a §5.2 placement edit whose mechanism the code lane had withdrawn; CODE-6's call-site table read a `req.GetBindAttempt()` that SCHEMA-1 does not add to `StartSessionRequest`. When reviewing after a multi-file revision, read ACROSS the spec and non-spec files rather than down either one.
- **MISTAKE: a scope call resting on the shipped tree when a staged deliverable moves it.** Recorded again this window in the §5.2 `**Max retries:**` residue and in the "only production call site is `applySlotRetryPolicy`" attribution. Check the ground against the STAGED tree.

- **Do NOT split the pre-`running` cleanup trigger by POD CLASS.** "On a pod serving concurrent
sessions", and an "except on a pod serving one session" carve-out, are false twice: a §7.3 re-attach
reclaim onto a one-session replacement pod IS inside the staged §7.1 scope and does drive
`receiving_uploads → slot_cleanup` there, and the adapter's failed-start cleanup runs on either pod
class. The correct split is by WHICH BIND PATH the §7.1 obligation reaches, and the only uncovered
case is a pre-attached bind on a one-session pod, where `Binder.Prepare`'s reclaim is `failPhase`. A
reviewer's suggested wording invites the wrong split.
- **Leave the matched strings byte-identical when rewording the fence annotation.** The arrow string
`receiving_uploads ──→ slot_cleanup`, the two backticked cell values in DOCS-1's table row and
CODE-3's `{ReceivingUploads, SlotCleanup}` entry are each matched by a staged assertion; the tier-11
gate reads neither the parenthetical annotation nor the row's third column, which is why only those
two were reworded.
- **Do NOT write that the published error catalog "gains rows" for the two new codes.** That is the
pre-revision wording and it is wrong in both directions: DOCS-3 adds no row, and the two adapter
`ErrorCode` values are gateway-to-adapter codes that never reach the client-facing page. The
checklist's S7 line still carries the wrong form, which is why the standing Deferred names it.
- **`.spec-changes.md` has no trailing newline.** A scripted replacement whose search string ends in
`\n` fails on the last paragraph of that file.
- **review-log.md:2260 and :2268 record a DECISION to delete DOCS-3 and a WATCHOUT not to touch
`docs/reference/error-catalog.md:129`. Both are RETIRED** by the 2026-09-16 hand revision and the
round-5 §15.1 widening. A later agent reading either cold will undo DOCS-3; re-deleting it loops the
proposal.
- **MISTAKE nearly filed, and it cost a third of a round: SPEC-5's §6.2 `**Client visibility:**`
replacement and its "any other gRPC code".** It reads as though "any other" has no antecedent, so
the falsified quantification looks like it survives the re-key. It does not: the clause's first half
names the deterministic non-zero setup-command exit surfacing as `SETUP_COMMAND_FAILED`, and §15.1's
own row states that cause's code, so "any other" resolves to "other than `FailedPrecondition`" and
yields the intended meaning. Do not re-file it.
- **Do NOT file "§7.1's carve-out reads as a universal that excludes a §7.3 re-attach onto a
one-session pod".** `spec.2.review-feasibility.1` adjudicated that the implication runs one way only
(outside the obligation → one-session pod, never the converse), so a re-attach onto an exclusive
replacement pod is inside the obligation and does send a reclaim. Frame any exclusive-pod finding on
the §15.1/start arm, which IS explicitly concurrency-scoped.
- **Declined, so nobody re-derives it: the edge-case bullet's "refused on the reclaim hold while the
cleanup runs and on the attempt token afterwards" states disjoint sub-cases rather than two phases of
one.** When the adapter executed the cleanup and only the response was lost, the entry is gone
afterwards and the retry SUCCEEDS; when the adapter never received the reclaim, no hold exists and
only the token refuses; when the cleanup itself failed, staged §5.2 holds the identifier until the
pod terminates, so there is no "afterwards" at all. It over-claims pessimism only and lands in no
applied spec text.
- **Declined: the reclaim is sent once, with no durable record and no re-drive,** so a gateway crash,
a preStop drain or a rolling update between abandoning an attempt and sending its compensation
leaves a tokened entry no live attempt can adopt. It is stated as an accepted failure mode with two
named out-of-scope recoveries, and the residue bullets are barred from reopening. What is still owed
to a human is the standing Open on a rolling update versus a crash.
- **Do NOT file the §15.4 "`Shutdown` is not held" carve-out against §5.2's and §4.7.1's wide hold
predicate, now for the seventh time.** They reconcile on the vacuous-resolve reading: during the
hold the entry is already deregistered, so a `Shutdown` resolves nothing and the outcome is `absent`
either way. If a later round wants it closed, the fix is one clause in the §5.2 hold paragraph
naming the `Shutdown` exemption, never a §15.4 edit.
- **`spec/07:23`'s atomicity paragraph does not close with "The client never receives a
`session_id`..."** — it closes "...regardless of the flag." — although `spec-changes.md:38-40` says
it does. The insertion instruction at :444-447 quotes the real ending, so the edit applies cleanly.
Below the bar; do not re-derive.
- **A format pass may not mint a recommendation, alternatives-and-why-each-lost, or a confidence for
open decision 20.** It carries its question and its ground only, and it is now the section's sole
entry. The next adjudication firing owes the other three.
- **The 0078 overlap in `shutdown_drain_gate_race_test.go` is a merge hazard, not a design
conflict.** The two proposals' edit regions inside that file are distinct: 0078 touches
`socketDrainPod`'s accept timeout and the sequenced leg's assertion, while this proposal sets a
request field on four `ShutdownRequest` literals. The corrected impacts row says so in those terms,
so a later reader does not read it as a design conflict.
- **MISTAKE: a recorded WATCHOUT on a normative predicate sat unfiled for six rounds and shipped into five paraphrases.** The `mid_session` zero-value hazard was written down as a WATCHOUT (a later frame whose `mid_session` is false where the first was true IS a refusal) and no round filed it, so the symmetric per-frame predicate refused every ordinary multi-chunk §7.4 mid-session upload for six rounds of staging. Its "CODE-8 is the sender" framing is what kept it out of the spec lane, although the spec half was filable on its own. A recorded WATCHOUT on a normative predicate is filed or refuted, never carried.
- **The `PrepareWorkspace` per-frame predicate lives at SEVEN sites and they must stay one statement.** Two are normative (the §4.7.1 `PrepareWorkspace is client-streaming` paragraph and the §15.4 clause beginning "An adapter does not conform when it resolves the slot identifier more than once"); five are paraphrases in `non-spec-changes.md` (CODE-6's staging.go description :1307, CONF-1 clause (i) :1554, CONF-1's negative-case walkthrough :1609, the gateway tier-1 case :1966, the tier-3 clause list :2276). A fix touching only the normative pair leaves a conformance battery encoding the withdrawn symmetric rule.
- **The zero-value hazard has a SECOND face: the well-formedness pair.** If "non-mid-session with an empty `bind_attempt` is `INVALID_ARGUMENT`" is applied per frame, every later frame of a mid-session upload (empty token, default-false marker) is ill-formed and the upload is refused by well-formedness before the disagreement rule is reached. The fix has to say in the same paragraph that the admission rules are decided on the resolving frame.
- **Do NOT touch the Recycle-edges group's `a failed session` clause at spec/06:114-118.** It is correct for its own group, whose preamble requires the claim to be patched `bound → recycling` at the recycle boundary first, and deleting it breaks the clean-recycle retirement path. The defect is the OTHER `claimed ──→ draining` entry, the one under "Occupancy projection".
- **spec/06:80 carries TWO claim-deletion clauses and only one is corrected.** "a claim deleted on a pod with `recycle.enabled: false`" is the over-narrow one. "a claim deleted on a recycling pod under its limits projects `idle`" describes the `reserved → idle` edge after the claim has been patched, matches `case state.Reserved` in occupancy.go, and is correct as written.
- **The tempting wrong fix for the recycle qualifier is an exception clause** reading "and on a recycle-enabled pool the pre-attached failure is treated as a failed session". No actor writes that disposition on the pre-attached path, so the clause is a fiction the tree does not satisfy. It is exactly the hair the loop exists to prevent.
- **MISTAKE: the round-7 rewrite replaced an unconditional statement with a two-branch one.** The settled entry stated the retirement unconditionally; `git diff c1ab102f3..096b39656` on spec-changes.md replaced it with a two-branch form that reads as a tightening of the citation and was in fact the introduction of a claim no actor produces. Cost: one deep finding and a whole design round. The staged text drifted from its own settled record; check a "tightening" against the standing entry it narrows.
- **The one-session carve-out is a SWEEP, not a single edit.** Three rounds have now each found one more un-narrowed universal left behind by it: the §5.2 append, the accepted-failure bullet, and §4.7.9 step 5's trailing "rather than leaving the pod holding the session's state". Before declaring the sweep done, grep spec-changes.md for every sentence asserting that a pre-`running` bind failure is reclaimed, cleaned up, or leaves no state on the pod, and check each against the carve-out at spec-changes.md:641,:704.
- **The carve-out is scoped by STAGE, not by pod class, and that is what rescues it from the standing "do not split by pod class" trap.** It covers only a bind that fails at the workspace-preparation, setup or credential-assignment stage. `Binder.Resume` issues NO `PrepareWorkspace`, `FinalizeWorkspace`, `RunSetup` or `AssignCredentials` — it calls `connect`, `reserveResumeSlot` (kube and Redis only) and then `cl.Resume` — so no §7.3 re-attach can fail at one of those three stages and the carve-out and the re-attach arm do not overlap. Two round-8 lenses each nearly filed the pod-class objection. Do not re-file it without first showing a re-attach RPC at one of those stages. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/binder.go:1590-1629.
- **MISTAKE: a fix round corrected the `superseded` gloss at the §16.1 row only,** leaving the same too-narrow gloss standing at the §4.7 row, §4.7.1's outcome paragraph and §15.4's republication, even though the round that filed it wrote "if a fixer touches one, it must touch all three". Filed again at round 8. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:400,:741,:793 (narrow) versus :463,:894 (wide).
- **Do NOT "fix" spec-changes.md:400 by making a named reclaim REMOVE an untokened entry.** An untokened entry may be a live successor's own `StartSession` entry. The fix is the outcome NAME only: widen the gloss to cover an entry no bind attempt owns, leaving "performs neither, removes nothing" exactly as staged.
- **UNVERIFIED (two round-8 shards disagree and neither corrects the other):** `spec.8.review-client-surface.2` records the `superseded` gloss drift as twice-refuted and says do not file it a third time, while `spec.8.review-mechanism.2` filed it as two substantiated findings on the ground that :400's defect is an enumeration HOLE and :741/:793's is a false rationale clause. The newer mechanism reading is kept; whoever settles it should start from whether the two shards are describing the same defect.
- **The snapshot trap fired again, in its twelfth recorded form.** `diff -rq spec-r8 <live>` reports only the review log differing, while the real r7→r8 delta is 189 lines against `spec-r7`. Compounding it, `spec-r9` exists and is dated 2026-09-10, so it is PRE-revision epoch content. Take the newest snapshot that DIFFERS, by mtime, and never the highest-numbered one.
- **MISTAKE: a resumed round re-executes lenses whose shard is already on disk.** The round-8 shard directory already held a file for every lens, and several lenses re-ran a pass whose honest answer was already recorded. Read your own shard first and diff the staging's mtime against the shard's before spending the round; if the staging has not moved, say so and re-confirm rather than re-derive. A resumed run must still show its own evidence, so re-run the two or three mechanical checks that carry the verdict.
- **"Admission" throughout the staged §4.7.1 and §15.4 blocks means the ADAPTER's own gRPC request admission, never a Kubernetes admission webhook.** Third recording of this trap.
- **`onHoldTimeout` deregisters EVERY started session in pass 1 and runs the cleanups sequentially in pass 2,** so under the reclaim hold ("from the critical section that deregisters the entry until the cleanup finishes") the hold on member N is open for the whole of members 1..N-1. At the top `maxConcurrentSessions` that is a long hold. It is NOT a regression, because every one of those sessions is being terminated anyway. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/holdstate.go:189-205.
- **The remedy for the two accepted-residue findings is ONE SENTENCE EACH in the staged SPEC-5 §4.7.1 block,** never a request to build remediation position 2 (a durable pending-reclaim record, a reaper, a tree generation, or a bind-scoped lock). A fixer who reads the finding as "close the residue" changes the design the loop settled. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:284-289,:309-314.
- **MISTAKE: a fix stage declared a lane converged without sweeping `### Open` for entries marked FILED.** Round 7 ran two fix groups plus a corrections pass and touched neither of the two accepted-residue findings, which the standing `### Open` still recorded as FILED with an unapplied remedy. Sweeping `### Open` for FILED-and-unapplied against the staging took ten minutes at round 8 and found both live. Do that sweep before declaring convergence.
- **The two new §16.1 rows for the GATEWAY counters have NO gate at all.** `adapter_metric_catalog_test.go` reads only `pkg/adapter/metrics.go`, and nothing reconciles the gateway catalog against §16.1 or metrics.md, so if CODE-9's metrics.md rows are dropped, tier 11 stays green.
- **DOCS-2 claims `docs/reference/adapter-contract.md` "publishes `ConfigureWorkspace` as idempotent". The page does not.** Its row reads only "Point a pre-connected session at the finalized `cwd` (SDK-warm mode). Timeout: 10s."; the idempotency claim lives at spec/04:673 alone. DOCS-2 must either add the idempotency clause to that row or drop the self-reference. A separate trap bars filing the neighbouring `DemoteSDK` row; this is a different row and a different defect. EVIDENCE: docs/reference/adapter-contract.md:63; non-spec-changes.md:1826,:1828.
- **`ConfigureWorkspace` DOES create a registry entry** (`claimSessionSlot` at sdkwarm.go:216), so §5.2's entry-creating request list is complete and correct. A lens that assumes `ConfigureWorkspace` only "points" an existing session at a cwd will wrongly file the list as over-broad.
- **CORRECTS the orchestrator brief: `ShutdownResponse` does not gain `slot_was_started`, and `slot_reclaim` is new rather than kept.** The brief for this run states "ShutdownResponse keeps slot_reclaim and gains slot_was_started (4)"; both halves are wrong against the staging. A lens checking the §15.4 wire enumeration against the brief will file a phantom omission. Three round-8 lenses each spent time on it.
- **The §5.2 `**Slot cleanup:**` pointer is the one seam in the append where two readings survive.** The bullet sits under the `maxConcurrentSessions > 1` heading and its own trigger is "On slot completion or failure"; SPEC-3's pointer carries the ACTION LIST across the concurrency boundary while SPEC-3's next sentences deny the bullet's trigger on a one-session pod. One round-8 lens read the pointer as scoped to the action list and declined; the standing dead-end entry explicitly leaves a re-open argued from the bullet's own trigger open. EVIDENCE: spec/05:543,:545; spec-changes.md:641.
- **`sessionPolicy` "Pod reuse" (`maxConcurrentSessions: 1` with `recycle.enabled: true`) is a REAL configuration,** so the recycling half of the one-session carve-out is not vacuous. A reviewer who assumes one-session pods never recycle will wrongly refute any finding that rests on it. EVIDENCE: spec/05:425.
- **Candidate DROPPED, do not re-file: a surviving residue entry on a reused single-session pod suppresses the next session's MCP arming.** `claimPodMCPStartLocked` arms the intra-pod MCP surface only when `len(s.slots) != 1` is false, and `slotCount()` is the quantity §28.5.3's resolve-or-reject rule reads, so one residue entry makes every unaddressed frame unresolvable for the life of the pod. The behaviour is real, shipped and unchanged by any staged deliverable, and the staged sentence asserts nothing false, so the remedy would be new spec text about a pre-existing residue class. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/slotsession.go:109-115,:401-407; pkg/adapter/podscrub.go:39-81.
- **Candidate DROPPED, do not re-file: the crash-stranded tokened entry is a failure mode less reliable than shipped.** Today a retry ADOPTS the stale entry (`ensureSlotStateLocked` returns the existing `*slotState`) and succeeds; under the staging every later attempt at that session on that pod is refused `SLOT_BIND_ATTEMPT_SUPERSEDED` until the pod is replaced. The mechanism is real and the adoption half is verifiable, but the proposal states it bullet for bullet as an accepted failure mode, its incidence is crash-only, and the accounting drains the pod at `maxConcurrentSessions: 2` on one refusal. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/slot.go:104-107; spec-changes.md:296-307.
- **Candidate DROPPED, and it cost the most time of any round-8 candidate: "SPEC-4's retiring exit is infeasible on a sequential-reuse pool."** The spec sentence it rests on is real (spec/06:80) and `failPhase`'s drain really is a bare `DeleteClaim`, but the shipped projection resolves a claim DELETE on a `claimed` pod to `Draining` unconditionally of recycle, and spec/05:455 plus §6.2's pod-crash paragraph both retire a failed session's pod regardless of recycle settings. Two rounds have now walked this to the end.
- **MISTAKE: the r8→r9 rewrite deleted the §4.7 row's clean-exit sentence and broadened §7.1's leak predicate in the same round.** Each change is defensible alone; together the broadened predicate lost its referent, and a conforming third-party adapter could answer `absent` with `exited_cleanly: false` and leak the slot. Cost: one design-defect finding, a round, and three stale back-references that still asserted the deleted sentence. When a fix deletes a sentence another section depends on, grep the whole proposal for the claim before landing it.
- **MISTAKE: an earlier round declared spec/06:80's `idle` clause "correct as written" and told the fixer to leave it.** It was correct only while its sibling still carried the `recycle.enabled: false` qualifier. Widening the sibling to "either recycle setting" made both clauses true at once for a claim deleted on a `maxConcurrentSessions: 1` + `recycle.enabled: true` pod under its limits, which is exactly what a failed bind produces, and one input got two answers. Widening one clause of a two-clause partition is not a local edit; both clauses move together.
- **MISTAKE: SPEC-4's own rationale said the claim-deletion rule was stated "twice".** That count is what kept eight rounds from looking in spec/04, where it is stated twice more. It is five sites across two sections. `grep -rn "one-session-only invariant" spec/` and `grep -rn "recycling pod under its limits" spec/ docs/` find every one in two commands, and the second also surfaces the docs mirror. Cost: one full round.
- **MISTAKE: a client-surface shard dropped the §15.1 "bind-sequence request" finding on the wrong ground,** writing "it dies on the scope word: the staged sentence says 'a setup-window request'". The sentence's SECOND disjunct was "a bind-sequence request", which §4.7.1 binds to six RPCs, five of which never reach `writeSetupCommandError`. The drop cost three rounds of the defect standing. Read every disjunct of a sentence before dropping a narrowing finding on one of them.
- **Do NOT file the §15.1 exclusion sentence's parenthetical as an incomplete partition.** After the narrowing, a `FAILED_PRECONDITION` answered to a non-setup-command bind request is not this code and stays the retryable fallback, which is the CORRECT disposition, so only the appositive gloss ("every gRPC code other than `FailedPrecondition`") is imprecise. Two lenses reached it and both declined; a filing needs a wrong OUTCOME rather than an imprecise gloss.

- **Reading this file needs the Read tool in passes of 100 to 150 lines, and `### Traps` and `### Open` first.** Bash output above roughly 2KB is persisted rather than shown and the Read tool refuses more than 25k tokens per call, so the whole log is roughly twenty-five calls. The cost is dominated by the number of TURNS rather than the number of calls, because each turn re-sends the accumulated context: issue several Read calls in one turn.

- **MISTAKE, three rounds on ONE sentence: the per-slot guard's scope written as an RPC enumeration.** Round 8 stated it as a list of RPC names; the list omitted `Resume`, whose checkpoint extraction is the adapter's biggest unlocked tree write, and included `Shutdown` whole, which put the two arms that remove nothing behind an unbounded materialization. Round 9 found both. Adding `Resume` to the list is the same answer one step smaller, so the replacement is a stated PREDICATE plus a derivation table with a "path work outside `s.mu`" column, which is checkable against the tree while a list is not. - **MISTAKE, two more rounds on the same paragraph: bounding a WAIT and protecting the WORK AFTER the wait are different problems.** Round 10 answered "§10.1.4 pass 2 blocks on an uninterruptible guard" by making the acquisition context-bounded, which stopped the wait running past the budget but not CONSUMING it; round 11's suggested fix (slice the shared budget per member) repeats the kind. The answer that held decouples the two budgets. A related false universal, "one section's holder cannot delay another section past its own budget", was DELETED rather than qualified, because a caller-budget claim stated inside a paragraph about the guard HELPERS is false wherever two sections share a context, which §10.1.4 pass 2 does.

- **WATCHOUT: Go defers run LIFO, so a defer registered LATER runs FIRST.** A design note saying the guard unlock should be "deferred after `defer release()` so the guard outlives the destructive section" has it backwards: `defer unlockSlot()` must be registered BEFORE `defer release()`. This was written down wrong once and corrected in the same round.
- **WATCHOUT: dropping a guard-table entry never blocks a holder.** The holder keeps the old channel and the next acquirer mints a second one, so a drop voids the guard at exactly the moment it is needed. The table is pod-lifetime and deliberately never dropped; a refcount was rejected as a second mechanism with its own leak mode for a table the pod's served-session count already bounds. A finding's own closing sentence ("having `Shutdown` hold the guard across its destructive section closes this too") is wrong on its face — the deletion would happen while `Shutdown` itself holds the guard.
- **WATCHOUT: the expiry disposition must NOT be uniform across the two hand-out forms.** A destructive section that cannot obtain the guard proceeds and destroys unguarded, which is today's shipped behaviour; an admission RPC that cannot obtain it must be refused, because proceeding unguarded is how a materialization re-creates the tree a reclaim just removed. One rule for both is the tempting simplification and it reintroduces the original race.
