- **Should §5.2's cleanup action list name the session and artifact directories?** — OPEN: `RemoveTree` removes them as part of the one act the new exception's parenthetical describes, and §6.4's own slot-tree enumeration names three directories where the parenthetical names four. Judged below the bar because the list is not stated as exhaustive. See `non-spec-recheck-2.4.fix-design-G1.1` and `non-spec-recheck-2.5.review-fresh.1`.
- **Is the staged §5.2 ten-second graceful window a normative constant that wants an operator tunable and a §11.3 row?** — OPEN: §10.1 owns the hold timeout and states no such window, §11.3's tunables table carries the 120s timeout and would carry no row for this one, and `terminateHeldSession` passes no grace deadline today. See `spec.1.review-kubernetes.1` and `spec.1.review-security.1`.
- **Is the §15.4 published hold block owed the "life of the pod" clause?** — OPEN: §4.7.1, §5.2, §6.2 and §7.1 all carry it and §15.4 stops at "until the cleanup that reclaims the slot has completed". Logically entailed rather than contradicted, and §15.4 defers scope to §5.2, so three lenses declined it; a fixer touching any of the four should touch all four. See `non-spec-recheck-2.3.review-security.1`.
- **Does `pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/binder_envtest_test.go` have a reachable envtest home?** — UNVERIFIED and DISPUTED with no correction between the readings: `non-spec.14.review-kubernetes.1` says yes (the package's tests are `package podsession_test` and `binder_test.go:180` calls the shared `envtest.Start(t)` helper with the import at :41), while `non-spec-recheck-2.5.review-citations.1` says no file in that package uses envtest today and the envtest files are all under `podclaim/`. The newer reading is kept here; whoever lands the tier-2 case should settle it first.
- **Does `buf breaking` actually run in this repo's gates?** — UNVERIFIED: the Design asserts it "has nothing to fire on" for an additive window, and nobody found the gate that runs it. Someone auditing the SCHEMA-1 step should confirm the gate exists before relying on the sentence. See `non-spec-recheck-2.5.review-applicability.1`.
- **Which client envelope does a rule-6 `FAILED_PRECONDITION` at a non-setup-command bind request reach?** — UNVERIFIED: SPEC-5's §6.2 re-key asserts the retryable `STARTING_FAILED` fallback, while shipped `SlotBindError.Reason()` exempts only `Stage == "workspace_prep"`, so a `FAILED_PRECONDITION` at the credential-assignment or session-start stage classifies `policy_rejection` and renders the non-retryable 422 `SLOT_FAILED`. Reaching it needs an untokened started entry. See `spec.1.review-client-surface.1` and `spec.1.review-fresh.1`.
- **Is the §6.2 `**Client visibility:**` bullet left assigning the started-session refusal to neither envelope?** — OPEN: SPEC-5 narrows the exclusion clause and leaves the inclusion clause keyed on a deterministic non-zero setup-command exit. §15.1's row does cover it and the equivalent §15.1 asymmetry was refuted on "restricting a subject is not an affirmative denial", so it was left; widen the inclusion clause in the same edit if a fixer touches the clause. See `spec-recheck-2.5.review-fresh.1`.
- **Does the §5.2 whole-pod scrub racing the per-slot cleanup on the removing arm want an ordering statement?** — OPEN: `answerShutdown` fires the asynchronous `startPodScrub` while the slot guard and the reclaim hold are held and the tree removal is in flight, and no staged text orders them. See `non-spec.12.fix-G3.1`.
- **Does the §16.1 superseded row's "the slot is not leaked" want §7.1's clean-exit qualifier?** — OPEN, adjudicated NOT a defect once: §4.7.1's reclaim-outcome rule makes `superseded` imply a clean exit normatively, so the row states the conforming meaning and §7.1's wide predicate is the gateway's defence against a non-conforming adapter. Recorded so it is not re-derived. See `spec-recheck-2.3.review-operational.1`.
- **Where in `docs/reference/metrics.md` does the gateway-side `lenny_slot_compensation_superseded_total` row go?** — OPEN: the adapter-side row is placed ("under that page's `## Adapter metrics` table") and the gateway one is not. The obvious home is beside `lenny_slot_failure_total` at :166. See `non-spec-recheck-2.3.review-operational.1`.
- **Is `Client.Shutdown`'s doc comment at `adapterclient/client.go:793-799` false after CODE-1?** — OPEN: "the adapter closes its runtime" is now gated on an admitted start, and "a request naming a session the adapter has already released is a no-op reporting a clean exit" is now the `absent` outcome. CODE-4 and CODE-7 both open that file. Go doc comments are outside the enumerated surfaces, which is why it was not filed. See `non-spec.14.review-fresh.1`.
- **Does the CODE-1 leak-accounting paragraph read as two opposed statements about one counterfactual?** — OPEN: "the slot never reaches `lenny_adapter_leaked_slots` and never counts toward `ceil(maxConcurrentSessions/2)`" sits two clauses before a sentence saying the leak count is persistent and stamps `lenny.dev/drain-request` at the threshold. A `leaked` scrub report does not touch the gauge but DOES feed `tracker.RecordLeak`. Judged rationale imprecision. See `non-spec-recheck-2.5.review-operational.1`.
- **Does a `Shutdown` carrying the recycle disposition still run the whole-pod scrub when the no-entry rule fires first?** — UNVERIFIED: the rule says such a request "removes nothing, performs neither teardown, and answers `absent`", while SPEC-1's §4.1 replacement keeps the scrub independent. Unreachable from a conforming gateway. Any future fix to rule 11 or that §4.1 sentence must keep the two statements together. See `spec-recheck-2.5.review-security.1` and `spec.1.review-security.1`.
- **Can a coordinator handoff mid-bind fence the compensating reclaim?** — UNVERIFIED: `ShutdownRequest` carries `coordination_generation` and the proto claims a pod validates it on every RPC, but only `CoordinatorFence` and `CheckpointBarrier` read it, so a pod holds no generation for a session never fenced, and a bind attempt does not fence. Someone who can establish whether a fence can land mid-bind should close it. See `spec.1.review-performance.1`.
- **Does `summary.md`'s SPEC-3 deliverable-index entry owe the third §5.2 anchor?** — OPEN across four rounds: the checklist S4 line and the spec files-touched list were both updated for it and the index entry still names only the action list, the scrub model and the reclaim hold. Declined twice as bookkeeping. See `non-spec-recheck-2.4.review-citations.1` and `.review-fresh.1`.
- **Does `pkg/adapter/manifest_fields_test.go:220` belong in the files-touched test list?** — UNVERIFIED: it calls `ensureSlotStateLocked` and the other six call sites are listed. The proposal hedges with "roughly twenty-two test call sites ... not enumerated here", and the real count is nearer twenty-nine. Below the bar as hedged non-enumeration. See `non-spec-recheck-2.5.review-citations.1`.
- **Do S16, S18 and S21's `Depends-on` lines owe S15 and S1?** — OPEN: S16 consumes `lockSlotGuard`, which S15 creates, and S18 implements SPEC-5's start-confirmation rule from S1. Harmless while the checklist executes as one ordered sequence; it bites anything that schedules off the dependency edges. See `non-spec.14.review-applicability.1` and `non-spec-recheck-2.5.review-applicability.1`.
- **Does CODE-7's S11 or CODE-4's S13 own the `client.go` request-side widenings?** — OPEN: both claim the identical set. Landing them at S11 breaks `binder.go:1327` and `upload_to_session.go:128` at compile time until S13; landing them at S13 leaves S11 a pure-translation step. Either resolution works, so nothing was filed, but a reader deciding S11's scope has two answers. See `non-spec.14.review-applicability.1`.
- **Does the `Binder.Resume` compensation's accepted stall raise the leak rate materially on a checkpoint-heavy pool?** — UNVERIFIED: a large `ExtractTree` holds the slot guard past the reclaim budget, the reclaim answers nothing, and the slot books `leaked`. The proposal accepts it explicitly, so it is a quantification question. See `non-spec-recheck-2.4.review-performance.1`.

- DEFERRED [schemas/lenny-adapter.proto]: the `Shutdown` RPC comment ("asks the adapter to terminate the agent and release the pod ... Returns when the agent process has exited") and the `ReportSessionScrub` / `SessionScrubOutcome` comments ("on every session release") are the proto-side statement of the contract §15.4 says is "kept in sync with the prose". SPEC-1's two-teardown split, its no-op clean-exit answer, and SPEC-3's withheld report all falsify parts of them. GROUND CHANGED: the amendment retracted the S-2 reading this entry rested on, because SCHEMA-1 now opens `schemas/lenny-adapter.proto` under S-2's second window (summary.md:94-95) at S8. Two lenses nonetheless declined to file the comment repair here, on the ground that the `Shutdown` comment is ALREADY false today for a session the adapter holds no entry for and for a per-slot `Shutdown` on a co-tenanted pod, which is the pre-existing-looseness class prior rounds killed. So the correction is now LANDABLE here rather than barred, and whether to land it or leave it to R1b is a live choice rather than a constraint. The R1b attribution below is the older disposition. The `Shutdown` comment is already stale for a co-tenanted pod, so this is a widening rather than a new break. NARROWED: `ShutdownResponse` carries no doc comment at all (:1665-1668 is a bare three-line message), so CODE-1's re-keying of `exited_cleanly` owes nothing here and the entry is the three comments named below and no more. - DEFERRED [pkg/gateway/runtime/adapterclient/client.go, the `Client.Shutdown` doc comment at :797-798]: it says "A zero deadline lets the adapter apply its default grace period". That is false. When the caller's ctx carries a deadline, `resolveShutdownGrace` takes the ctx's remaining time in preference to both the runtime-configured grace and the package default, so a zero deadline yields the CALLER's own remaining budget; the adapter's default applies only when the caller set no deadline at all. OWNER CHANGED: `adapterclient` IS a target now — CODE-6 opens `pkg/gateway/runtime/adapterclient/client.go` for the epoch latch, `BindEpoch` and `ShutdownReclaim` — so the one-sentence repair can be staged under CODE-6 at S9 rather than left to whoever next opens the file. - DEFERRED [proposals/0081_.../0081_....non-spec-changes.md, CODE-4]: CODE-4 opens `pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/binder.go` and stages no correction to `drain`'s doc comment at :1191-1194, which reads "On a `recycle.enabled: false` pool a claim DELETE projects `draining` then `terminated`; on a recycling pool under its limits the projection returns the pod to `idle`". Both halves are falsified by SPEC-4 and by the controller. What is true instead: a claim DELETE while the pod projects `claimed` projects `draining` then `terminated` on a pool of either recycle setting, and the return to inventory is the `reserved → idle` edge reached only after the claim has been patched through `recycling`. Comment-only, no behavioural change; fourth recording.

- DEFERRED [implementation-checklist.md, S15]: S15 is INCOMPLETE and partly false after the round-9 through round-11 guard work, and this SUPERSEDES every narrower form of the S15 deferral. Its opening "A per-slot mutex" names a representation CODE-6 no longer stages (the guard is a per-slot capacity-one channel); its "The guard is acquired ahead of `s.mu` in both" is false for `Shutdown`, which compares under `s.mu` first and acquires the guard only on the arm that destroys; its guard-scope enumeration omits `Resume`; its hand-out sentence contrasts the hold-checking form with "letting the caller block", which leads straight to the unbounded acquisition the change removes. What is true instead: a section acquires the slot's guard before the resolve that hands out its paths and holds it until its path work ends, which is `PrepareWorkspace`, `FinalizeWorkspace`, `RunSetup` and `Resume`; the three destructive sections acquire it before `s.mu`, except `Shutdown`; both hand-out forms take the caller's context and neither wait outlives it; a destructive section whose acquisition expires destroys unguarded and logs `slot_guard_not_acquired`, an admission RPC whose acquisition expires is refused; `onHoldTimeout`'s ten-second context is the pass's guard-acquisition deadline alone and each member mints its own ten-second close context for `emitFinalUsage` and `Runtime.Close`; and S15's test list gains the three tier-1 expiry cases (destructive fall-through, admission refusal, per-member close budget) plus a whole-file 4.7.1 credit for `pkg/adapter/holdstate_test.go` in `tests/spec-map.json`.

S7 also names only the `TestAdapterContractNamesTheShutdownRPCUnderItsWireName` extension where `## Testing` requires both shipped tests in `tests/tier11_docs/per_slot_substate_scope_doc_reconciliation_test.go` to be extended in place. What is true instead: the deferred closure takes the release only when the cleanup completed, expressed as `completed := false`, `defer func(){ if completed { release() } }()`, and `completed = closeErr == nil && treeErr == nil` after the tree removal; and the handler's cleanup-outcome report stays keyed on `closeErr` alone, with a `slot_tree_removal_failed` warn recording the tree error.

The `upload_to_session.go` edit is load-bearing: once `validateBindFields` lands, a §7.4 mid-session `PrepareWorkspace` carrying no token and `mid_session` false is `INVALID_ARGUMENT`, so the whole mid-session upload route refuses and the tier-7a reclaim-hold §7.4 arm goes red. No checklist step names the file either. Not filed, because the client.go signature change breaks the caller at compile time in the same step and the value is stated twice.
- DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md, CODE-6's heading file list]: it omits `pkg/adapter/credentials.go`, `pkg/adapter/server.go` and `pkg/adapter/sdkwarm.go`, all three of which its own body and `## Files touched` assign to it. Re-derive the heading from `## Files touched` rather than patching one name at a time; the identical defect against `resume.go` was confirmed and fixed in an earlier round, so the class is live.
- DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md, CODE-5's `Targets:` list]: it does not name `isTransientPodClaimError`, although the checklist line, the summary index line and `## Files touched` all assign the `codes.Aborted` arm to CODE-5 and the arm is specified in CODE-5's own body. The list owes `pkg/gateway/sessionserver/start.go` `isTransientPodClaimError` beside its existing entries.
- DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md, the DOCS-3 closing-sentence replacement]: it publishes the superseded refusal as "a request refused because **a newer start** of the same session has superseded this one". The staged rule fires on token INEQUALITY with no ordering, and the proposal's own canonical case is the inverse ("Attempt 2 is refused while attempt 1's entry stands"), so the blocking attempt is OLDER. The other four DOCS-3 cells use the correct direction-neutral vocabulary, "another start of the same session already holds the pod slot". What is true instead: use that phrasing in the fifth cell too. `grep -n "a newer"` over the whole staging returns that one site. FILED.
- DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md, the resume-path test bullet]: it asserts that `Binder.Resume` "releases no gateway-side credential lease: the `fakeAssigner` records every `ReleaseSession` call in `released` ..., so the assertion is that the list stays empty". `Binder.Resume` calls neither `ReleaseSession` nor `Release(leaseID)`, so an assertion on `released` alone is true on every path. What is true instead: assert the new `Release(leaseID)` recorder empty as well, or state the property the arm is actually about.
- DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md, the tier-7a §10.1.4 ordering arm]: its second run was non-discriminating in both directions and the close-budget property moved to a deterministic tier-1 case. What is true instead: the tier-7a arm keeps only the ordering and no-residue assertions, with a park SHORTER than the acquisition deadline so the guard is genuinely acquired; a park longer than the blocked caller's own context is a passing run that asserts nothing.
- DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md, the "slot tree removal failed" logging convention]: the new prose claims `slot_tree_removal_failed` "is the form every site this deliverable stops discarding a tree-removal error at uses", while the staged `terminateHeldSession` captures `treeErr` and logs only `runtime_close_failed`. What is true instead: either log the tree-removal warning there too or narrow the sentence.
- DEFERRED [pkg/gateway/runtime/adapterclient/client.go, the `Client.Shutdown` doc comment at :797-798]: it says "A zero deadline lets the adapter apply its default grace period". That is false. When the caller's ctx carries a deadline, `resolveShutdownGrace` takes the ctx's remaining time in preference to both the runtime-configured grace and the package default, so a zero deadline yields the CALLER's own remaining budget; the adapter's default applies only when the caller set no deadline at all. `adapterclient/client.go` IS a target (CODE-4 and CODE-7 both open it), so the one-sentence repair can be staged there. Derived independently by six shards across three windows and still unapplied.
- DEFERRED [pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/binder.go:1191-1195, `Binder.drain`'s doc comment]: it states the retired projection keying, "On a `recycle.enabled: false` pool a claim DELETE projects `draining` then `terminated`; on a recycling pool under its limits the projection returns the pod to `idle`". Both halves are falsified by SPEC-4 and by `ProjectOccupancyPhase`, which returns `Draining` from `Claimed` on every pool. What is true instead: a claim DELETE while the pod projects `claimed` projects `draining` then `terminated` on a pool of either recycle setting, and the return to inventory is the `reserved → idle` edge reached only after the claim has been patched through `recycling`. `ReclaimClaimed`'s comment at :1184-1189 carries the same gloss. Comment-only; fifth recording, and CODE-4 opens the file.
- DEFERRED [spec-changes.md, "Spec files touched" and the Design pointer list, three corrections the spec lane's own passes could not take]: the list still describes the §4.7 `Shutdown` row edit as "(first sentence plus one sentence)" where the replacement swaps two sentences for a multi-sentence block; it still describes the §6.2 client-visibility edit as "one clause re-keyed on the gRPC code" where the clause is re-keyed on the request the adapter answered as well as on the code; and the Design section's pointer list omits the SPEC-2 §7.2 snapshot-close edits. Every statement is mutually consistent with the applied spec, so nothing breaks. A fixer with the spec lane open takes all three.
- DEFERRED [spec-changes.md, the §15.4 table's dependants]: the table's deletion also breaks spec-changes.md:909 ("§15.4 publishes the status its failure is answered on", about rule 8's `ABORTED`, which §4.7.1 states) and the files-touched line describing §15.4 as "a pointer at §4.7.1 and a table of the wire observables per numbered rule", plus summary.md:944 and :958 and non-spec-changes.md:2375. They were listed in scope for the deleting fixer; whoever next opens those files should confirm each moved.
- DEFERRED [spec-changes.md:277-279, the untokened-entry edge-case bullet]: it calls the §10.1 hold-timeout termination "a request that removes an entry without naming a bind attempt", while the staged §5.2 hold paragraph says that same termination "runs under no request", and the tree agrees (`deregisterStartedSessions` is an internal pass, not an RPC handler). The remover SET is correct; only the genus noun is wrong. Say "an unconditional teardown, an SDK demotion, or the §10.1 hold-timeout termination, which runs under no request".
- DEFERRED [spec-changes.md and implementation-checklist.md, the duplicated noun]: the S4 gloss and the §5.2 reclaim-hold paragraph both now read "the slot's slot tree" after the widening. Wording only; a copy-edit pass should take it.

### Retired

Retired in compaction pass 22, each closed rather than dropped. Two `### Open` items: the CODE-1 comment-phrasing UNVERIFIED, whose quoted sentence no longer exists at `non-spec-changes.md:72-77` (`non-spec.14.review-fresh.1` read lines 60-84 and found the mid-session-conditioning paragraphs there instead), and "Does a gateway-side catalog sweep hold `lenny_slot_compensation_superseded_total` to both §16.1 and `docs/reference/metrics.md`?", answered NO by `spec-recheck-2.5.review-edit-sites.1`: `adapter_metric_catalog_test.go` reads `pkg/adapter/metrics.go` alone and `catalog_crosscheck_test.go` is the ALERT catalog, so the gateway series is held by nothing and the adapter series is held by both. Seventeen `### Deferred` entries are marked RETIRED IN PASS 22 in place rather than deleted, so their citations still resolve: the eight checklist deferrals `non-spec.14.review-applicability.1` verified discharged line by line (S1, S3, S6, S7, S13, S15, S17, S20) plus the S5/S7 scope pair, the nine `non-spec-changes.md` entries `non-spec.14.review-fresh.1` reported FIXED in the staging, the missing-DOCS-3 deliverable-index entry that `f8.cleanup` records as present, and the renumber-staleness entry, which is closed by construction now that step numbers live only in the checklist. The `sessionscrubreporter.go` doc-comment Deferred four shards raised died with the `errors.Join` revert and was never added. The "Tier-4 fixture extension" Open was CORRECTED rather than retired: `recycle_scrub_path_test.go` does carry a concurrent-slot fixture.

Retired in compaction pass 18, each with the reason that keeps it closed. Three Open items were retired because the 2026-09-16 revision deleted the mechanism they rested on, and each has a token-era sibling that stands: "Does CONF-1 owe a fifth property?" (its four properties were all bind-epoch and its `PrepareWorkspace` clause was about minting more than one epoch per call; the live form is "Does CONF-1 owe more properties, and does the reclaim hold owe one?"); "May an intermediate commit carry a `WIRED` claim row whose reader is two steps away?" (it turned on `Client.BindEpoch` at S9; the live form merges both halves as "Are the two `WIRED` claim-register rows owed at all, and may an intermediate commit carry one whose reader is two steps away?"); and "Does the tier-3 `adapter_bind_epoch` directory name survive the rename to the token?" (`tests/tier3_contract/adapter_bind_epoch/` is named by no staged deliverable, and the surviving half, that naming a test file with `slot` in its basename pulls it into `slotAddressCaseFiles`, is kept as its own entry). One Deferred was retired as superseded: the epoch-vocabulary duplicate of CONF-1's property count, whose token-vocabulary form stands unchanged beside it.

Three further Open items were retired in pass 18 as ANSWERED by round 8 rather than by the revision, their answers moved into `### Settled`: whether a coordinator handoff or a rolling update can strand a tokened entry (it cannot; only a SIGKILL, crash or OOM can); whether a `Shutdown` carrying neither a graceful window nor a deadline leaves the hold unbounded (the tree bounds it through `resolveShutdownGrace`'s fallback chain, and what the staged sentence lacks is the fallback rather than a bound); and the pre-attribution of the second of those to `spec.8.review-reliability.1`, which looked and did not file.

Retired in passes 1 and 2, each closed rather than dropped, with the closure reason that keeps it closed: the `coordination_generation` question (`Shutdown` is unfenced, validated only in `CoordinatorFence` and `CheckpointBarrier`); whether `Binder.Resume`'s compensation needs its own §7.3 sentence (answered by lifting the obligation into its own §7.1 paragraph scoped to any bind attempt); the CODE-2 second `ReportSessionScrub` DEFERRED (the call and the `errStartRaceReclaimed` sentinel were deleted); whether the Design paragraph's deferral of the credential file and the §4.9 timers to §5.2 is a false attribution (SPEC-3's first anchor widens the action list to name both); the four condensed checklist DEFERREDs of the pass-1 preamble (superseded by the fuller per-group versions); "Vacuous parenthetical at steps 2-8" (§29.2's mirror covers steps 2-10, which issue no pod-side RPC); "Recycle boundary on a failed bind" (`recycle` rather than `leaked` patches `bound → recycling`, and `ReleaseSlotReservation` passes `recycle=false`, so occupancy zero DELETEs the claim); "SPEC-3's closing note may be stale" (the edit landed and the four-site drift is gone); and the "On the default disposition the pod is replaced." dispute (one site rather than two, rewritten in Traps).

Two pass-2 retirements carry a residue that is still live. The OPEN "Resume path and the exclusion" was closed in the proposal's favour by nine lenses (`Binder.Resume` claims through `b.connect` → `podclaim.Claimer.Claim`, an idle-only whole-pod claim, never through `ClaimSlot`), and `spec.5.review-mechanism.1`'s disagreement is carried as the UNVERIFIED beside the Settled entry rather than dropped. The pass-1 six-anchor sweep entry was DELETED rather than retired, because a warning that a sweep is finished is worse than useless once nine more anchors have been minted; the sixteen-site list in Settled replaces it.

Retired in compaction pass 3, all closed rather than dropped:

- The OPEN "Does the resume compensation feed the slothealth ledger?". Answered NO and fixed: `podsession` reaches no tracker, gauge or registry, and CODE-5 now gives `accountSlotFailure` a third caller on `resumeOnPod`'s `podBinder.Resume` failure branch. The durable statement is in Settled.
- The UNVERIFIED "Is §29.4 scoped to a started session?". Settled by §29.4's own `**Preconditions.**` paragraph, which scopes the whole trace to a completed §29.2 startup. Four lenses read it cold and agreed, and the round-7 pair that filed the opposite is superseded rather than merely outvoted, because the paragraph they did not cite decides it.
- The two tier-7a items, "drain-gate assertion" and "closed exactly once". The first is closed: every `Shutdown` target in the shipped drain-gate tests is driven through `startDrainSession`, so CODE-1's gate move cannot reach them. The second is closed by deletion: the clause was false as a call count and as a teardown count, and the trap that replaces it says why.
- The UNVERIFIED "Does CODE-1's drain move lose a DRAINING obligation?". Answered: §15.4.2 owes none for a bound-but-unstarted session, and the withheld frame is now pinned by a tier-1 assertion.
- The OPEN "No section says positively that a reclaim can find a slot in `running`". Resolved as no-sentence-added: SPEC-4's `**Pre-`running` slot cleanup.**` paragraph already states it.
- The OPEN "Redis rehydration loses leaked occupancy". Resolved as its own proposal and recorded as a row under the summary's shipped-defects section.
- The UNVERIFIED "'on a pod of either concurrency' and the leaked sentence" and the OPEN "Pre-start cleanup failure and the §5.2 threshold". Both closed by the same concurrency split, which states the accounting for a concurrent pod and points at §7.1 for a one-session pod.
- The UNVERIFIED "tier 11 against the adapter-contract drift". Closed: the gate is a substring presence check and passes silently. The fact moved to Settled and the consequence to the Deferred entry it belongs with.
- The two fixture items, "Tier-4 fixture pool size" and "Tier-1 adapter fixtures". Both read: the tier-4 file seeds one Sandbox and `export_test.go:45` is already a `noteRuntimeStarted` seam. What survives is the narrower fixture-extension OPEN.
- The OPEN "0080 §1.19 membership" and its sibling DEFERRED against the summary row. Both discharged by the index-and-checklist reconciliation pass, which rewrote the row to state all three membership cases and the placement consequence.
- The OPEN "Unbound co-tenant and the drain gate". Superseded: after CODE-1 the signal needs `started` and no bound entry, and the residual case is an ordinary started session end. It is now a "do not file" line in Traps rather than an open question.
- The OPEN "This lens has swept itself out". Corrected in place rather than kept: the operational lens returned findings on the non-spec staging.
- Seven checklist and summary DEFERREDs (S1, S2, S3, S7, S8, the older S10 on the no-retry rule, and the 0080 §1.19 row), plus the CODE-2 entry and the blob-store edge-case clause. Each was applied: the first seven by the index-and-checklist reconciliation pass, the CODE-2 residue by round 2 taking the second branch and recording both orderings in the accepted-failure-modes list, and the blob-store clause by the edge-case rewrite that states the windowed-counter arithmetic honestly. The newer S9 and S10 corrections that landed after them are carried in Deferred, and the blob-store clause's SPEC-side twin is carried there too, because that pass could not edit `spec-changes.md`.

Retired in compaction pass 5, all closed rather than dropped:

- The UNVERIFIED "Does `releaseCredentials` cover user-source leases?", which was disputed with no correction between the two readings. Closed affirmative: `credassign.Service.ReleaseSession` iterates `s.leases.LeasesBySession([sessionID])` over the shared lease store and the `UserCredentialAssigner` doc comment states it outright. The reading that `releaseCredentials` touches only `b.Credentials` is right about the call and wrong about the consequence, because `b.Credentials` is the store both minters write into. Two lenses reached it independently.
- The OPEN "CODE-2's rollbacks run on the inbound context". Closed: all three `RuntimeProcess.Close` implementations use the context only to derive the SIGTERM grace and none checks `ctx.Err()`, so a cancelled context makes the close harsher rather than absent and the active-set removal the rollback exists for always lands. `context.WithoutCancel` is therefore not owed there.
- The OPEN "The reserved-branch defects row contradicts its own header". Closed by deletion: the `f4` firing removed the row from `## Defects in the shipped tree that this proposal does not stage`, because CODE-5 stages the fix for the exact defect it named and the section is the inventory a later reader uses to learn what 0081 leaves open. The shipped-tree fact survives in CODE-5's trade paragraph and the no-carve-out argument in its doc comment.
- The UNVERIFIED "Tier-1 resume accounting fixture". Closed on the seam question: `s.podBinder` is a concrete `*podsession.Binder` so no interface seam exists, but the package's own tests already build real `podsession.Binder` values against a fake or envtest client at five named sites and one of them already drives `resumeOnPod`. What remains is a fixture build rather than an open question.
- The OPEN "The operational lens is no longer swept out". It was a correction rather than a question, it has been acted on for several rounds, and the surfaces it named are recorded as Settled facts.
- The disputed DEFERRED on `docs/reference/state-machines.md:251`. Settled STALE by derivation rather than by vote: after application, staged §7.1 and SPEC-3's §5.2 scrub-model append both scope the `leaked` disposition to a pod serving concurrent sessions and give the exclusive pod §7.1's retirement disposition, so the doc prose agrees with the applied spec and is not an edit site. The sibling asymmetry it used to be paired with survives as the standing Open on §6.2's fence versus §6.2's prose at concurrency 1.
- Two claims this window superseded rather than closed, kept here so nobody re-derives the old form. `SlotID == SessionID` is stated in `spec/` (spec/05:395), so §7.1 restates rather than mints it; and `validate-maps` enforces per-file spec-map membership at tier 0, so the three entries that declined the spec-map item on "existence, version and dangling paths only" declined it on incomplete ground. Both corrections are applied in Settled and in Open.

Retired in compaction pass 6, all closed rather than dropped:

- The OPEN "The reclaim's deadline has no margin over the adapter's own cleanup budget". Closed by the fix it produced: `compensateFailedSlotBind` now sends `budget/2` as the graceful window inside an RPC deadline of `budget`, so the adapter's SIGTERM pivot and the gateway's give-up instant are no longer the same moment and a completed reclaim is no longer recorded `Leaked`. The fix shard named the Open as the finding it was filing. Its sibling, "Nothing at any tier pins that the compensating `Shutdown` stays inside its budget", is NOT closed: the tier-1 case now pins the two values' relation and nothing pins the wall-clock wait.
- The UNVERIFIED "Does `spec/18` need an edit?". Answered NO by three lenses on the same derivation: spec/18's phase-12c deliverable lines state the slot and recycle surfaces at the level of the split itself and enumerate no precondition, report rule or state-machine edge, so nothing SPEC-1 through SPEC-4 stages can make a phase deliverable stale. The durable statement is in Settled.
- The UNVERIFIED "Does §5.2's `Client error on exhaustion` want a fourth reason value for a reclaimed-slot race?". Answered NO: `codes.Aborted` passes through `classifySlotBindFailure` unclassified and lands on the existing retryable `STARTING_FAILED` / `SESSION_CREATION_FAILED` fallback in `writePodClaimError`, so no new client-visible code and no `docs/reference/error-catalog.md` row is owed.
- The DEFERRED against `spec-changes.md:98-102` (the blob-store edge-case clause). Retired because two entries disagreed and neither corrected the other: the Deferred said the spec half stood uncorrected while `non-spec-recheck-2.4.review-edit-sites.1`, reading `spec-changes.md:97-103` directly, reports the bullet already repaired to the windowed-counter wording and says not to re-apply it. The newer reading was kept, and the residual question is carried as an UNVERIFIED in Open so a fixer settles it before editing that bullet.
- One clause DELETED rather than retired: the standing Trap on the per-stage compensation table used to close with "the upload-free branch is listed separately only because it is the one stage failure that sends no RPC at all". `stageWorkspace` cannot fail on an upload-free plan at all, so the clause was false, it contradicted Settled #65, and the proposal had followed the Trap rather than the fact. The Trap now carries the correction and the filed finding.

Retired in compaction pass 7, both closed rather than dropped:

- The ground under the tier-7a co-tenanted variant, that it is the only thing exercising `SocketRuntimeProcess.Close`'s active-set early return. Closed: the shipped `TestSocketRuntimeProcessCloseScopedToSlot_spec_5_2` (socketruntime_test.go:252-300) exercises the sibling-active early return and the last close deterministically, and this proposal's tier-1 "Co-tenancy hazard" case covers the connected-with-empty-active-set state. The `gatedRuntime` fake holds no connection, child or listener, so the teardown effect is unobservable at tier 7a whichever fixture the case swaps in. The park-placement Open above SURVIVES, because it is about what the variant asserts rather than about which test owns the close.
- The pass-6 residual that the `budget/2` split's mechanism was traced by only one shard. Five independent lenses re-derived the whole chain this window — `Client.Shutdown`'s `time.Duration` fourth parameter, `drainViaLifecycle`, `contextWithGraceDeadline`'s parent-unchanged behaviour for a non-positive grace, `resolveShutdownGrace`'s preference for the ctx deadline, and both `Close` implementations returning nil on the kill arm — and each agreed. The Settled entries carry it; do not re-verify.

Two corrections applied in place rather than retired, kept here so the old forms are recognisable. The bind epoch is minted per admitted bind ATTEMPT, not per registry entry, and the per-entry form's conclusion ("neither the tree the retry staged nor the session it started can be taken down by the previous attempt's reclaim") was false in the ordering the mechanism exists for. The adapter-local property the proposal mints is the **slot-identifier reclaim hold**, windowed from deregistration to the end of the cleanup, not a second sense of "occupancy" starting at entry creation. SUPERSEDED IN PASS 8: the first of those two corrections was itself reversed by the r5→r6 fix pass, which restored per-entry minting and deleted the request-side echo. The per-attempt form is now the withdrawn alternative and is carried in Traps.

Retired in compaction pass 8:

- The OPEN "Can an adapter restart produce an epoch collision?". Closed, and the recorded ground was wrong: the agent podspec sets `RestartPolicy: Never` (pkg/controller/sandbox/podspec/podspec.go:980), so an adapter process cannot restart under a live pod. The no-re-dial rule does NOT carry the case, because `grpc.NewClient`'s `ClientConn` reconnects transparently. Two rounds reached the right conclusion from the wrong reason before the third caught it. CODE-6 additionally seeds the counter from `time.Now().UnixNano()`, which is stronger than the spec requires.
- The UNVERIFIED "How is a `Shutdown` carrying no epoch spelled on the wire?". Closed by the fix it produced: both SPEC-5 blocks now state that the epoch is a positive integer and that zero carries none, and §15.4 gained the missing non-conformance case for an adapter that compares a zero epoch or answers `superseded` for one. That clause is what keeps the §11.4 revoke fan-out, the occupancy-zero recycle edge and `Binder.ReleaseSlot` defined against a conforming third-party adapter.
- The OPEN "`AssignCredentialsResponse.bind_epoch = 1` is a hard field-number collision" and its sibling Trap. Both closed as REFUTED rather than resolved: the message is empty and the field number is correct. Eight lenses filed the correction and one recorded the `awk` artifact that produced the error. The correction is applied in Settled and the Trap now warns against acting on the old form.
- The OPEN "Nothing in the staged spec bounds the reclaim hold on an exclusive pool". Discharged by the r6→r8 rewrite: the hold paragraph now states its own three-way bound independent of §5.2's concurrency-scoped formula, and the edge-case bullet states the same three-way bound, so the two sites agree. The lens that opened it retired it.
- The OPEN "The epoch-reporting RPC set disagrees between the two staged blocks". Closed twice over: the r1→r2 fix replaced §4.7.1's wide predicate with the same closed seven-item list §15.4 carries, and the r6 revert then removed the request side entirely.
- The OPEN "The hold is stated with TWO different start points in the same document" and its later third site. Closed: §5.2, §6.2, §7.1 and §15.4 now agree word for word on both endpoints (the deregistration of the registry entry, to the cleanup finishing), and §6.2's own statement was replaced by a pointer so it cannot desynchronise again.
- The OPEN "Is `examples/runtimes/echo/` an adapter rather than a runtime binary?". Moot: `examples/` does not exist in the tree at all, so spec/15:1466 names a path with nothing behind it and there is no unstaged edit site. Pre-existing spec fiction, not 0081's.
- The OPEN "§4.7.1's caller rule may be wider than it needs to be for the two-step create path". Dissolved by the r6 revert, which deleted the request-side epoch the rule was about.
- The DEFERRED on the request side of the bind epoch (seven request fields, seven handlers, the client echo, a tier-3 echo case, and the field counts that would have become sixteen), and its §7.4 mid-session extension. Both dissolved by the r6 revert: no bind-sequence request carries an epoch, so there is nothing to wire, "nine fields" is correct again, and the mid-session upload needs no rule of its own. Recorded here rather than dropped, because the spec text those entries were written against was real for two rounds and the checklist still carries steps written for it.

Retired in compaction pass 9, all closed rather than dropped:

- The OPEN "Does the §7.1 compensation need an explicit exemption from the §10.1 generation fence?". Answered NO by the `f1.open-decisions.14` firing and deleted from the summary as open decision 14. A generation-stale rejection is a reclaim the adapter did not answer, which staged §7.1 already dispositions, and §10.1:66-68 tells such a replica to cancel and not retry, so an exemption would let a stale replica keep driving the pod. The unenforced fence is now a shipped-defect row.
- The OPEN "Does a `superseded` successor running on the predecessor's tree want a sentence?". Answered NO by the `f1.open-decisions.13` firing and deleted as open decision 13. The premise is false for `superseded`, whose definition requires the predecessor's entry to have been RELEASED, and a release runs the cleanup that removes the tree; the genuinely-inherited case is adoption of a surviving entry on the create-time-reserved path, which §5.2's policy does not place, and it is a shipped-defect row.
- The OPEN "Can an adapter restart produce an epoch collision?", re-retired with its ground restated once more because two rounds recorded the wrong one and a third caught it. The no-re-dial rule does NOT carry the case; `grpc.NewClient`'s `ClientConn` reconnects transparently. `RestartPolicy: Never` on the agent podspec is the ground, and CODE-6 additionally seeds the counter from `time.Now().UnixNano()`.
- The OPEN "The epoch-reporting RPC set disagrees between the two staged blocks", closed a second time. The r1→r2 fix gave §4.7.1 the same closed seven-item list §15.4 carries, and the same fix's exhaustiveness clause then wrongly excluded `Shutdown`; both blocks now carve `Shutdown` in. The predicate the later `spec.1` findings quote ("every RPC in the tables above that can create or resolve a slot registry entry") no longer exists anywhere in the proposal.
- The OPEN "How is a `Shutdown` carrying no epoch spelled on the wire?", and the §4.1 presence question with it. Both blocks now state that the epoch is a positive integer and that zero carries none, §15.4 gained the zero-comparison non-conformance case, and §4.1's epoch sentence was deleted outright because a bare proto3 scalar cannot engage a presence rule.
- The FILED "`ConfigureWorkspace` is documented idempotent while the admission rules refuse an epochless request onto a started entry". Dissolved by the r6 revert, which deleted the request-side epoch the admission rules were about. Two lenses had already refuted it under the r5 design.
- The two-start-points hold window, closed at its third site. §5.2, §6.2, §7.1 and §15.4 now agree word for word on both endpoints, and §6.2's own statement was replaced by a pointer so it cannot desynchronise again.
- The DEFERRED against the CODE-4 comment at non-spec-changes.md:558-560, which repeated the withdrawn "the state the failed attempt created is already gone" ground. It was in scope for the same fix that corrected the four spec sites and landed with them; its `return false` conclusion was correct throughout and stands.

Retired in compaction pass 10, all closed rather than dropped:

- The OPEN "Does the hold survive its own timeout?". Answered by the `f1.open-decisions.human-decisions.12` firing and deleted from the summary as open decision 12: the hold ends when the cleanup pass that took it RETURNS, whether that cleanup succeeded or failed. No staged text was edited, because the staging already states the terminal twice in §5.2, mirrors it in §15.4, and stages an unconditional `defer release()`. The two terminals that looked like they collided are different objects, and adding a `leaked` clause to the hold paragraph is barred by this log's own standing DECISION.
- The OPEN "Does CODE-4's `*SlotBindError` fold need a matching `isTransientPodClaimError` arm?". Answered YES and fixed: `non-spec.1.fix-G2.1` adds one arm keyed on the gRPC status code, owned by CODE-5, which also covers CODE-2's `Resume` rollback. The durable statement, including why the sentinel-matching alternative is unimplementable and where the test belongs, is in Settled.
- The OPEN "S1 lands spec text that cites a §4.7.1 statement S5 lands later". Dissolved by the resequence: SPEC-5 now leads at S1, so the §4.7 row's pointer resolves the moment the row lands at S2. The one citation the order cannot put first is SPEC-5's own pointer at §5.2's reclaim hold, which S1's line states so the residual is visible rather than silent.
- The OPEN "`compensateFailedSlotBind`'s signature on the resume path", which five lenses had recorded and none filed. Filed and fixed: the parameters narrow to scalars, matching the precedent CODE-5 had already set for `accountSlotFailure`. The rejected alternatives are in Settled so the synthetic-request form is not re-derived.
- The two spec-map OPENs, "`tests/spec-map.json`" and "The tier-7a case names no target file". Both filed as findings and both widened: the amendment added three more orphaned test surfaces beyond the tier-7a file the Opens named, and the registration is now attributed per creating step. The tier-7a case's filename is named in the staging; the map entry is what was missing.
- The UNVERIFIED "Is CODE-6's `Client.DemoteSDK` latch clear worth landing when no reachable trace exercises it?". Closed as landed: CODE-6 stages the clear on `DemoteSDK` and on a `RECLAIMED` reclaim, and a tier-1 case asserts `BindEpoch` answers zero after each. What survives is the narrower Trap that the clear does NOT fire on a plain `Shutdown`/`ShutdownRecycle`, which five lenses judged inert.
- Eleven Deferred entries applied by the three index-and-checklist reconciliation passes and the `non-spec.1.fix-*` groups: the two checklist entries on S9 and S10 (now S12 and S13, both rewritten), the summary's CODE-4 credential-lease line, the `TestValidTransitions_spec_6_2` "Watch out for" bullet, the `pkg/gen` path at all three sites, the CODE-6 `adapterclient` latch entry, the five-steps checklist entry (one repaired, four already true against the reverted staging), the open-decisions preamble, the design-narrative mirror entry, the CONF-1 and tier-3 entry, and the tier-3 case titled `Shutdown with the field unset`, which no longer survives in the file.
- Two Deferred entries discharged by a staged deliverable rather than by an edit: `docs/reference/adapter-contract.md:75` and its `:64` sibling. DOCS-2 now exists and folds into S6, after eleven lenses filed :75 independently across six loops. It was the single most-filed, least-acted item on the proposal.
- One Deferred dissolved: the summary's open-decision-9 framing entry. Entry 9 has left the section, and only the past-tense closure prose under `## Decisions` remains, which the entry itself states is correct.

Retired in compaction pass 11, all closed rather than dropped:

- The OPEN "Does §7.1's exclusive-pod clause need a mid-resume carve-out?". Answered NO by the `f1.open-decisions.human-decisions.15` firing and deleted from the summary as open decision 15. §7.1 already ends with the pre-attached disposition governing the pod and this reclaim governing the slot state, and §7.2 step 3 states no pod outcome by design, so no staged text changed. The pod's disposition is enumerated by §6.2's self-declared authoritative `resuming` subsection and disposed of in both pool configurations by shipped text; both facts are in Settled.
- The UNVERIFIED "Does §5.2's hold obligation reach the sixteen non-`Shutdown` release sites?". Retired as STALE in the proposal's favour: it rested on "CODE-6 takes the hold only inside `Shutdown`", and CODE-6's current text routes every site that deregisters an entry it then destroys through `reclaimSlotLocked`, of which there are three (`Shutdown`, `releaseSessionSlot`, and the §10.1.4 hold termination). The staged code implements the universal obligation rather than a `Shutdown`-only subset, so the spec-versus-code contradiction the item anticipated does not exist.
- The UNVERIFIED "Can the gateway placing a §5.2 retry actually see that a pod holds an incomplete reclaim?". Answered YES for the scope the staged sentence carries: §5.2 governs "the retries this policy places", and `applySlotRetryPolicy` places them inside one client request holding the `*SlotBindRequest` whose `ExcludePods` the same iteration appended after reading `sbe.Leaked`. The residual, that a SECOND client request cannot see the first one's incomplete reclaim, is the separate standing Open on the placement guarantee.

One item retired as MOOT rather than closed, kept so nobody re-derives it: the standing Open on `examples/runtimes/echo/` stays retired, and this window added the reason spec/15 is self-inconsistent about it as well as wrong about the tree — :1466 calls it a reference implementation of the ADAPTER while :1491 and :1812 describe it as a RUNTIME writing JSONL to stdout. Pre-existing spec fiction, not 0081's.

CORRECTED: a server-side interceptor on the REAL adapter server records the inbound `ShutdownRequest` without introducing a fake, so "over the real gRPC transport rather than against a fake" is satisfiable at S9 even though the adapter neither compares nor answers on the epoch until S10.
- The DEFERRED asking whether SPEC-4's fence annotation should be reworded to the arrival moment, with `docs/reference/state-machines.md`'s mirrored row added to DOCS-1. Moot: that was the option-(b) remedy for a contradiction the third route closed without touching either file.

Retired in compaction pass 13, all closed rather than dropped:

Retired in compaction pass 16. The 2026-09-16 hand revision withdrew two whole mechanisms, so this
pass retires the entries that stated either of them as live. Every one is SUPERSEDED rather than
refuted, and the tree facts each carried survive in the entries beside it.

- The bind-epoch design, in all its forms. DELETED outright, because each would have told a later
  agent to act on a mechanism that no longer exists: the DECISION that the epoch is minted per
  registry entry; the DECISION that the epoch rules are stated once in §4.7.1 and published once in
  §15.4; the DECISION that deleted §4.1's epoch sentence; the two exhaustiveness-carve-in
  DECISIONS; the "epochless" wire definition; the escape-hatch entry phrased on an epochless
  `Shutdown`; the no-re-dial rule's corrected reason; and the lazy-seed anti-reuse argument. The
  REMAINING epoch-era entries are left in place and are governed by one rule, stated in the
  changelog and in the Settled "DESIGN GENERATION" entry: their TREE facts survive and their DESIGN
  claims do not. A later pass with a working line-editor should sweep the rest; this one could not.
  The reclaim hold SURVIVES unchanged and is the one half of the old pair that did not move.
- The `ExcludePod(s)` placement mechanism. DELETED: the DECISION that the no-retry clause is
  replaced by a placement rule, and its round-2 successor. `spec.1.fix-G3.1` filed the CORRECTS
  against both by name. The remaining `ExcludePods` entries (the `ClaimSlot` sentinel and
  one-`continue` entries, the pointer threading, the `req` helper inventory, the accounting-versus-
  placement split, the `slotretry_load_test.go` goroutine entry, the new-identifier sweep) are left
  in place under the same reading rule. What governs a retry meeting the reclaiming pod is now the
  attempt token or the reclaim hold, and both refusals are transient.
- The fourteen-step checklist. DELETED: the resequence-and-renumber entry. The sequence is
  twenty-two steps and the live step identities are in Settled; any other entry naming S1 through
  S14 with epoch-era contents is reading the superseded sequence.
- One Open CLOSED: "Is a third-party adapter that omits the start-side epoch confirmation
  conforming?" Answered no and published. `spec.6.fix-G2.1` landed the obligation as one §4.7.1
  paragraph plus §15.4 clause (j), stated universally rather than scoped to the starts the gateway
  compensates, because the scoping condition is invisible to the third-party adapter the clause is
  written for.
- Two Settled claims CORRECTED rather than deleted, kept here so the old forms are recognisable.
  The `## Spec files touched` list is no longer "six files, thirteen edit sites": the §7.1 atomicity
  parenthetical and the §5.2 `**Max retries:**` sentence are no longer staged and §16.1 joined, so
  the site count must be re-derived from the staged blocks. And `spec/16_observability.md` DOES take
  an edit now, so the entry saying it needs none holds only in its alert half: there is still no
  §16.5 alert or recording rule keyed on any `lenny_slot_*` series, so nothing orphans and no
  runbook obligation is created.
- Two Traps SUPERSEDED but deliberately KEPT, with this note as their correction: the two "dead
  end: `ConfigureWorkspace` is documented idempotent while the admission rules refuse an epochless
  request onto a started entry" entries. Both refutations rest on the retired epoch design. Under
  the token the started-session rule is unconditional and `ConfigureWorkspace` carries no token, so
  the idempotency conflict was LIVE and is now closed by an explicit exemption in §4.7.1 and §15.4.
  Do not re-file it as a dead end and do not delete the exemption.
- The withdrawn-alternative Traps for the per-attempt and per-entry epoch forms are kept rather than
  retired, because the token design is close enough to the per-attempt form that a later round
  re-deriving either needs to see why each was withdrawn and why the token replaced both.

Retired in compaction pass 17. This pass ran the deletion pass 16 recorded that it could not run,
and it retired the Open and UNVERIFIED items whose subject the 2026-09-16 revision deleted. Every

beside it.

- Twenty-one Settled entries whose subject was the bind epoch's DESIGN, deleted outright because

  `claimSessionSlot` epoch-return entry; the `resolvePrepareStagingDir` epoch-widening entry; the

Retired in compaction pass 19. The caller's instruction was to retire every Open and UNVERIFIED
entry whose subject the 2026-09-16 revision deleted. None remained: passes 17 and 18 retired the
epoch trust argument, the epoch latch, the `bind_epoch` response fields, the `claimSessionSlot`
epoch return, `tests/tier3_contract/adapter_bind_epoch/`, `bind_epoch_conformance_test.go` and the
excluded-pod items already, and `grep -ci epoch` on `spec-changes.md` and `grep -c ExcludePods` on
every staged file both return 0. What this pass retired instead is twelve Open items the rounds it
read ANSWERED, each closed rather than dropped:

- "'Does not acknowledge' versus an unclean answer". ANSWERED by the r9→r10 fix: §7.1's predicate
  now reads "one whose answer does not report a clean exit, whatever outcome that answer carries",
  and §4.7.1's reclaim-outcome rule ties `absent` and `superseded` to a clean exit. The answer is in
  Settled as the resolved open decision 23.
- "Does any gateway path patch `SandboxClaim.status.phase` to `failed`?". ANSWERED NO: the
  pre-attached failure disposition is a bare claim DELETE through `failPhase` → `drain` →
  `podclaim.DeleteClaim`, so any reasoning resting on a terminal disposition at that point is wrong.
- "What does a third-party adapter answering `SLOT_RECLAIM_OUTCOME_UNSPECIFIED` mean?". The
  mechanism it rested on is gone: CODE-4 no longer branches on the outcome value, so there is no
  `default: return false` arm and no unrecognized-outcome counter. The zero value's meaning is
  unstated and now inert.
- "Does `superseded` fix a value for `exited_cleanly`?". ANSWERED by SPEC-5's reclaim-outcome rule,
  which states it for both non-removing outcomes.
- "Two mentions of other proposals sit outside `## Impacts on other proposals`". ANSWERED by the
  `f1.post-spec-loop` firings, which wrote 0072 and 0079 rows; the section now carries one row per
  proposal or programme step.
- "Does `SLOT_BIND_ALREADY_STARTED` on `AssignCredentials` render as a credential-catalog code?".
  ANSWERED NO: on the concurrent path it is a `SlotFailedError` at the credential-assignment stage
  and on the base path a bare wrap, so no `CREDENTIAL_*` §15.1 row is falsified.
- "Does the §4.7.9 credential-rotation flow need naming in §7.1's obligation?". ANSWERED NO:
  rotation goes through `RotateCredentials`, and the adapter `AssignCredentials` RPC has exactly two
  non-test callers, both bind-path.
- "SCHEMA-1 says 'ten fields' and its table lists nine". APPLIED by the reconciliation pass, which
  changed "ten fields" to "nine fields" at four sites.
- "§16.1.1's 'Used on' cell for `k8s_pod_name`". ANSWERED: §16.1.1's normative rule is table
  MEMBERSHIP, both labels are members, the column is descriptive and no gate parses it. Three lenses
  paid for this call.
- "SPEC-6's adapter-row wording versus the shipped deferral sentence". ANSWERED: no tier-11 gate
  matches on the §16.9 anchor or on any deferral phrasing, so the shorter wording is style.
- "Is the one-session 'return-to-pool exit' reachable for this residue class?". ANSWERED YES: it is
  reached by the §7.2 step-3 path, where the half-claimed replacement pod is released back to the
  pool and the §7.1 reclaim may not complete. Do not simplify SPEC-4's paragraph to one exit.
- "Does the §10.1 hold-timeout path open a reclaim hold that never closes?". ANSWERED NO, on a false
  premise: `terminateHeldSession`'s pass 2 is not `emitFinalUsage` plus `Runtime.Close` alone, it
  calls `removeSlotTree` immediately after the close, so a reclaiming cleanup does run and the
  hold-release condition is satisfiable there.

One Settled claim CORRECTED in place rather than retired, kept here so the old form is recognisable:
SPEC-6 stages TWO §16.1 rows, not three. `lenny_slot_compensation_outcome_unrecognized_total` is
gone repo-wide, and at least four lenses spent a grep each on the stale "three rows" entry.

Retired in compaction pass 20. The caller's instruction was again to retire every Open and
UNVERIFIED entry whose subject the 2026-09-16 revision deleted, and again none remained: passes 17
through 19 retired every epoch- and exclusion-named item, and `grep -ci epoch` on `spec-changes.md`
and `grep -c ExcludePods` on every staged file both still return 0. What this pass retired instead
is four Open items the non-spec window ANSWERED and five Deferred entries it APPLIED, each closed
rather than dropped:

- The OPEN "Is CODE-9's `alert_catalog_crosscheck_test.go` entry needed at all when no alert is
  added?" ANSWERED NO. There is no such file under `tests/tier11_docs/`; the only file of that
  basename is `pkg/observability/metrics/alert_catalog_crosscheck_test.go`, a unit-tier package
  test that derives every set it compares at run time and reads no documentation page, so a
  counter with no alert referencing it owes it nothing. The citation must be DELETED rather than
  repointed, which is the durable half and is in Settled.
- The UNVERIFIED "Does SPEC-1's §15.1 widening leave the row's endpoint enumeration complete?"
  ANSWERED YES. `RunSetup` has exactly two gateway call sites, `materializeSlot` (the concurrent
  `/{id}/start` path) and `Binder.Prepare` (reached from `/finalize`, `/v1/sessions/start` and
  `/{id}/resume`), so the four endpoints the row names are exactly the reachable set and the
  started-session refusal cannot surface at a fifth.
- The OPEN "Is the `ABORTED` hold refusal owed an adapter `ErrorCode` of its own?" ANSWERED NO. The
  gateway branches on the gRPC status rather than on `Error.code` for it: CODE-7's double-`%w` keeps
  `status.Code` walking to the original status and CODE-5 adds one `codes.Aborted` arm to
  `isTransientPodClaimError`, so a bare status classifies correctly.
- The UNVERIFIED "Does the SIGTERM `ShutdownDemoteSDK` path race `sdkwarm.go:261`?" retired as
  STALE. It rested on CODE-2 making that site a bare `_ = s.noteRuntimeStarted(...)` with no
  rollback. CODE-2 now gives it a real refusal arm that calls `SDKWarmRuntime.DemoteSDK`, clears
  `s.sdkConnected`, removes no entry and no tree and answers `codes.Aborted`, with a deterministic
  tier-1 case in `pkg/adapter/sdkwarm_test.go`.
- The DEFERRED obliging DOCS-1 to take the three projection-clause replacements at
  `docs/reference/state-machines.md:138`, and its S7/summary sibling. APPLIED: DOCS-1 now carries
  "**The pod state machine paragraph.**" with all three replacements and the widened file-list line,

- The two DEFERRED entries against DOCS-2's bind-attempt block (the `ConfigureWorkspace`

One Trap DELETED rather than retired, because a warning about a trap that no longer exists costs
every future agent a detour: "Do NOT file CODE-1's epoch early-return skipping clause three". Its
ground was the retired per-entry-epoch design. Under the token the staged `shutdownReclaimOutcome`
switch tests entry ABSENCE before the unconditional flag, so the concurrent `ShutdownRecycle` — which
always names a session the adapter holds no entry for — took the non-removing early return and
dropped the whole-pod scrub on a production path. The finding was confirmed and fixed by the single
`answerShutdown` exit. A Trap in its place records why the bar is dead.

One Settled claim CORRECTED in place, kept here so the old form is recognisable: "the occupancy-zero
recycle `Shutdown` ... always names a session the adapter holds no entry for" is true of the
CONCURRENT sender only. `Binder.Release`'s recycle branch sends the recycle-carrying request as the
session's only teardown, so the entry is live and the outcome is `reclaimed` on the removing arm.

Retired in compaction pass 21, each closed or superseded rather than dropped. One UNVERIFIED was
retired on the caller's instruction: the clause riding inside the Settled `ClaimSlot` pass-1 entry,
which asked whether a leaked slot's held occupancy makes the pod pass 1's own candidate and so
contradicts the §7.3 idle-inventory entry. Its subject is the placement exclusion, and no staged
deliverable now names that mechanism, so the question cannot be settled and cannot matter. The
Settled entry keeps its tree facts and its "do not generalise the idle-inventory argument to the
bind path" warning.

Three epoch-named Deferred entries were retired, each because a token-era sibling carrying the same
correction stands in `### Deferred`. The `## Files touched on application (non-spec)` entry against
"two of the seven epoch-reporting handlers", whose whole subject is the `bind_epoch` response fields
on `PrepareWorkspaceResponse` and `StartSessionResponse`: no staged deliverable names those fields.
The `non-spec-changes.md:1067-1070` entry against CODE-6's `ConfigureWorkspace` idempotent-repeat
arm, worded as a second read of the entry's EPOCH; the later entry against the same arm states the
same correction without the epoch ("have `claimSessionSlotUnderLock` return what the caller needs on
its `idempotentRepeat` early-return arm") and is the one to apply. The `SCHEMA-1's quoted gate
failure text` entry, whose quotation is the epoch-era `expected_bind_epoch` field name; the later
SCHEMA-1 entry carries both halves, the missing `= ` and the withdrawn field name, and supersedes it.

Three further Deferred entries were retired as duplicates their own successors carry whole: the
`spec-changes.md` Design-section pointer list omitting the SPEC-2 §7.2 edits, which the later
four-correction `spec-changes.md` entry carries; the first of the two identical CODE-4 `drain`
doc-comment entries, the survivor of which records itself as the fourth recording; and the second of
the two identical CODE-1 credential-file entries, the survivor of which carries the EVIDENCE lines.
The aggregate "outside every proposal file, five corrections" entry was retired for the same reason:
every one of its five (the proto comments, `Client.Shutdown`'s doc comment, spec/06:156 with its
docs mirror, §5.2's action list against `RemoveTree`'s four trees, and the release-by-session-id
class fix) stands as its own entry above it.

## Ledger

### [spec.2.fix-G1.1]

DECISION: closed the "§5.2 delegates the adapter-handler residue into a §6.2 paragraph whose scope excludes it" finding by REMOVING the delegation rather than by widening §6.2 or by re-inlining the two exit arms. SPEC-3's §5.2 scrub-model append now reads, for that sentence: "A cleanup the adapter runs inside the failed start's own handler reports nothing and no response carries its outcome, so one that does not complete reaches neither the `leaked` sub-state nor the whole-pod replacement trigger on a pod of either concurrency. What it leaves is the slot workspace tree and the slot credential directory, which do not outlive the pod, and the slot identifier, which the **Slot-identifier reclaim hold.** paragraph below governs." — BECAUSE §5.2 already owns all three things this residue consists of (disk residue via the scrub model, per-slot accounting via the Slot cleanup bullet, the identifier via the reclaim hold), so the disposition resolves without naming another section, and the single universal bound "do not outlive the pod" is true on a recycling pool and on a non-recycling one, so it needs no `recycle.enabled` qualifier and no exit enumeration — ALTERNATIVES: (a) widen the staged §6.2 pre-`running` paragraph off "on a pod serving one session" and off its "no cleanup reclaimed it" premise; rejected because §6.2's retiring exit is the pre-attached failure disposition, which §7.1 reaches only for a one-session pod (spec-changes.md:459), so a widened §6.2 would have to invent concurrent-pod exit terms, and it would silently resolve open decision 22. (b) re-inline the two arms at §5.2 ("on a recycling pod the occupancy-zero scrub reaches it; a pod that does not recycle retires"); rejected because that is the round-1 text the previous finding removed as a second full statement of §6.2's exit terms. (c) unify the two performers so any incomplete pre-`running` cleanup is `leaked`; rejected because it contradicts the code lane.

WATCHOUT: the reflex fix here is to add "on a recycling pod" back into the sentence, because the finding names the missing qualifier. Qualifying is narrowing, which is the failure this location kept exhibiting. The sentence must stop asserting the scrub's coverage at all and assert the bound instead. — EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:453 (the shipped scrub-model sentence scopes the whole-pod scrub to a recycling pod)

FACT: the performer split in §5.2's scrub-model append is load-bearing and is backed by the tree. `releaseSessionSlot` discards `removeSlotTree`'s error, the gateway's compensation is answered `ABSENT`, and CODE-4 reads `ABSENT` as a completed reclaim with `sbe.Leaked` false, so a cleanup that runs inside the adapter's own start handler and does not complete provably reaches no `leaked` sub-state and contributes nothing to the whole-pod replacement trigger. Any later fix that unifies the `Shutdown`-performed path and the handler path to save text stages a defect. — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/slotsession.go:217; proposals/0081_fix_a-failed-session-bind-leaves-an-adapter-slot-registry/0081_fix_a-failed-session-bind-leaves-an-adapter-slot-registry.non-spec-changes.md:3400-3417

FACT: open decision 22's ground survives this fix untouched. Its reasoning is that §6.2's staged paragraph covers only a workspace-preparation, setup or credential-assignment failure on a one-session pod, and a §7.3 re-attach has none of those stages. This fix edits no §6.2 text and removes the delegation only for the adapter-handler sentence; sentence 4's §6.2 citation for the one-session no-cleanup case is unchanged. — EVIDENCE: proposals/0081_fix_a-failed-session-bind-leaves-an-adapter-slot-registry/0081_fix_a-failed-session-bind-leaves-an-adapter-slot-registry.summary.md:640-647

DEFERRED [proposals/0081_fix_a-failed-session-bind-leaves-an-adapter-slot-registry/0081_fix_a-failed-session-bind-leaves-an-adapter-slot-registry.non-spec-changes.md]: the accepted-residue bullet at non-spec-changes.md:3411-3413 still enumerates the two whole-pod-boundary arms in full ("on a recycling pod the occupancy-zero whole-pod scrub removes both and verifies their absence, and a pod that does not recycle retires at that boundary"). It remains true, so this edit did not falsify it and the non-spec grant did not open it. What is now the case is that it is the last full statement of those arms outside §6.2, and §5.2 now states the bound without enumerating exits. It should become a citation of §5.2's bound ("the slot workspace tree and the slot credential directory, which do not outlive the pod") rather than a second enumeration.

### [spec.2.fix-G2.1]

DECISION: Replaced §15.4's conformance criterion (spec-changes.md:924) with a request-quantified sentence that names no rule and no selection predicate: "An adapter conforms when, on every request, it behaves as [Section 4.7.1](...) states for that request: it refuses or admits as that section states, performs the acts it states and no others, and answers on the gRPC status, the `ErrorCode` and the `Shutdown` outcome it states. An adapter that behaves otherwise on any request does not conform." — BECAUSE §4.7.1's cascade is total and first-match, so "the behaviour §4.7.1 states for that request" has exactly one value per request; the old rule-quantified form ("for every request meeting a numbered rule's condition") was unsatisfiable on any request meeting two rules' conditions, the designed instance being a stale-token bind against a started entry, which meets rule 5 and rule 6 at once. The trailing clause "reading the status of rule 2 from the block below and the status of rules 11 through 14 from rule 15" was deleted rather than carried: spec-changes.md:922 already states both exceptions. — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's suggested wording ("the earliest-numbered rule whose condition the request meets") was rejected because it writes §4.7.1's cascade out in full at a second site, which is the defect class commit fcac6a13c ended; deleting the criterion outright was rejected because the criterion is cited as an existing object by the checklist, CONF-1, DOCS-2 and §4.7.1's own closing prose; an exception clause naming the rule 5 / rule 6 pair was rejected as incomplete the moment a rule is added.

WATCHOUT: §15.4 is now the third attempt at this one paragraph. Round 1 staged a fifteen-row per-rule table, round 2 staged a universally rule-quantified criterion, and both failed because §15.4 was saying in its own words which rule governs a request. Any future edit that reintroduces a rule number, a rule's condition, or a selection predicate into §15.4's criterion is the same defect a third time. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:924; review-log.md:833, :4911.

FACT: the whole bind-attempt-token mechanism is staged only. Repo-wide greps over spec/, docs/, schemas/ and charts/ for `bind_attempt`, `SLOT_BIND_ATTEMPT_SUPERSEDED`, `SLOT_BIND_ALREADY_STARTED` and "conforms when" return zero hits, so no tree file can be falsified by a wording change here. EVIDENCE: verified this round against /home/ec2-user/lenny/spec, docs, schemas, charts.

FACT: the sites naming "§15.4's conformance criterion" all name it in the abstract and commit to no quantifier language, so none was falsified by this edit: spec-changes.md:238-243, :894, :1085; summary.md:960; non-spec-changes.md CONF-1 and DOCS-2 paragraphs. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:894 ("§15.4's conformance criterion ... reach a rule by its number and name rather than restating it").

FACT: non-spec-changes.md:2139 ("The request meets both rules' conditions, and §15.4 publishes applying them in the other order as non-conformance") was FALSE under the old criterion and becomes TRUE under this one, so the repair-only trigger did not fire and non-spec-changes.md was not edited. An optional later tightening, for precision rather than correctness: "§4.7.1's cascade fixes rule 5's answer for that request, and §15.4's criterion makes any other answer non-conforming."

DEFERRED [/home/ec2-user/lenny/proposals/0081_fix_a-failed-session-bind-leaves-an-adapter-slot-registry/0081_fix_a-failed-session-bind-leaves-an-adapter-slot-registry.implementation-checklist.md]: step S1 at :17 still reads "§15.4 is re-cut to name the rule set, state the conformance criterion, and state the three non-conformances that are not any single rule's condition: a wrong evaluation order, a separable step, and a gRPC error for an outcome the reclaim-outcome rule reports." That is false against the staged text and stays false after this edit. What is true instead: "§15.4 is re-cut to name the rule set and to state the conformance criterion by reference to §4.7.1, and it states no rule of its own. The non-conformances that are not any single rule's condition are stated by §4.7.1: a wrong evaluation order by its cascade sentence, a separable step by its stamp-once rule, and a gRPC error for a reported reclaim outcome by rule 15." The G2 design listed this checklist edit as part of the fix; the HARD CONSTRAINT puts the checklist out of bounds, so it is deferred here rather than applied.

CORRECTS [review-log.md:4871]: that earlier DEFERRED on S1 gave the corrected wording as "§15.4 is re-cut to name the rule set and state the conformance criterion". After this round the criterion is stated by reference to §4.7.1 and §15.4 states no rule of its own, so the replacement wording in the DEFERRED line above supersedes it. The two are the same defect; close them as one.
